\chapter{\texorpdfstring{$E_n$}{E-n}-Factorization and Higher Chiral Structure}
\label{ch:en-factorization}

\providecommand{\bY}{\mathbb{Y}}
\providecommand{\DR}{\mathrm{DR}}
\providecommand{\Env}{\mathrm{Env}}

\begin{remark}[Stage-1 and Stage-2 targets of the \texorpdfstring{$E_n$}{E-n}-operad apparatus]
\label{rem:en-factorization-stage1-target}
The $E_n$-operad machinery in this chapter targets Stage-1 outputs
$\PhiFA_d(\cA) \in E_d\text{-HolFA}(X)$. The Stage-2 output
$A_\cA=\Phi_d(\cA)$ has dimension-dependent chiral level
\[
 A_\cA\in
 \begin{cases}
 \Etwo\text{-}\mathrm{ChAlg}(C), & d\leq 2,\\
 \Eone\text{-}\mathrm{ChAlg}(C), & d\geq 3.
 \end{cases}
\]
For $d\geq3$ the transverse $E_{d-1}$-factor in
$E_d\simeq \Eone\otimes E_{d-1}$ is not a second native chiral
operation on $A_\cA$; its first visible $\Etwo$ incarnation is the
derived centre $Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_\cA)$, equivalently
the Drinfeld centre of $\Rep^{\Eone}(A_\cA)$ when the BZFN hypotheses
hold.
\end{remark}

The two-stage factorisation of Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-two-stage-factorisation}
assigns to a CY$_d$ category $\cC$ a canonical $E_d$-holomorphic
factorisation algebra $\PhiFA_d(\cC) \in \EdHolFA(X)$ on the CY$_d$
variety, followed by the chiral specialisation
$\SpCh_{\Sigma_{d-1}, C}$ to a reference curve $C$.  The Stage-1 level
is $E_d$.  The Stage-2 level is $\Etwo$ for $d\leq2$ and $\Eone$ for
$d\geq3$.  At $d=1$ the $\Etwo$ output may factor through a
commutative $E_\infty$ refinement; the target of the correspondence is
still the $\Etwo$-chiral category.  At $d\geq3$ the first $\Etwo$
object attached to $A_\cC=\Phi_d(\cC)$ is
$Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_\cC)$, or equivalently
$\cZ(\Rep^{\Eone}(A_\cC))$ on the categorical side; it is not
$A_\cC$ itself.

The operative mechanism is stabilization. The $\bS^d$-framing on
$\HH_\bullet(\cC)$ provides an $E_d$-algebra structure before the curve
readout, but for $d \geq 3$ Dunn additivity leaves only the
$\Eone$-factor tangent to $C$ inside the chiral algebra. The higher
$E_d$-structure survives as shifted symplectic, shifted Poisson, and
centre data beyond the base $\Eone$ level, classified by the framing
obstruction.

The governing input is Bott periodicity, but two distinct periodicities are at play and the literature regularly conflates them. The complex Bott periodicity $\pi_d(BU) = \mathrm{KU}^{-d}$ has \emph{period $2$}: $\Z$ for $d$ even, $0$ for $d$ odd. The real Bott periodicity $\pi_d(BO) = \mathrm{KO}^{-d}$ and the symplectic variant $\pi_d(B\Sp)$ each have \emph{period $8$}, not $2$; the $8$-periodicity is never a property of $\pi_d(BU)$. The CY framing obstruction lives in the complex group $\pi_d(BU)$ as the primary obstruction (from the holomorphic $U(n)$ reduction), with a secondary $\mathrm{Sp}$-refinement in $\pi_d(B\Sp)$ activated at odd $d$. The effective obstruction $\Obs_{\mathrm{eff}}(d)$ is the colimit of these two towers and inherits the period-$8$ pattern from the real side. The main result of this chapter (Theorem~\ref{thm:e1-stabilization-cy}) assembles these obstruction computations into a single statement: $\Obs_{\mathrm{eff}}(d)$ is trivial precisely when $d \bmod 8 \in \{1, 3, 7\}$, it is $\Z$-valued at all even $d$ from $\pi_d(BU)$, and it is $\Z/2$-valued at $d \equiv 5 \pmod 8$ from the $\pi_d(B\Sp)$ refinement (since $\pi_d(BU) = 0$ at odd $d$, the Sp-refinement is the only nontrivial datum there). Phrasing such as ``$\pi_d(BU)$ is $8$-periodic'' is always a category error: $\pi_d(BU)$ is $2$-periodic; the $8$-periodicity belongs to the real/symplectic K-theory groups.


\section{Dunn additivity and the \texorpdfstring{$E_n$}{E-n} hierarchy}
\label{sec:dunn-additivity}

Recall Dunn additivity in Boardman--Vogt form:
\[
 E_{a+b}\simeq E_a\otimes E_b .
\]
In particular \(E_d\simeq \Eone\otimes E_{d-1}\), after choosing the
curve direction, and \(\Etwo\simeq \Eone\otimes\Eone\).  The symbol
\(E_0\), when used, denotes pointed objects; it is not an additional
associative multiplication.

For the two-stage factorisation of Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-two-stage-factorisation},
the CY dimension $d$ determines the native operadic level of stage~1:
$\PhiFA_d(\cC)$ is $E_d$-holomorphic on $X$
(Proposition~\ref{prop:native-en-level}).
The $\bS^d$-framing on Hochschild homology $\HH_\bullet(\cC)$ carries an $E_d$-algebra structure. The Stage-2 chiral output has the following scope:
\begin{itemize}
 \item $d = 1$: $\Phi_1(\cC)$ is an $\Etwo$-chiral algebra with a commutative $E_\infty$ refinement in the free elliptic cases; the refinement is stronger than the target, not a different target.
 \item $d = 2$: $\Phi_2(\cC)$ is $\Etwo$-chiral. The braided monoidal structure belongs to the output itself.
 \item $d = 3$: the Stage-1 output $\PhiFA_3(\cC)$ is $E_3$-holomorphic on $X$. The Stage-2 chiral readout along $(\Sigma_2,C)$ is $\Eone$. The genuine nonsymmetric $\Etwo$ quantum group braiding arises on the derived object
 \[
  Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}\bigl(\SpCh_{\Sigma_2,C}(\PhiFA_3(\cC))\bigr),
 \]
 equivalently on the Drinfeld centre of the $\Eone$-monoidal representation category (Theorem~\ref{thm:c3-drinfeld-center}), not on the chiral algebra $\SpCh_{\Sigma_2,C}(\PhiFA_3(\cC))$ itself.
\end{itemize}

For $d \geq 4$, once the stage-1 witness is constructed, the output
$\PhiFA_d(\cC)$ remains $E_d$-holomorphic on $X$, while the CY chiral algebra
$\SpCh_{\Sigma_{d-1},C}(\PhiFA_d(\cC))$ is always $\Eone$. The higher
$E_d$ structure manifests as \emph{additional shifted structure}
(shifted symplectic, shifted Poisson) beyond the base $\Eone$ curve
level. The existence of the \(d\geq4\) \(\PhiFA_d\) host itself remains
the CY-A$_d$ conditional input.

\begin{proposition}[Dunn--Lurie additivity and the scope of \texorpdfstring{$\SpCh$}{SpCh}-specialisation]
\label{prop:dunn-lurie-sp-factorisation-homology}
Let $\cC$ be a CY$_d$ category with $d \geq 2$, $X$ a CY$_d$ variety, $\PhiFA_d(\cC) \in \EdHolFA(X)$ the stage-$1$ holomorphic factorisation algebra, $\Sigma_{d-1} \subset X$ a closed oriented $(d{-}1)$-cycle with a compatible framing, and $C \subset X$ a reference curve meeting $\Sigma_{d-1}$ transversely in a finite set.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{Dunn--Lurie additivity.}
 $E_d \simeq \Eone \otimes E_{d-1}$ as symmetric monoidal $\infty$-operads, with the $\Eone$-factor along the specialisation direction on $C$ and the $E_{d-1}$-factor along the transverse $\Sigma_{d-1}$-directions. Iterated: $E_d \simeq \Eone^{\otimes d}$, one $\Eone$-factor per complex direction.
 \item \emph{Specialisation is framed factorisation homology.} The stage-$2$ specialisation factors as
 \[
 \SpCh_{\Sigma_{d-1}, C}(\PhiFA_d(\cC))
 \;\simeq\;
 \Bigl(\int_{\Sigma_{d-1}} \PhiFA_d(\cC)\Bigr)\Big|_C
 \]
 where $\int_{\Sigma_{d-1}}$ is the Lurie--Francis framed factorisation homology of an $E_d$-algebra, equivalently of an $E_{d-1}$-algebra object in $\Eone$-algebras, and $(-)|_C$ is pullback along $C \hookrightarrow X$.
 \item \emph{Francis~$2013$ identification.} Under Dunn--Lurie and the Ayala--Francis--Rozenblyum framed-manifold calculus, the framed factorisation homology over $\Sigma_{d-1}$ is the enveloping functor
 \[
 U_{\Sigma_{d-1}} \colon \mathrm{Alg}_{E_{d-1}}(\cD) \longrightarrow \mathrm{Alg}_{\Eone}(\cD),
 \qquad
 A \longmapsto \int_{\Sigma_{d-1}} A,
 \]
 identified by Francis~$2013$ \textup{Thm.~$4.1$} with the universal $\Eone$-enveloping algebra of the transverse \(E_{d-1}\)-structure; $\cD$ is the target symmetric monoidal $\infty$-category of chain complexes.
 \item \emph{Output scope.} For $d\geq3$, the result of this enveloping operation is an $\Eone$-chiral algebra on $C$.  The associated $\Etwo$ structure is
 \[
  Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}\!\left(\SpCh_{\Sigma_{d-1},C}(\PhiFA_d(\cC))\right)
  \quad\text{or}\quad
  \cZ\!\left(\Rep^{\Eone}(\SpCh_{\Sigma_{d-1},C}(\PhiFA_d(\cC)))\right),
 \]
 not a second multiplication on the Stage-2 algebra itself.  For $d\leq2$, the functor $\Phi_d$ lands directly in $\Etwo$-chiral algebras; the $d=1$ commutative refinement is an $E_\infty$ enhancement of that $\Etwo$ target.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Claim~\textup{(i)} is Dunn's theorem \textup{\cite[\S 5.1.2]{LurieHA}}: the iterated tensor product of $\Eone$-operads computes the $E_n$-operad. The symmetric monoidal structure on $\mathrm{Alg}_{\Eone}$ gives the inner tensor product, and the associativity of this iteration gives $E_d \simeq \Eone \otimes E_{d-1}$ for any partition $d = 1 + (d-1)$.

Claim~\textup{(ii)}: by Costello--Gwilliam locality, $\PhiFA_d(\cC)$ is a holomorphic $E_d$-factorisation algebra on $X$, and $\SpCh_{\Sigma_{d-1}, C}$ is defined (Definition~\ref{def:phi-fa-and-sp}) as the composition of factorisation-homology integration over $\Sigma_{d-1}$ with pullback to $C$. Integration of an $E_d$-algebra over a closed framed $(d{-}1)$-manifold is the Lurie--Francis framed factorisation homology \textup{\cite[\S 5.5]{LurieHA}}; on the Dunn summand $E_{d-1}$ this is genuine factorisation homology of an $E_{d-1}$-algebra, while the $\Eone$-summand is carried along unchanged and restricted to $C$.

Claim~\textup{(iii)}: Francis~$2013$ \textup{\cite[Thm.~$4.1$]{Francis2013}} identifies $\int_{\Sigma}$ on an $E_n$-algebra over a framed closed $(n{-}1)$-manifold $\Sigma$ with the left adjoint to the restriction-of-structure functor along the operadic inclusion $\Eone \hookrightarrow E_n$ coming from the Dunn decomposition. The identification is compatible with Ayala--Francis' treatment of framed manifold calculus \textup{\cite{AyalaFrancis2015, AyalaFrancis2015b}}, which gives the universal property of $\int_{\Sigma}$ as the enveloping $\Eone$-algebra of the $E_{n-1}$-structure. Applied to $n = d$ and $\Sigma = \Sigma_{d-1}$, the enveloping-functor description of (ii) follows.

Claim~\textup{(iv)}: the operadic output of \(U_{\Sigma_{d-1}}\) is an
\(\Eone\)-algebra because the functor has codomain
\(\mathrm{Alg}_{\Eone}(\cD)\).  The transverse \(E_{d-1}\)-structure is
remembered by the dependence on \(\Sigma_{d-1}\), by shifted
symplectic and shifted Poisson classes, and by the higher Deligne
centre of the resulting \(\Eone\)-algebra.  The BZFN comparison places
the first braided object in the Drinfeld centre of the representation
category, while the higher Deligne conjecture places it in
\(\HH^\bullet(A,A)\).  Neither construction changes the native
Stage-2 algebra \(A\).  Dunn additivity identifies the operadic target;
it does not prove \(\HH^{-2}\)-vanishing, contractibility of lifting
spaces, or compact CY$_3$ closure.  In dimensions \(d\leq2\), the definition of
\(\Phi_d\) lands directly in the \(\Etwo\)-chiral target, and the
\(d=1\) commutative refinement restricts along \(\Etwo\to E_\infty\).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[The \texorpdfstring{$(\Sigma_{d-1}, C)$}{(Sigma-d-1, C)}-moduli as the parameter space of \texorpdfstring{$\Eone$}{E1}-shadows]
\label{rem:sigma-c-moduli-e1-shadows}
Proposition~\ref{prop:dunn-lurie-sp-factorisation-homology}(ii)--(iii) makes precise the statement of Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-two-stage-factorisation} that stage~$1$ is pinned (Kontsevich--Tamarkin $E_d$-formality up to $\mathrm{GRT}_1$-torsor) while stage~$2$ is a moduli parametrised by $(\Sigma_{d-1}, C) \in \mathrm{Cyc}_{d-1}(X) \times \mathrm{Curves}(X)$: different $\Sigma_{d-1}$ give genuinely distinct $\Eone$-shadows, because the enveloping functor $U_{\Sigma_{d-1}}$ depends on the diffeomorphism type and framing of $\Sigma_{d-1}$ through its $(d{-}1)$-manifold invariants (linking numbers, secondary characteristic classes). On $X = K3 \times E$ at $d = 3$, this Stage-$2$ moduli supplies the comparison surface for the correspondence-based route. The six routes to $G(K3 \times E)$ themselves are six distinct constructions: only the framed $K3$-fibre route is a direct $\Phi_3$ specialisation, while the Borcherds, CHL, lattice, sigma-model, and Schur-sector routes have independent inputs and are compared to Stage-$2$ shadows by separate bridge data. The categorical Euler characteristic $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(K3 \times E) = \chi(\cO_{K3}) \cdot \chi(\cO_E) = 2 \cdot 0 = 0$ is a stage-$1$ invariant, K\"unneth-multiplicative on products and independent of $\Sigma_2$; the $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$-spectrum is Borcherds-weight data, $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Phi_N)=c_N(0)/2$, not a family of compact total-space supertraces.
\end{remark}


\section{Factorization algebras on \texorpdfstring{$\C^n$}{C-n}}
\label{sec:fact-cn}

For the toric CY category $\cC = D^b(\Coh(\C^d))$ with its $\GL(d)$-equivariant structure, the factorization algebra $\Fact_X(\mathfrak{L}_\cC)$ on a curve $X$ is constructed from the Lie conformal algebra of $\GL(d)$-invariant polyvector fields (Chapter~\ref{ch:cy-to-chiral}). For $d \geq 3$, the $\GL(d)$-invariant Schouten--Nijenhuis brackets vanish identically (Theorem~\ref{thm:c3-abelian-bracket}), so the classical Lie conformal algebra is abelian and all noncommutative structure arises from the $\Omega$-deformation. For general (non-toric) CY$_d$ categories with $d \geq 3$, the Schouten--Nijenhuis bracket on $\HH^\bullet(\cC)$ need not vanish, but its Gerstenhaber degree $1 - d \leq -2$ prevents the construction of braiding: the algebra is $\Eone$ by degree, not by abelianity.

The equivariant deformation parameters are $\sigma_3, \sigma_4, \ldots, \sigma_d$ (the elementary symmetric polynomials of $h_1, \ldots, h_d$ subject to $\sum h_i = 0$), giving a $(d-2)$-dimensional deformation space. For $d = 3$, this is the single parameter $\sigma_3 = h_1 h_2 h_3$ of the affine Yangian.

\subsection{Prefactorisation algebras, Weiss covers, factorisation algebras}
\label{subsec:prefact-weiss-fact}

The passage from a local disc-level $\En$-algebra structure to a global factorisation algebra on a space $X$ is not automatic: it depends on a cover-theoretic axiom (the Weiss condition) that governs the interaction between the cover and the finite-configuration spaces of~$X$.

\begin{definition}[Prefactorisation algebra, Weiss cover, factorisation algebra]
\label{def:prefact-weiss-fact}
Fix a topological space $X$, an $\infty$-category $\cV$ with a symmetric monoidal structure (typically chain complexes over $k$), and an integer $n \geq 1$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item A \emph{prefactorisation algebra} on $X$ valued in $\cV$ is an assignment $U \mapsto \cF(U) \in \cV$ for each open $U \subseteq X$, together with factorisation products $m_{U_1, \ldots, U_k; V} : \cF(U_1) \otimes \cdots \otimes \cF(U_k) \to \cF(V)$ for every $k \geq 0$ and every $(U_1, \ldots, U_k; V)$ with the $U_i$ pairwise disjoint and $\sqcup_i U_i \subseteq V$, satisfying associativity, unitality, and $\Sigma_k$-equivariance \textup{(}Costello--Gwilliam \emph{Factorization Algebras in Quantum Field Theory} Vol.~I, CUP~$2021$, Chapter~$3$; the same construction is taken up in Vol.~II, arXiv:$2210.13036$, Chapter~$1$\textup{).}
 \item A family $\mathfrak U = \{U_\alpha\}_{\alpha \in A}$ of opens in $X$ is a \emph{Weiss cover} of $X$ when, for every integer $k \geq 1$ and every finite set of points $\{x_1, \ldots, x_k\} \subset X$, there exists $\alpha \in A$ such that $\{x_1, \ldots, x_k\} \subset U_\alpha$ \textup{(}Costello--Gwilliam Vol.~I Definition~$6.1.0.5$; the Weiss covers of $X$ generate the \emph{Weiss Grothendieck topology} on $\mathrm{Opens}(X)$\textup{).} Equivalently, for every $k \geq 1$ the family $\{U_\alpha^k\}_{\alpha \in A}$ of Cartesian powers covers the configuration space $\mathrm{Conf}_k(X) \subset X^k$, and the passage $\mathfrak U \rightsquigarrow \{U_\alpha^k\}_{\alpha, k}$ is the defining cofinality. A Weiss cover is in particular an ordinary open cover of $X$ \textup{(}take $k = 1$\textup{);} the converse fails, and the difference is the content of the descent dichotomy \textup{(}Proposition~\ref{prop:weiss-refinement-upgrade}\textup{).}
 \item A prefactorisation algebra $\cF$ on $X$ is a \emph{factorisation algebra} when for every open $U \subseteq X$ and every Weiss cover $\mathfrak U$ of $U$, the Čech complex $\check{C}(\mathfrak U, \cF)$ is quasi-isomorphic to $\cF(U)$; equivalently the natural map
 \[
 \mathrm{hocolim}_{\mathfrak U}\, \cF \;\xrightarrow{\;\sim\;}\; \cF(U)
 \]
 is an equivalence in $\cV$, i.e.\ $\cF$ is a homotopy cosheaf on the Weiss topology of $X$ \textup{(}Costello--Gwilliam Vol.~I Definition~$6.1.1.1$ for the lax axiom and Definition~$6.1.2.1$ for the homotopy cosheaf axiom; Francis--Gaitsgory arXiv:$1103.5803$ Theorem~$1.2.4$ and Theorem~$5.2.1$, where the Ran-space factorisation datum is realised as a chiral-commutative coalgebra on $\Ran X$ whose image under $\mathrm{Prim}^{\mathrm{ch}}[1]$ is a chiral Lie algebra on~$X$\textup{).}
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Weiss refinement upgrades prefactorisation to factorisation]
\label{prop:weiss-refinement-upgrade}
Let $\cF$ be a prefactorisation algebra on $X$ and $\mathfrak U = \{U_\alpha\}$ an open cover that is not Weiss. The \emph{Weiss refinement} generated by $\mathfrak U$ is the cover $\mathfrak U^\sqcup$ whose members are the finite disjoint unions $V_1 \sqcup \cdots \sqcup V_k \subset X$ with each $V_i$ a contractible open of $X$ contained in some $U_{\alpha(i)}$ and the $V_i$ pairwise disjoint; in Costello--Gwilliam Vol.~I Definition~$6.1.0.6$ this is the condition that $\mathfrak U$ \emph{generates} the Weiss cover $\mathfrak U^\sqcup$. The refinement $\mathfrak U^\sqcup$ is a Weiss cover of $X$: any finite $\{x_1, \ldots, x_k\} \subset X$ with $x_i \in U_{\alpha(i)}$ embeds in $V_1 \sqcup \cdots \sqcup V_k$ for small $V_i \ni x_i$ pairwise disjoint. The homotopy colimit
\[
 \cF^{\mathrm{FA}} \;:=\; \mathrm{hocolim}_{\mathfrak U^\sqcup}\, \cF
\]
is a factorisation algebra on $X$ \textup{(}Costello--Gwilliam Vol.~I \S$6.1.1$--$6.1.2$: the Čech complex on a Weiss cover computes the global sections of any homotopy factorisation algebra\textup{).} The unrefined homotopy colimit $\cF^{\mathrm{QC}} := \mathrm{hocolim}_{\mathfrak U}\, \cF$ satisfies only the quasi-coherent descent condition \textup{(}single-chart opens only, no disjoint-union axiom\textup{);} it is a \emph{sheaf of $\En$-algebras} on $X$ in the classical sense, not a factorisation algebra. Under the Ran-space stratification $\Ran(X) = \bigsqcup_{k \geq 1} \mathrm{Conf}_k(X) / \Sigma_k$, the comparison map $\cF^{\mathrm{QC}} \to \cF^{\mathrm{FA}}$ is the composition of the $k = 1$-truncation $\cF^{\mathrm{QC}} \simeq \cF^{\mathrm{FA}}|_{\Conf_1(X)}$ with the inclusion $\Conf_1(X) = X \hookrightarrow \Ran(X)$; it factors through the single-point stratum and is an equivalence exactly when $\Conf_{\geq 2}(X)$ contributes trivially to the Weiss-refinement hocolim, which fails on any non-Weiss~$\mathfrak U$ that admits a two-point configuration $S$ lying in the complement of every single $U_\alpha$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}[Scope]
Definition~$6.1.0.6$ of Costello--Gwilliam Vol.~I introduces the \emph{generates} relation $\mathfrak U \rightsquigarrow \mathfrak U^\sqcup$; that $\mathfrak U^\sqcup$ is a Weiss cover follows directly from Definition~$6.1.0.5$ by choosing pairwise disjoint contractible opens around each $x_i$. The Weiss-descent equivalence with factorisation algebras is the content of the homotopy cosheaf axiom, Vol.~I Definition~$6.1.2.1$, and is the reason the Čech complex on any Weiss cover of $U$ computes $\cF(U)$ \textup{(}Vol.~I \S$6.1.1$--$6.1.2$; the translation-invariant case is Vol.~II arXiv:$2210.13036$ Chapter~$1$\textup{).} The injective-comparison $\cF^{\mathrm{QC}} \hookrightarrow \cF^{\mathrm{FA}}$ statement follows from the inclusion $\mathfrak U \hookrightarrow \mathfrak U^\sqcup$ of Weiss-topology cover categories and the universal property of hocolim; strictness on non-Weiss~$\mathfrak U$ follows from the existence of disjoint-union opens in $\mathfrak U^\sqcup$ absent from $\mathfrak U$, which support genuinely new factorisation products $m_{V_1, \ldots, V_k; V_1 \sqcup \cdots \sqcup V_k}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Load-bearing example: resolved conifold]
\label{rem:resolved-conifold-weiss}
The resolved conifold $\bY = \mathrm{Tot}(\cO_{\bP^1}(-1) \oplus \cO_{\bP^1}(-1))$ admits the two-chart cover $\{U_+, U_-\}$ with $U_\pm \cong \C^3$ \textup{(}Section~\ref{sec:conifold-two-chart-cech} of Chapter~\ref{ch:toric-coha}\textup{).} The cover fails the Weiss axiom at $k = 2$: pick $x_+ \in U_+ \setminus U_-$ above the north pole of $\bP^1$ and $x_- \in U_- \setminus U_+$ above the south pole; the configuration $\{x_+, x_-\}$ is not contained in any single $U_\pm$, so no $\alpha \in \{+, -\}$ satisfies $\{x_+, x_-\} \subset U_\alpha$. The Weiss refinement $\{U_+, U_-\}^\sqcup$ adjoins the disjoint-union open $V_+ \sqcup V_-$ with $V_\pm \subset U_\pm$ small contractible neighbourhoods of $x_\pm$, and this is the open that houses $\{x_+, x_-\}$. The QC-descent version of Theorem~\ref{thm:conifold-E3-cech-gluing} produces $\mathrm{CoHA}(\bY)^{\mathrm{QC}}$ as a sheaf of associative algebras \textup{(}single-chart configurations only, $k = 1$-truncation of Weiss-descent\textup{);} the Weiss-refinement version produces the $E_3$-factorisation algebra $\mathrm{CoHA}(\bY)^{\mathrm{FA}}$ with non-trivial $k \geq 2$-configuration data. Both are load-bearing: the QC version supports the motivic-DT / Kontsevich--Soibelman analysis; the FA version supports the $\PhiFA_3$-stage of the two-stage CY-to-chiral factorisation \textup{(}Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-two-stage-factorisation}\textup{).}
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Ran-space reformulation]
\label{rem:ran-reformulation}
Equivalently, a factorisation algebra on $X$ is a Ran-space cosheaf: a $\cV$-valued cosheaf on the Ran space $\Ran(X) = \colim_I X^I / \mathrm{diag}$ whose fibre at a configuration $\{x_1, \ldots, x_k\} \in \Ran(X)$ is the $k$-fold factorisation product \textup{(}Beilinson--Drinfeld \emph{Chiral Algebras} AMS~$51$, \S$3.4.11$, where the factorisation-Lie equivalence is Theorem~$3.4.9$; Francis--Gaitsgory arXiv:$1103.5803$ Theorem~$1.2.4$, which identifies chiral Lie algebras $\mathrm{Lie}\text{-alg}^{\mathrm{ch}}(\Ran X)$ with factorisation coalgebras $\mathrm{Com}\text{-coalg}^{\mathrm{ch,fact}}(\Ran X) = \mathrm{Fact}(X)$ via the chiral-homology functor $C^{\mathrm{ch}}$ and its left adjoint $\mathrm{Prim}^{\mathrm{ch}}[1]$; Lurie \emph{Higher Algebra} §$5.5.4$\textup{).} The Weiss condition on a cover $\{U_\alpha\}$ of $X$ is the condition that the induced family $\{U_\alpha^{[k]}\}_{k \geq 1}$ of configuration-space opens covers $\mathrm{Ran}(X)$; without it, the configuration-space descent fails at $k \geq 2$.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Homotopy colimits in the $(\infty, 1)$-category, not the $1$-category]
\label{rem:hocolim-inf-cat}
The homotopy colimits in Definition~\ref{def:prefact-weiss-fact} and Proposition~\ref{prop:weiss-refinement-upgrade} are computed in the $(\infty, 1)$-category of $\En$-algebras in $\cV$; equivalently, in the $(2, 1)$-truncation given by the $2$-category with dg functors as $1$-morphisms and natural transformations as $2$-morphisms. A $1$-categorical (strict) coequaliser produces the wrong invariant: the Fourier--Mukai transitions on overlaps of a non-trivial cover carry coherent homotopy data that the strict $1$-colimit discards (Toën--Vezzosi arXiv:math/$0404373$ §$1.4$; Lurie \emph{Higher Algebra} §$5.5.4$). The $(2, 1)$-hocolim records the self-composition $T_{-+} \circ T_{+-} \simeq \mathrm{id}$ as a coherent $2$-cell, not as a strict identity.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Factorisation category variant}
\label{subsec:fact-category-variant}

The same dichotomy categorifies. For a CY category $\cC$ and a $\PhiFA_d$-stage output living on a variety $X$, the descent analysis applies to factorisation \emph{categories} \textup{(}Definition~\ref{def:factorization-category} of Chapter~\ref{ch:braided-factorization}\textup{):} QC-descent through a non-Weiss cover produces a sheaf of dg categories on $X$; Weiss-refinement descent produces an $\En$-factorisation category on $X$. The Kazhdan--Lusztig and Heisenberg factorisation categories of §\ref{sec:factorization-categories} live at the Weiss level; the NCCR-local / BKR-local incarnations \textup{(}Van~den~Bergh arXiv:math/$9908027$; Bridgeland--King--Reid arXiv:math/$0009053$\textup{)} live at the QC level. The categorical $R$-matrix of §\ref{sec:bf-categorical-r-matrix} is the holonomy of the $\Etwo$-factorisation product around the generating loop of $\pi_1(\Conf_2(\R^2)) = \Z$ inside $\Conf_2(C) \setminus \Delta$ \textup{(}Lurie \emph{HA} Example~$5.1.2.4$, Dunn-additivity braiding via Theorem~$5.1.2.2$ and the Eckmann--Hilton isomorphism $X \otimes Y \simeq Y \otimes X$\textup{):} it is a two-point disjoint-configuration datum, visible only at the Weiss level; QC-descent retains $\Conf_1$ only and forgets~$\Conf_{\geq 2}$.


\section{The \texorpdfstring{$E_n$}{E-n}-chiral bar complex}
\label{sec:en-bar}

Construction~\ref{constr:three-bar-complexes} (in Chapter~\ref{ch:e2-chiral}) gives the three bar complexes $B_{\Eone}$, $B_{\Etwo}$, $B_{\Einf}$ for an $\Etwo$-chiral algebra. For a CY$_d$ chiral algebra with $d \geq 4$, only the $\Eone$-bar is native. The $\Etwo$-bar belongs to the derived-centre or Drinfeld-centre object, and the $\Einf$-bar requires a separate commutative refinement.


\section{The framing obstruction tower}
\label{sec:framing-tower}

The $\bS^d$-framing on $\HH_\bullet(\cC)$ requires a trivialization of a certain bundle classified by $\pi_d$ of the structure group of the CY pairing. Two independent computations of this obstruction:

\begin{enumerate}[label=(\Alph*)]
 \item \emph{Unitary path.} The complex structure gives a $U(n)$ reduction. By Bott periodicity (period~$2$ for $BU$):
 \[
 \pi_k(BU) = \begin{cases} \Z & \text{if $k$ is even and $k \geq 2$,} \\ 0 & \text{otherwise.} \end{cases}
 \]
 The $BU$ obstruction is: $\pi_d(BU) = \Z$ for $d$ even, $0$ for $d$ odd.

 \item \emph{Symplectic/orthogonal path.} For $d$ odd, the CY pairing is antisymmetric, giving structure group $\Sp(2m)$. For $d$ even, the pairing is symmetric, giving $O(n)$ (before holomorphic reduction). The stable homotopy groups are given by Bott periodicity (period~$8$):
 \begin{center}
 \renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.1}
 \begin{tabular}{c|cccccccc}
 $k \bmod 8$ & 0 & 1 & 2 & 3 & 4 & 5 & 6 & 7 \\
 \hline
 $\pi_k(\Sp)$ & 0 & 0 & 0 & $\Z$ & $\Z_2$ & $\Z_2$ & 0 & $\Z$ \\
 $\pi_k(O)$ & $\Z_2$ & $\Z_2$ & 0 & $\Z$ & 0 & 0 & 0 & $\Z$
 \end{tabular}
 \end{center}
 The classifying-space obstruction is $\pi_d(B\Sp) = \pi_{d-1}(\Sp)$ for $d$ odd, and $\pi_d(BO) = \pi_{d-1}(O)$ for $d$ even.
\end{enumerate}


\section{The \texorpdfstring{$\En$}{E-n} landscape: the \texorpdfstring{$\Eone$}{E1} stabilization theorem}
\label{sec:e1-stabilization}

\begin{theorem}[\texorpdfstring{$\Eone$}{E1} stabilization for CY chiral algebras]
\label{thm:e1-stabilization-cy}
\noindent\emph{Hypotheses.} The obstruction computation is unconditional. Its application to $A_\cC$ is unconditional at $d = 2$; at $d = 3$ it applies on the witnessed filtered locus of Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}, with the corrected TCFT datum, filtered Hochschild comparison, and admissible specialisation datum fixed; at $d \geq 4$ it assumes CY-A$_d$.
On each such locus with $d \geq 3$, the two-stage construction produces a chiral algebra $A_\cC = \Phi_d(\cC)$ from a smooth CY$_d$ category $\cC$, and $A_\cC$ is an $\Eone$-chiral algebra. The additional shifted structure is classified by the \emph{effective framing obstruction}:
\[
 \Obs_{\mathrm{eff}}(d) \;=\;
 \begin{cases}
 \pi_d(BU) & \text{if $\pi_d(BU) \neq 0$ (primary obstruction nontrivial),} \\
 \pi_d(B\Sp) & \text{if $d$ is odd and $\pi_d(BU) = 0$ (Sp refinement),} \\
 0 & \text{otherwise.}
 \end{cases}
\]
The complete Stage-2 $\En$ landscape for CY$_d$ categories:
\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}
\small
\begin{tabular}{llllll}
 \toprule
 $d$ & $\Phi_d$ output & $\pi_d(BU)$ & $\pi_d(B\Sp)$ & $\Obs_{\mathrm{eff}}$ & Shifted structure \\
 \midrule
 $1$ & $\Etwo$ with $E_\infty$ refinement & $0$ & $0$ & $0$ & none (commutative refinement) \\
 $2$ & $\Etwo$ & $\Z$ & $0$ & $\Z$ & braiding parameter ($c_1$) \\
 $3$ & $\Eone$ & $0$ & $0$ & $0$ & none ($\bS^3$-framing trivial) \\
 $4$ & $\Eone$ & $\Z$ & $\Z$ & $\Z$ & $\Z$-shifted symplectic ($p_1$) \\
 $5$ & $\Eone$ & $0$ & $\Z_2$ & $\Z_2$ & $\Z_2$-shifted ($\Sp$ refinement) \\
 $6$ & $\Eone$ & $\Z$ & $\Z_2$ & $\Z$ & $\Z$-shifted ($c_3$) \\
 $7$ & $\Eone$ & $0$ & $0$ & $0$ & none (trivial, mirrors $d = 3$) \\
 $8$ & $\Eone$ & $\Z$ & $\Z$ & $\Z$ & $\Z$-shifted (Bott-periodic) \\
 \bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The pattern repeats with period~$8$ by Bott periodicity of $\Sp$ and $O$. In the Bott $8$-fold cycle $d \bmod 8 = 1, 2, \ldots, 8$, the effective obstruction is:
\[
 0, \; \Z, \; 0, \; \Z, \; \Z_2, \; \Z, \; 0, \; \Z.
\]
The obstruction is \emph{trivial} precisely at $d \bmod 8 \in \{1, 3, 7\}$. All even $d \geq 2$ have $\Z$-valued obstruction (from $\pi_d(BU) = \Z$).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The proof has three parts.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Part 1: $\Eone$ is the floor for $d \geq 3$.}
The Gerstenhaber bracket on $\HH^\bullet(\cC)$ has degree $1 - d$. For $d \geq 3$, this degree is $\leq -2$: the bracket is too shifted to produce braiding, since $\pi_1(\Conf_2(\R^d)) = 0$ for $d \geq 3$ (ordered configuration spaces of $\R^d$ are simply connected). The native structure is therefore $\Eone$.

For toric CY$_d$ ($\C^d$ with $\GL(d)$ action), a stronger statement holds: the $\GL(d)$-invariant Schouten--Nijenhuis brackets vanish identically (Theorem~\ref{thm:c3-abelian-bracket}, degree overflow and graded skew-symmetry), so the classical Lie conformal algebra is abelian and all noncommutative structure arises from the $\Omega$-deformation. For non-toric CY$_d$ with $d \geq 3$, the bracket need not vanish, but the degree constraint still forces $\Eone$.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Part 2: The framing obstruction.}
The $\bS^d$-framing obstruction lives in $\pi_d$ of the classifying space of the structure group. The CY condition $\omega_X \cong \cO_X$ determines the structure group:
\begin{itemize}
 \item The complex structure reduces $\GL(n, \R)$ to $U(n)$. The obstruction in $\pi_d(BU)$ is $\Z$ for $d$ even and $0$ for $d$ odd (Bott periodicity, period~$2$).
 \item For $d$ odd (antisymmetric CY pairing), the further reduction to $\Sp(2m)$ gives a refined obstruction in $\pi_d(B\Sp) = \pi_{d-1}(\Sp)$. This is nontrivial ($\Z_2$) precisely when $d \equiv 5 \pmod{8}$ (since $\pi_4(\Sp) = \Z_2$).
 \item For $d$ even (symmetric pairing), the orthogonal reduction gives $\pi_d(BO) = \pi_{d-1}(O)$, but the holomorphic structure typically refines back to $BU$, so the $BU$ obstruction is the primary one.
\end{itemize}

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Part 3: The $d = 4$ obstruction.}
At $d = 4$, $\pi_4(BU) = \pi_3(U) = \Z$. The generator is the first Pontryagin class $p_1$. A CY$_4$ category $\cC$ has $p_1(\Ext^\bullet_\cC(E, E)) \in \Z$, which is the obstruction to an $\bS^4$-framing. When $p_1 \neq 0$, the chiral algebra carries a $\Z$-shifted symplectic structure: the factorization algebra structure is $\Eone$ with a choice of integer ``level'' (analogous to the level of a Kac--Moody algebra) determined by $p_1$. For $\C^4$, the flat tangent bundle gives $p_1 = 0$ and hence the untwisted representative.
\end{proof}


\begin{remark}[Why \texorpdfstring{$E_0$}{E-0} is not an extra multiplication]
\label{rem:e0-nonexistence}
The arity-zero little-disks convention \(E_0\) governs pointed
objects, not associative algebras. An \(E_0\)-algebra in chain
complexes is a chain complex with a distinguished element, carrying no
multiplicative structure. The correct interpretation for $d = 4$ is:
the CY chiral algebra is $\Eone$ (with multiplication), and the $p_1$
class provides additional structure \emph{beyond} the operad level: a
shifted symplectic form on the tangent complex of the MC moduli.
\end{remark}


\begin{corollary}[\texorpdfstring{$d = 4$}{d = 4}: the first Pontryagin obstruction]
\label{cor:d4-pontryagin}
\noindent\emph{Proof input.} The obstruction computation proves the topological assertion. Its application to $A_\cC$ assumes CY-A$_4$.
For a CY$_4$ category $\cC$ admitting a chiral algebra $A_\cC$ (open for $d = 4$):
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item The chiral algebra $A_\cC$ is $\Eone$.
 \item The $\bS^4$-framing obstruction is $p_1 \in \pi_4(BU) = \Z$, the first Pontryagin class. All three paths ($BU$, $B\Sp$, $BO$) give $\Z$:
 \[
 \pi_4(BU) = \pi_4(B\Sp) = \pi_4(BO) = \Z.
 \]
 \item The $\Etwo$ braided structure is recovered via the Drinfeld center $\cZ(\Rep^{\Eone}(A_\cC))$, with the half-braiding carrying a $\Z$-valued twisting datum from $p_1$.
 \item The deformation space of $\C^4$ is $2$-dimensional, spanned by $\sigma_3 = \sum_{i<j<k} h_i h_j h_k$ and $\sigma_4 = h_1 h_2 h_3 h_4$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Parts~(i) and~(ii) follow from Theorem~\ref{thm:e1-stabilization-cy} at $d = 4$. For part~(ii), the three paths:
\begin{itemize}
 \item $\pi_4(BU) = \pi_3(U) = \Z$ (Bott: $3$ is odd, $\pi_3(U) = \Z$).
 \item $\pi_4(B\Sp) = \pi_3(\Sp) = \Z$ (the generator is the symplectic structure on $\Sp(2m)$).
 \item $\pi_4(BO) = \pi_3(O) = \Z$ (the generator is the spin structure).
\end{itemize}
All three agree, giving a well-defined $\Z$-valued obstruction.

Part~(iii): the Drinfeld center construction of Theorem~\ref{thm:c3-drinfeld-center} generalizes from $d = 3$ to $d = 4$ as a construction on \(\Rep^{\Eone}(A_\cC)\), not as an \(\Etwo\)-operation on \(A_\cC\). The half-braiding on Fock modules of the CY$_4$ CoHA carries a $\Z$-grading from $p_1$.

Part~(iv): the equivariant parameters $h_1, \ldots, h_4$ satisfy $h_1 + h_2 + h_3 + h_4 = 0$ (CY condition), leaving $3$ free parameters. The elementary symmetric polynomials $\sigma_2, \sigma_3, \sigma_4$ parametrize the deformation space; $\sigma_2$ generates a trivial deformation (rescaling), leaving $\sigma_3$ and $\sigma_4$ as the $2$ effective parameters.
\end{proof}


\begin{corollary}[\texorpdfstring{$d = 5$}{d = 5}: the \texorpdfstring{$\Z_2$}{Z-2} Sp-refinement]
\label{cor:d5-z2}
\noindent\emph{Proof input.} The obstruction computation proves the topological assertion. Its application to $A_\cC$ assumes CY-A$_5$.
For a CY$_5$ category $\cC$ admitting a chiral algebra $A_\cC$ (open for $d = 5$):
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item The chiral algebra $A_\cC$ is $\Eone$.
 \item The primary framing obstruction $\pi_5(BU) = 0$ is trivial ($5$ is odd).
 \item The refined obstruction $\pi_5(B\Sp) = \pi_4(\Sp) = \Z_2$ is \emph{nontrivial}. The $\bS^5$-framing carries a $\Z_2$-valued invariant from the symplectic structure group.
 \item The $\Z_2$-shifted structure manifests as a super-$\Eone$ grading: the chiral algebra has a $\Z_2$-grading compatible with the $\Eone$-operad structure, arising from the mod-$2$ Pontryagin class.
 \item The deformation space of $\C^5$ is $3$-dimensional ($\sigma_3, \sigma_4, \sigma_5$).
\end{enumerate}
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Parts~(i)--(iii) follow from Theorem~\ref{thm:e1-stabilization-cy} at $d = 5$, using the Bott periodicity table: $\pi_4(\Sp) = \Z_2$ (verified independently via the computation $\pi_4(\Sp) = \pi_4(\Sp(4)) = \Z_2$ in the stable range).

Part~(iv) is a consequence of the $\Z_2$-valued obstruction: the half-braiding in the Drinfeld center of \(\Rep^{\Eone}(A_\cC)\) carries a $\Z_2$-grading. The algebra \(A_\cC\) itself remains \(\Eone\).

Part~(v): the equivariant parameters $h_1, \ldots, h_5$ with $\sum h_i = 0$ give $4$ free parameters, with $\sigma_2$ trivial, leaving $\sigma_3, \sigma_4, \sigma_5$.
\end{proof}


\begin{corollary}[\texorpdfstring{$d = 7$}{d = 7} mirrors \texorpdfstring{$d = 3$}{d = 3}; \texorpdfstring{$d = 8$}{d = 8} mirrors \texorpdfstring{$d = 4$}{d = 4}]
\label{cor:d7-d8-bott}
\noindent\emph{Proof input.} The obstruction computation proves the topological assertion. Its application to $A_\cC$ assumes CY-A$_d$ for $d \geq 4$; the $d=3$ case is proved.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item At $d = 7$: the framing obstruction is trivial. Both $\pi_7(BU) = 0$ and $\pi_7(B\Sp) = \pi_6(\Sp) = 0$. The CY$_7$ chiral algebra is unobstructed $\Eone$, mirroring $d = 3$.
 \item At $d = 8$: the framing obstruction is $\pi_8(BU) = \Z$ (Bott-periodic, corresponding to $d = 0$ shifted by the $8$-fold periodicity of $B\Sp$). The CY$_8$ chiral algebra carries a $\Z$-shifted structure, mirroring $d = 4$.
\end{enumerate}
More generally, $d$ and $d + 8$ have isomorphic framing obstruction groups (Bott periodicity).
\end{corollary}


\section{Explicit computations for \texorpdfstring{$d = 4$}{d = 4} varieties}
\label{sec:d4-explicit}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$\C^4$}{C-4}}
\label{subsec:c4}

The simplest CY$_4$ is $\C^4$. The $\GL(4)$-invariant polyvector fields $\PV^p(\C^4)$ have $\dim \PV^p = \binom{4}{p}$ in the constant-coefficient sector:
\[
 \dim(\PV^0, \PV^1, \PV^2, \PV^3, \PV^4) = (1, 4, 6, 4, 1), \qquad \dim_{\mathrm{total}} = 2^4 = 16.
\]
The Schouten--Nijenhuis brackets on the $\GL(4)$-invariant sector vanish for the same reasons as $d = 3$ (degree overflow and graded skew-symmetry). The Bogomolov--Tian--Todorov theorem gives $\HH^3 = 0$ (unobstructedness).

The $2$-dimensional deformation space is spanned by
\[
 \sigma_3 = \sum_{i < j < k} h_i h_j h_k, \qquad \sigma_4 = h_1 h_2 h_3 h_4,
\]
subject to $h_1 + h_2 + h_3 + h_4 = 0$. At the self-dual point (one $h_i = 0$, say $h_4 = 0$), the CY$_4$ functor reduces to the CY$_3$ functor for $\C^3$ with parameters $(h_1, h_2, h_3)$, and the chiral algebra is $\cW_{1+\infty}$ at $c = 1$ (the Heisenberg VOA $H_1$, $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 1$).


\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$K3 \times K3$}{K3 x K3}}
\label{subsec:k3xk3}

The product $K3 \times K3$ is a CY$_4$ with Euler characteristic
\[
 \chi(K3 \times K3) = \chi(K3)^2 = 24^2 = 576
\]
and Hodge numbers computed by K\"unneth from the K3 diamond. The key Hodge numbers:
$h^{1,1} = 40$, $h^{2,0} = h^{0,2} = 2$, $h^{4,0} = h^{0,4} = 1$.

The expected chiral algebra is the tensor product of two copies of the K3 Mukai lattice VOA. The reasoning: the CY-to-chiral correspondence $\Phi_4$ should respect products (conjectural functoriality, see rem:phi-not-unified-functor and conj:phi-d-functoriality), and $D^b(\mathrm{Coh}(K3 \times K3)) \simeq D^b(\mathrm{Coh}(K3)) \boxtimes D^b(\mathrm{Coh}(K3))$ by K\"unneth for derived categories, so the chiral algebra should factor accordingly. Conditional on CY-A$_4$ (open), this gives:
\[
 A_{K3 \times K3} = V_{\Lambda_{K3}} \otimes V_{\Lambda_{K3}},
\]
where $\Lambda_{K3}$ is the Mukai lattice of rank~$24$ (the full lattice $H^*(K3, \Z)$, not the middle cohomology lattice of rank~$22$). By Vol~I additivity (Theorem~D) and the lattice VOA formula $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(V_\Lambda) = \mathrm{rank}(\Lambda)$, the expected modular characteristic is
\[
 \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_{K3 \times K3}) = \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(V_{\Lambda_{K3}}) + \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(V_{\Lambda_{K3}}) = 24 + 24 = 48.
\]
The shadow obstruction tower is class~$\mathbf{G}$ (Gaussian, $r_{\max} = 2$) since the lattice VOA is a free-field algebra. All statements in this subsection are conditional on the existence of CY-A$_4$.


\subsection{The sextic \texorpdfstring{$4$}{4}-fold in \texorpdfstring{$\bP^5$}{P-5}}
\label{subsec:sextic}

The degree-$6$ smooth hypersurface $X_6 \subset \bP^5$ is a compact CY$_4$ with $h^{3,1} = 426$, $h^{2,2} = 1752$, $h^{1,1} = 1$, and $\chi(X_6) = 2610$. The holomorphic Euler characteristic is $\chi(\cO_{X_6}) = \sum_{p=0}^{4} (-1)^p h^{p,0} = 1 - 0 + 0 - 0 + 1 = 2$ (using $h^{0,0} = h^{4,0} = 1$ and $h^{1,0} = h^{2,0} = h^{3,0} = 0$).


\subsection{The \texorpdfstring{$2$}{2}-dimensional deformation space and the \texorpdfstring{$\sigma_4$}{sigma-4} invariant}
\label{subsec:cy4-deformation-space}

A key structural difference between $d = 3$ and $d = 4$ is the dimension of the effective $\Omega$-deformation space. For $\C^d$ with equivariant parameters $h_1, \ldots, h_d$ subject to $\sum h_i = 0$ (the CY condition), the elementary symmetric polynomials $\sigma_2, \ldots, \sigma_d$ parameterize the deformation space. Since $\sigma_2$ generates a trivial (rescaling) deformation, the effective dimension is $d - 2$: one parameter ($\sigma_3$) at $d = 3$, and two parameters ($\sigma_3, \sigma_4$) at $d = 4$.

\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}
\begin{tabular}{lccl}
 \toprule
 $d$ & Free params & Effective params & Generators \\
 \midrule
 $3$ & $2$ & $1$ & $\sigma_3 = h_1 h_2 h_3$ \\
 $4$ & $3$ & $2$ & $\sigma_3 = \sum_{i<j<k} h_i h_j h_k$, \; $\sigma_4 = h_1 h_2 h_3 h_4$ \\
 $5$ & $4$ & $3$ & $\sigma_3, \sigma_4, \sigma_5$ \\
 \bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

The projective ratio $[\sigma_3 : \sigma_4] \in \bP^1$ parameterizes a family of CY$_4$ chiral algebras. Three distinguished points:
\begin{itemize}
 \item $[1 : 0]$ (the \emph{CY$_3$ boundary}): $\sigma_4 = 0$, one $h_i = 0$, and the algebra reduces to the CY$_3$ chiral algebra $\cW_{1+\infty}$ with parameters $(h_1, h_2, h_3)$.
 \item $[0 : 1]$ (the \emph{purely quartic locus}): $\sigma_3 = 0$, $h = (a, a, -a, -a)$ for some $a$. The $A_\infty$-operation $m_3 = 0$ (since $m_3 \propto \sigma_3$), and the first noncommutative correction is $m_4 \propto \sigma_4$. The shadow class is~$\mathbf{G}$ (Gaussian) at this locus.
 \item Generic $[\sigma_3 : \sigma_4]$: both parameters nonzero, the algebra is conjecturally class~$\mathbf{L}$ (rational).
\end{itemize}


\subsection{The \texorpdfstring{$p_1$}{p-1}-twisted Drinfeld center and the CY\texorpdfstring{$_4$}{-4} ``Yangian'' question}
\label{subsec:cy4-twisted-center}

For CY$_3$: the Drinfeld center $\cZ(\Rep^{\Eone}(A))$ is $\Etwo$-braided with an \emph{untwisted} half-braiding, since $\pi_3(BU) = 0$ (trivial framing obstruction). The resulting braided monoidal category is the representation category of the affine Yangian $Y(\hat{\gl}_1)$.

For CY$_4$: the framing obstruction $\pi_4(BU) = \Z$ is nontrivial, generated by $p_1$. This modifies the Drinfeld center construction: the half-braiding carries a $\Z$-valued twist parameterized by $p_1(TX) \in \Z$.

\begin{conjecture}[\texorpdfstring{$p_1$}{p-1}-twisted Drinfeld center at \texorpdfstring{$d = 4$}{d = 4}]
\label{conj:cy4-twisted-center}
For a CY$_4$ category $\cC$ admitting a chiral algebra $A_\cC$ (conditional on CY-A$_4$):
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item The Drinfeld center $\cZ(\Rep^{\Eone}(A_\cC))$ is $\Etwo$-braided with a $\Z$-twisted half-braiding. The twist is parameterized by $p_1(T\cC)$.
 \item The $R$-matrix of the braided category satisfies a \emph{twisted Yang--Baxter equation}: the standard YBE acquires $p_1$-dependent correction terms.
 \item At $p_1 = 0$ (e.g., $\C^4$), the twisted center reduces to the untwisted CY$_3$ Drinfeld center.
 \item The correct algebraic structure is a $p_1$-twisted double current algebra, distinct from a Yangian. In particular, at generic compact CY$_4$ with nonvanishing $p_1(T_X) \in H^4(X; \Z)$, no native CY$_4$ Yangian exists: the spectral-parameter shift symmetry is broken by the $p_1$ twist. At the flat point $p_1(T_X) = 0$ (e.g. $\C^4$, or the abelian CY$_4$ $\C^4 / \Lambda$, or any product $K3 \times T^4$ where the K3 factor contributes $p_1$ in a direction whose square already vanishes by dimension) the obstruction class degenerates to zero and the construction recovers the CY$_3$ untwisted case per clause (iii). The nonvanishing of $\pi_4(BU) = \Z$ supplies the obstruction group; the obstruction class is nontrivial precisely when $[p_1(T_X)] \neq 0$ in $H^4(X; \Z)$, which is the generic case for compact CY$_4$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{conjecture}

The Pontryagin class values for the CY$_4$ landscape:
\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}
\begin{tabular}{lccl}
 \toprule
 CY$_4$ & $c_2$ & $p_1 = -2c_2$ & Twist \\
 \midrule
 $\C^4$ & $0$ & $0$ & untwisted \\
 $K3 \times K3$ & $48$ & $-96$ & $\Z$-twisted \\
 $X_6 \subset \bP^5$ & $15 H^2$ & $-30 H^2$ & $\Z$-twisted \\
 \bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}


\subsection{The \texorpdfstring{$\En$}{E-n} cascade at \texorpdfstring{$d = 4$}{d = 4}: same maximum as \texorpdfstring{$d = 3$}{d = 3}}
\label{subsec:cy4-cascade}

The derived centre cascade attached to a CY$_4$ output proceeds as a
centre construction on the \(\Eone\)-algebra \(A_\cC\):
\[
 A_\cC
 \xrightarrow{\;\cZ(\Rep^{\Eone}(-))\; \text{or}\; Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}\;}
 \Etwo
 \xrightarrow{\;\text{derived centre (higher Deligne)}\;}
 E_3
 \xrightarrow{\;\text{iterated centre}\;}
 E_4_{\mathrm{alg}} .
\]
The crucial observation: the native CY$_4$ chiral algebra remains
\(\Eone\), despite CY$_4$ having four complex directions at Stage~1.
The fourth direction contributes a $\Z$-valued twist (from $p_1$)
rather than a fourth \(\Eone\)-factor on \(A_\cC\). The \(E_4\) term is
algebraic deformation theory of an iterated centre, not a value of
\(\Phi_4\).

\begin{proposition}[\texorpdfstring{$E_3$}{E-3} is the cascade maximum at \texorpdfstring{$d = 4$}{d = 4}]
\label{prop:e3-max-cy4}
\noindent\emph{Hypotheses.} Assume CY-A$_4$.
For a CY$_4$ chiral algebra $A_\cC$:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item Step~$1$: $\cZ(\Rep^{\Eone}(A_\cC)) \cong \Rep^{\Etwo}(Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_\cC))$, with the $\Etwo$ braiding carrying a $\Z$-twist from $p_1$.
 \item Step~$2$: $\HH^*(Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_\cC), Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_\cC))$ is $E_3$ by the higher Deligne conjecture. The $p_1$ twist propagates from Step~$1$.
 \item Termination: the \(E_4\) object is an iterated centre. The obstruction $\pi_4(BU) = \Z$ prevents the fourth complex direction from becoming a native operation on \(A_\cC\); it contributes a $\Z$-valued twist instead.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}
\begin{remark}[The \texorpdfstring{$d = 4$}{d = 4} deformation space and the \texorpdfstring{$p_1$}{p-1}-twisted Drinfeld center]
\label{rem:cy4-deformation-space}
The CY$_4$ $\Omega$-deformation has a \emph{two-dimensional} effective parameter space, in contrast to the one-dimensional CY$_3$ case. The equivariant parameters $(h_1, h_2, h_3, h_4)$ with $h_1 + h_2 + h_3 + h_4 = 0$ (CY condition) give elementary symmetric polynomials $\sigma_1 = 0$, $\sigma_2$ (trivial rescaling), $\sigma_3$, and $\sigma_4 = h_1 h_2 h_3 h_4$. The effective deformation parameters are $(\sigma_3, \sigma_4)$; the projective ratio $[\sigma_3 : \sigma_4] \in \mathbb{P}^1$ parameterises a family of deformations. At the boundary $\sigma_4 = 0$ (one $h_i$ vanishes), the CY$_4$ deformation reduces to a CY$_3$ deformation: $h_4 = 0$ gives $(h_1, h_2, h_3)$ with $h_1 + h_2 + h_3 = 0$.

The Drinfeld center $\cZ(\Rep^{\Eone}(A_\cC))$ at $d = 4$ is \emph{$\Z$-twisted}: the half-braiding $\sigma_{V,W} \colon V \otimes W \to W \otimes V$ satisfies the hexagon axiom, but the natural isomorphism carries a phase depending on the first Pontryagin class $p_1 \in \pi_4(BU) = \Z$ (Corollary~\ref{cor:d4-pontryagin}). For compact CY$_4$ manifolds: $p_1 = -2c_2$ (since $c_1 = 0$). In the landscape: $p_1(\mathbb{C}^4) = 0$ (flat, untwisted), $p_1(K3 \times K3) = -96$ (product formula: $-48 + (-48)$), and $p_1(X_6) = -30 H^2$ for the sextic in $\mathbb{P}^5$. At $p_1 = 0$ the twist vanishes and the Drinfeld center reduces to the CY$_3$ untwisted case, but the $\Eone$-stabilisation (Theorem~\ref{thm:e1-stabilization-cy}) still applies; the Drinfeld center is needed to recover $\Etwo$ even for flat targets.
\end{remark}

\begin{conjecture}[The \texorpdfstring{$d = 4$}{d = 4} \texorpdfstring{$\En$}{E-n} stabilisation: \texorpdfstring{$p_1$}{p-1}-twisted algebras and shadow tower]
\label{conj:cy4-en-stabilisation}
For a CY$_4$ chiral algebra $A_\cC$ (conditional on CY-A$_4$):
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{No native CY$_4$ Yangian.} The CY$_3$ Yangian $Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ arises from the CoHA of $\mathbb{C}^3$ via the Drinfeld center (untwisted, $\pi_3(BU) = 0$). At $d = 4$, the CoHA of $\mathbb{C}^4$ is $\Eone$ (associative Hall product) and the Drinfeld center gives $\Etwo$, but with a $\Z$-twisted half-braiding from $p_1$. The $R$-matrix acquires $p_1$-dependent correction terms that prevent it from satisfying the standard Yang--Baxter equation. The correct algebraic structure is a \emph{$p_1$-twisted double current algebra}, a deformation of $Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ classified by the $2$-dimensional Hochschild cohomology $\HH^2(Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1)) \cong \mathbb{C}^2$ (matching the effective deformation space $(\sigma_3, \sigma_4)$). At the self-dual boundary $\sigma_4 = 0$, this reduces to $Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$.

 \item \emph{Shadow tower at $d = 4$.} The shadow class depends on the locus in $\mathbb{P}^1$:
 \begin{center}
 \small
 \renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.15}
 \begin{tabular}{llll}
 \toprule
 Locus & $\sigma_3$ & $\sigma_4$ & Shadow class \\
 \midrule
 Self-dual ($h_4 = 0$) & $\neq 0$ & $0$ & $L$ (reduces to CY$_3$) \\
 $S_4$-symmetric ($h = (1,1,-1,-1)$) & $0$ & $\neq 0$ & $G$ (purely quartic) \\
 Generic & $\neq 0$ & $\neq 0$ & $L$ (conjectural) \\
 \bottomrule
 \end{tabular}
 \end{center}
 At the $S_4$-symmetric point $\sigma_3 = 0$: the cubic $A_\infty$ operation $m_3$ vanishes (it is proportional to $\sigma_3$), the first noncommutative correction is $m_4$ (proportional to $\sigma_4$), and the shadow tower terminates at depth~$2$ (class~$G$). The conjectural class~$L$ at generic $(\sigma_3, \sigma_4)$ follows from upper semicontinuity of the shadow class hierarchy $G < L < C < M$ under smooth deformation.

 \item \emph{New shadow invariant $S_4^{\mathrm{new}}$.} The CY$_4$ shadow tower admits a conjectural new invariant
 \[
 S_4^{\mathrm{new}} \;=\; \frac{2\,\sigma_4}{\sigma_3} \qquad (\sigma_3 \neq 0),
 \]
 parameterising the deviation from the CY$_3$ shadow tower. At $\sigma_4 = 0$: $S_4^{\mathrm{new}} = 0$ (CY$_3$ recovery). At the $S_4$-symmetric point: $\sigma_3 = 0$ and the invariant is undefined (the algebra jumps to class~$G$). The projective ratio $[\sigma_3 : \sigma_4]$ is the natural coordinate on $\mathbb{P}^1$, and $S_4^{\mathrm{new}}$ is the affine coordinate on the complement of $[\sigma_3 : 0]$.

 \item \emph{Cascade termination mechanism.} The derived centre cascade $\Eone \to \Etwo \to E_3$ is a centre construction on \(A_\cC\) for \emph{all} \(d\geq3\), not the native operadic level of \(A_\cC\) (Proposition~\ref{prop:e3-max-cy4}, Proposition~\ref{prop:cascade-termination}). At $d = 4$, $E_4 \cong \Eone^{\otimes 4}$ (Dunn additivity) would be an iterated-centre deformation object. Although CY$_4$ provides four complex directions at Stage~1, the Stage~2 algebra remains \(\Eone\); the fourth direction contributes a \(\Z\)-valued twist (the \(p_1\) class), not an \(\Etwo\)-structure on \(A_\cC\). The distinction is between a deformation object attached to the centre and the chiral algebra produced by \(\Phi_4\).
\end{enumerate}
\end{conjecture}


\section{The \texorpdfstring{$d = 5$}{d = 5} functor: \texorpdfstring{$\Z_2$}{Z-2} obstruction from \texorpdfstring{$\Sp$}{Sp}}
\label{sec:d5-functor}

The CY$_5$ functor introduces a genuinely new phenomenon: a $\Z_2$-valued framing obstruction that is invisible to the unitary structure group but visible to the symplectic refinement.

For $\C^5$: the polyvector field dimensions are $\dim \PV^p = \binom{5}{p}$, giving total dimension $2^5 = 32$. The deformation space is $3$-dimensional ($\sigma_3, \sigma_4, \sigma_5$).

The framing obstruction:
\begin{itemize}
 \item $\pi_5(BU) = 0$ (primary, from unitary structure: $5$ is odd).
 \item $\pi_5(B\Sp) = \pi_4(\Sp) = \Z_2$ (refined, from symplectic structure).
\end{itemize}
The discrepancy between the $BU$ and $B\Sp$ obstructions means that the chain-level $\bS^5$-framing is topologically trivializable at the unitary level, but the symplectic refinement carries a $\Z_2$-valued obstruction. This obstruction is the mod-$2$ analogue of the first Pontryagin class at $d = 4$.


\section{The \texorpdfstring{$d = 6$}{d = 6} case and higher}
\label{sec:d6-and-higher}

\begin{remark}[\texorpdfstring{$d = 6$}{d = 6}: \texorpdfstring{$BU$}{BU} vs \texorpdfstring{$BO$}{BO} discrepancy]
\label{rem:d6-discrepancy}
At $d = 6$: $\pi_6(BU) = \Z$ (nontrivial) but $\pi_6(BO) = \pi_5(O) = 0$ (trivial). The real orthogonal structure group sees no obstruction, but the complex/holomorphic structure (captured by $BU$) does. This means the $\Z$-valued obstruction at $d = 6$ is purely holomorphic: it is visible only through the complex structure of the CY category, not through the underlying real topology.

The characteristic class is $c_3 \in H^6(B; \Z)$, the third Chern class of the tangent bundle. For a CY$_6$ manifold $X$ with $c_3(X) \neq 0$, the chiral algebra carries a $\Z$-shifted structure indexed by $c_3$.
\end{remark}


\begin{conjecture}[\texorpdfstring{$\En$}{E-n}-Koszul duality at \texorpdfstring{$d \geq 4$}{d >= 4}]
\label{conj:en-koszul-higher}
For a CY$_d$ chiral algebra $A_\cC$ with $d \geq 4$, the Koszul dual $A_\cC^! = \Phi_d(\cC^!)$ (the chiral algebra of the mirror category; the correspondence $\Phi_d$ is $d$-indexed, not a unified functor--see \texttt{rem:phi-not-unified-functor} and \texttt{conj:phi-d-functoriality}) satisfies:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item The framing obstruction of $A_\cC^!$ is the \emph{negation} (in the obstruction group) of the framing obstruction of $A_\cC$.
 \item For $d = 4$ ($\Z$-valued obstruction): $p_1(A_\cC^!) = -p_1(A_\cC)$. The Koszul dual carries the opposite Pontryagin class.
 \item For $d = 5$ ($\Z_2$-valued obstruction): $\Obs(A_\cC^!) = \Obs(A_\cC)$ (since $-1 = 1$ in $\Z_2$). The $\Z_2$-shifted structure is \emph{self-dual}: mirror symmetry preserves it.
 \item The modular characteristics satisfy $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_\cC) + \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_\cC^!) = 0$ (for the free-field case) or $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_\cC) + \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_\cC^!) = \rho_K$ (for $W$-algebra-type families), matching Vol~I Theorem~D.
\end{enumerate}
\end{conjecture}


\section{Relation to topological field theory}
\label{sec:en-tft}

The $\Eone$ stabilization theorem has a direct TFT interpretation. A CY$_d$ category determines a $d$-dimensional topological field theory (Costello--Li~2016). For $d \geq 3$, the TFT is ``fully extended'' down to the $\Eone$ level: it assigns categories to $(d-1)$-manifolds, functors to cobordisms, and natural transformations to higher cobordisms. The $\En$ level of the chiral algebra is the $\En$ level at which the fully extended TFT ``stops'':

\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}
\begin{tabular}{llll}
 \toprule
 $d$ & $\En$ level & TFT stops at & Obstruction \\
 \midrule
 $1$ & $\Etwo$ with $E_\infty$ refinement & fully local & none \\
 $2$ & $\Etwo$ & 2-categorical & $c_1 \in \Z$ \\
 $3$ & $\Eone$ & categorical & none \\
 $4$ & $\Eone$ & categorical & $p_1 \in \Z$ \\
 $5$ & $\Eone$ & categorical & $\Z_2$ from $\Sp$ \\
 $\geq 6$ & $\Eone$ & categorical & Bott-periodic \\
 \bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

For $d\geq3$, the passage from the $\Eone$ output to the first $\Etwo$ braided object is achieved through the Drinfeld or derived centre:
\[
 \cZ(\Rep^{\Eone}(A)) \simeq
 \Rep^{\Etwo}(Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}(A)).
\]
For $d\leq2$, the \(\Etwo\)-structure belongs to \(\Phi_d(\cC)\) itself.

\section{\texorpdfstring{$E_3$}{E-3}-chiral structure from holomorphic Chern--Simons on \texorpdfstring{$\C^3$}{C-3}}
\label{sec:e3-from-hcs}

The $\Eone$ stabilization theorem (Theorem~\ref{thm:e1-stabilization-cy}) concerns the CY-to-chiral correspondence programme $\{\Phi_d\}$ and shows that $d \geq 3$ produces at most $\Eone$-chiral algebras. A distinct source of higher $\En$ structure exists in the holomorphic Chern--Simons programme of Costello--Gwilliam--Li, where the $\En$ level is set by the \emph{complex dimension of the ambient space}, not by the CY dimension of the category. The ambient $E_3$ structure on $\C^3$ does not contradict $\Eone$ stabilization: it is a structure on the \emph{observables of the field theory}, not on the CY chiral algebra $\Phi_d(\cC)$. Costello--Francis--Gwilliam~\cite{CFG2026} supplies the ordinary $3$d Chern--Simons factorisation-homology analogue, not the $6$d hCS-to-Hall comparison theorem.

\begin{theorem}[Finite DWR/Ran Rees \texorpdfstring{$\hCS$}{hCS}--Hall compatibility]
\label{thm:finite-dwr-ran-rees-hcs-hall-compatibility}
Let $X$ be a smooth CY$_3$ equipped with a DWR-good finite Ran atlas
$\mathfrak U$, finite bounds $(N,r,L,m)$ on holomorphic jets,
renormalisation order, charge, and Ran arity, and Joyce--Kontsevich
orientation data.  For every simplex
\(\sigma\in\mathsf N^{\Ran}_{\le m}(\mathfrak U)\), let
\(\Obs_{\hCS}^{q,\le r}(\sigma)\) be the finite hCS observable complex
and let
\(\mathrm{Hall}^{\mathrm{Rees},\mathrm{or},\le N,\le L}_{\sigma}\)
be the oriented Rees critical Hall complex with its vanishing-cycle
filtration.  Assume the face-compatible cyclic contractions of
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-rees-bar-ce-dwr-ran}.  Then there is a
face-compatible multiplicative natural transformation of finite
filtered \(\Eone\)-complexes
\[
 \Theta_{\hCS\to\mathrm{Hall}}^{\mathrm{Rees};N,r,L,m}\colon
 \Tot_{\sigma\in\mathsf N^{\Ran}_{\le m}}
 \mathrm{Rees}_{F}^{\le N,\le L}B\Obs_{\hCS}^{q,\le r}(\sigma)
 \longrightarrow
 \Tot_{\sigma\in\mathsf N^{\Ran}_{\le m}}
 \mathrm{Hall}^{\mathrm{Rees},\mathrm{or},\le N,\le L}_{\sigma}.
\]
It commutes with Ran face maps, disjoint-union factorisation products,
and wall-crossing transition maps.  Its fibre at the Rees parameter
\(\hbar=1\) is the finite hCS-to-Hall comparison; its fibre at
\(\hbar=0\) is the associated-graded comparison between the bar--CE
model of hCS observables and the critical Hall complex.  The map is
\(\Eone\)-multiplicative.  It creates no native \(\Etwo\)-structure on
\(\Phi_3(\cC)\); the first \(\Etwo\)-object remains
\(Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}(\Phi_3(\cC))\) or
\(\cZ(\Rep^{\Eone}(\Phi_3(\cC)))\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
On a DWR/Ran simplex \(\sigma\), cyclic homological perturbation
transfers the finite hCS BV complex to a cyclic \(L_\infty\)-model
\(L_\sigma\).  The factorisation envelope satisfies the costalk
bar--CE identification
\[
 B(\Env^{\mathrm{fact}}(L_\sigma))_x\simeq \CE(L_{\sigma,x}),
 \qquad x\in\Ran(C),
\]
with charge and bar-length filtrations preserved.  The finite Rees
functor is exact on strict filtered maps, so this quasi-isomorphism
persists after applying
\(\mathrm{Rees}_{F}^{\le N,\le L}\).

The Fourier--BV transform for the cyclic pairing identifies
\(\CE(L_{\sigma,x})\) with the oriented critical Hall model: the
polyvector differential
\[
 d_{\CE}+\iota_{dW_\sigma}+\hbar\Delta_{\omega_\sigma}
\]
is carried to the Rees de Rham differential
\(\hbar d+dW_\sigma\wedge\), with the orientation line only shifting
the same complex.  This is the local zig-zag of
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-rees-bar-ce-dwr-ran}.

Face compatibility of the contractions makes the zig-zags relative
over \(\Omega^\bullet(\Delta^p)\).  Stokes' formula identifies the
relative de Rham differential with the alternating face differential
in the DWR/Ran totalisation.  Multiplicativity follows from
Thom--Sebastiani:
\[
 W_{\sigma_1\sqcup\sigma_2}=W_{\sigma_1}\boxplus W_{\sigma_2},
 \qquad
 \DR_{W_{\sigma_1},\hbar}\widehat\otimes
 \DR_{W_{\sigma_2},\hbar}
 \cong
 \DR_{W_{\sigma_1}\boxplus W_{\sigma_2},\hbar},
\]
which is exactly the compatibility of disjoint Ran configurations with
the Hall product.  The nerve and all filtrations are finite in
\((N,r,L,m)\); hence objectwise filtered quasi-isomorphisms induce a
filtered natural transformation on the total complex.  The fibres at
\(\hbar=1\) and \(\hbar=0\) are the defining fibres of the Rees
construction.  Every object in the construction is associative at the
chiral output level, so the comparison is \(\Eone\)-multiplicative;
	the Deligne or Drinfeld centre is a separate operation applied after
	the Stage-2 algebra has been formed.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Finite positive-half scope]
\label{rem:finite-rees-hcs-hall-positive-half-scope}
Theorem~\ref{thm:finite-dwr-ran-rees-hcs-hall-compatibility} constructs a finite Rees positive-half comparison. It does not construct a compact critical CoHA, a PBW/no-extra-relations theorem, a negative half, a non-degenerate Hopf pairing, a Hall--Drinfeld double, or a BKM algebra for compact $K3\times E$. Those are separate compact algebraization inputs; Dunn additivity and finite Rees maps do not promote \(\Phi_3(\cC)\) to a global quantum group \(G(\cC)\).
\end{remark}

\subsection{The \texorpdfstring{$\En$}{E-n} hierarchy from theory dimension}
\label{subsec:en-theory-dimension}

The Costello--Gwilliam--Li factorisation-observable framework assigns to a perturbative holomorphic field theory on an $n$-dimensional complex manifold $M$ a factorisation algebra $\cF$ on $M$. The topological shadow of $\cF$ (forgetting holomorphy) is organized by the real dimension, while the holomorphic refinement records one chiral direction per complex coordinate. In the CY$_3$ bridge this produces a holomorphic $E_3$ factorisation-algebra structure before any curve specialisation; it does not by itself identify the resulting observables with a Hall algebra.

\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}
\small
\begin{tabular}{lllll}
 \toprule
 CS dimension & Ambient $M$ & Topological $\En$ & Holomorphic $\En$ & Projection to $C$ \\
 \midrule
 3d & $C \times \R$ & $E_2$ (on $\R^2$) & $\Eone$-chiral on $C$ & $\Eone$-chiral \\
 5d & $\C^2 \times \R$ & $E_4$ (on $\R^4$) & $\Etwo$-chiral on $\C^2$ & $\Eone$-chiral \\
 6d & $\C^3$ & $E_6$ (on $\R^6$) & $E_3$-chiral on $\C^3$ & $\Eone$-chiral \\
 \bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent The holomorphic level is half the topological level: each complex direction in $M$ contributes $E_1$ rather than $E_2$, because the holomorphic constraint pins the factorization to a single chiral slice (Proposition~\ref{prop:holomorphic-e1}, Chapter~\ref{ch:e1-chiral}). The $\Eone$-chiral algebra on a curve $C \subset M$ is obtained by projecting from $E_n$ to $\Eone$ via the $n-1$ transverse complex directions; the $R$-matrix data of these transverse directions is remembered by the higher $\En$ structure and lost in the projection.

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$E_3$}{E-3}-chiral factorization on \texorpdfstring{$\C^3$}{C-3}: precise conditions}
\label{subsec:e3-precise}

The $E_3$ structure on $\C^3$ arises from two independent sources, which must not be conflated.

\begin{proposition}[Algebraic \texorpdfstring{$E_3$}{E-3} from the higher Deligne conjecture]
\label{prop:algebraic-e3-deligne}
The boundary chiral algebra $A = V_k(\frakg)$ of 3d holomorphic CS on $C \times \R$ is $E_\infty$-chiral (commutative vertex algebra). By the higher Deligne conjecture (Lurie \cite{LurieHA}, Theorem~5.3.1.30), the Hochschild cochains $\HH^\bullet(A, A)$ of an $E_n$-algebra carry $E_{n+1}$ structure. For $A$ an $E_\infty$-algebra, $\HH^\bullet(A, A)$ carries $E_\infty$ structure; the specific $E_3$ subalgebra generated by the cup product, the Gerstenhaber bracket, and the BV operator (from the $S^1$-action on the cyclic bar complex) is the relevant one for the comparison with the topological $E_3$ below.

\emph{Important scope restriction}: the algebraic $E_3$ here (via the higher Deligne conjecture on Hochschild cochains) is a priori distinct from the topological $E_3$ arising from $\Conf_n(\C^3)$ plus Sugawara at non-critical level; the two agree only rationally under formality. The specific algebraic $E_3$ described above (generated by the cup product, the Gerstenhaber bracket, and the BV operator) requires the input $A$ to be $E_\infty$ (commutative), because the BV operator arises from the $S^1$-action on the cyclic bar complex, which requires commutativity. For $\Eone$ input (e.g.\ the CoHA or a labeled-ordered bar algebra), the higher Deligne conjecture gives only $\Etwo$ on Hochschild cochains (Dunn additivity: $\Eone \otimes \Eone = \Etwo$). For $\Etwo$ input, it gives $E_3$, but this $E_3$ structure is the generic one from the higher Deligne conjecture, not the specific algebraic $E_3$ with BV operator.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}[Attribution]
Lurie, \emph{Higher Algebra} \cite{LurieHA}, Theorem~5.3.1.30. By the higher Deligne conjecture, $\HH^\bullet$ of an $E_n$-algebra carries $E_{n+1}$ structure. For $\Eone$ input: $\HH^\bullet$ is $\Etwo$ (Dunn: $\Eone \otimes \Eone = \Etwo$). For $\Etwo$ input: $\HH^\bullet$ is $E_3$. The specific algebraic $E_3$ in the proposition (cup $+$ Gerstenhaber $+$ BV) requires $E_\infty$ input because the BV operator needs the $S^1$-equivariant cyclic structure, which is available only for commutative algebras.
\end{proof}

\begin{conjecture}[Topological \texorpdfstring{$E_3$}{E-3} from 6d holomorphic theory and the comparison]
\label{conj:topological-e3-comparison}
Let $\cF$ be the factorization algebra of observables of the 6d holomorphic theory on $\C^3$ with gauge algebra $\frakg$ and Omega-background parameters $(h_1, h_2, h_3)$ satisfying $h_1 + h_2 + h_3 = 0$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
 \item \emph{Topological $E_3$ (configuration space).}
 The framed little $3$-disks operad $fE_3$ acts on $\Conf_n(\C^3)$ via the topological structure of $\R^6$ restricted to the holomorphic slice. The Omega-background deformation $(h_1, h_2, h_3)$ twists the framing by the equivariant parameters, producing a nontrivial $E_3$-chiral factorization on $\C^3$ (nontrivial braiding from the holomorphic configuration space, not from $\pi_1(\Conf_2(\R^6)) = 0$ which is trivial). At $\boldsymbol{h} = 0$, the $E_3$ reduces to $E_\infty$.

 \item \emph{Comparison with algebraic $E_3$.}
 Under Kontsevich formality ($\C_\bullet(E_n; \Q) \xrightarrow{\sim} H_\bullet(E_n; \Q)$), the topological $E_3$ from~(a) and the algebraic $E_3$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:algebraic-e3-deligne} agree rationally. Over $\Z$, they may differ by torsion. The holomorphic CS construction uses the topological $E_3$ from~(a); the algebraic $E_3$ provides the classical limit.
\end{enumerate}
Part~(a) is the holomorphic-BV input from Costello--Gwilliam--Li plus the equivariant refinement suggested by Costello's Omega-background programme; the Hall comparison is still conditional and is isolated in Problem~\ref{op:cy3-hcs-hall-comparison}. Part~(b) is the rational formality comparison and should not be read as an integral or compact-CY theorem.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[Why \texorpdfstring{$E_3$}{E-3} and not higher]
\label{rem:why-e3-not-higher}
For the 6d holomorphic theory on $\C^3$, the holomorphic $\En$ level is $E_3$ (three complex dimensions). One might ask whether additional structure from the 6d origin (topological $E_6$) survives. It does not, in the chiral setting: each complex direction contributes exactly one chiral level via the holomorphic constraint. The remaining $E_3$ worth of structure (the gap between holomorphic $E_3$ and topological $E_6$) is the antiholomorphic content, which is killed by the holomorphic twist. This is the higher-dimensional analogue of the statement that a factorization algebra on a Riemann surface is $\Etwo$ topologically but $\Eone$-chiral holomorphically (Proposition~\ref{prop:holomorphic-e1}).
\end{remark}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$E_3$}{E-3}-chiral Koszul duality}
\label{subsec:e3-koszul}

The $E_3$ structure on $\C^3$ induces an $E_3$-chiral Koszul duality that extends the $\Etwo$-chiral Koszul duality of Chapter~\ref{ch:e2-chiral} (Conjecture~\ref{conj:e2-koszul}).

\begin{conjecture}[\texorpdfstring{$E_3$}{E-3}-chiral Koszul duality from 6d theory]
\label{conj:e3-koszul-duality}
For the $E_3$-chiral factorization algebra $\cF$ on $\C^3$ arising from the 6d holomorphic theory:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item The bar complex $B_{E_3}(\cF)$ is a cofree conilpotent $E_3$-coalgebra on $\C^3$ with three commuting differentials $d_1, d_2, d_3$ from OPE residues in each complex direction, three commuting coproducts $\Delta_1, \Delta_2, \Delta_3$, and the $E_3$ braiding from the equivariant $R$-matrix.
 \item The Koszul dual $\cF^! = D_{\C^3}(B_{E_3}(\cF))$ carries $E_3$-chiral structure with inverted parameters $(h_1, h_2, h_3) \to (-h_1, -h_2, -h_3)$.
 \item On restriction to $\C^2 \subset \C^3$, the $E_3$ Koszul duality reduces to the $\Etwo$-chiral Koszul duality of Conjecture~\ref{conj:e2-koszul}. On further restriction to $C \subset \C^2$, it reduces to the $\Eone$-chiral Koszul duality of Vol~II.
 \item The modular characteristics satisfy $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cF|_C) + \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cF^!|_C) = \rho_K$ (the family-dependent Koszul conductor of Vol~I Theorem~C), consistent with the lower-dimensional Koszul duality statements.
\end{enumerate}
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[\texorpdfstring{$E_3$}{E-3} Koszul duality and quantum toroidal algebras]
\label{rem:e3-koszul-toroidal}
At the level of quantum toroidal algebras, the $E_3$ Koszul duality of Conjecture~\ref{conj:e3-koszul-duality}(ii) predicts the parameter-inversion symmetry
\[
 U_{q,t}(\widehat{\widehat{\fgl}}_1)^! \;\simeq\; U_{q^{-1}, t^{-1}}(\widehat{\widehat{\fgl}}_1),
\]
which is the conjectural Koszul duality of Chapter~\ref{ch:quantum-groups} (Definition~\ref{def:qgf-four-regimes}, toroidal regime). The $E_3$ framework provides the geometric origin of this parameter inversion: it is the Verdier duality on $\C^3$ factorization coalgebras.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[The Zamolodchikov tetrahedron equation fails for the Yang \texorpdfstring{$R$}{R}-matrix]
\label{thm:zte-failure}
Let $R(z) = (z \cdot \mathrm{Id} + \hbar_{\mathrm{Y}} \cdot P)/(z + \hbar_{\mathrm{Y}})$ be the Yang $R$-matrix on $\C^2 \otimes \C^2$ with $\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}} = h_1 h_2 h_3$, and define the $3$-particle $S$-operator
\[
 S_{ijk}(u_i, u_j, u_k) \;=\; R_{ij}(u_i - u_j) \, R_{ik}(u_i - u_k) \, R_{jk}(u_j - u_k)
\]
acting on $(\C^2)^{\otimes 3}$. Then:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item The Yang--Baxter equation $R_{12} R_{13} R_{23} = R_{23} R_{13} R_{12}$ is satisfied (positive control).
 \item The Zamolodchikov tetrahedron equation
 \[
 S_{012} \, S_{013} \, S_{023} \, S_{123}
 \;=\;
 S_{123} \, S_{023} \, S_{013} \, S_{012}
 \]
 on $(\C^2)^{\otimes 4}$ is \emph{not} satisfied. The obstruction scales as $O(\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}^2)$ and is antisymmetric.
 \item At $\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}} = 0$ (the permutation limit, $R(z) = P$), the ZTE is trivially satisfied (Kapranov--Voevodsky).
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Parts~(i) and~(iii) follow by direct multiplication of the Yang
$R$-matrix and its permutation limit. For part~(ii), explicit matrix
multiplication on the charge-$2$ sector of $(\C^2)^{\otimes 4}$
(dimension~$6$) gives a nonzero difference
$\mathrm{LHS}-\mathrm{RHS}$ at order $O(\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}^2)$; the
symbolic expansion in $\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}$ has a nonzero antisymmetric
leading term.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Consequence for \texorpdfstring{$E_3$}{E-3}-chiral structure]
\label{rem:zte-consequence}
Theorem~\ref{thm:zte-failure} proves that the $E_3$ coherence condition is \emph{genuinely nontrivial}: the na\"ive factorization $S_{ijk} = R_{ij} R_{ik} R_{jk}$ from pairwise Yang--Baxter solutions does \emph{not} produce a tetrahedron solution. The correct $3$-particle $S$-operator for $U_{q,t}(\widehat{\widehat{\fgl}}_1)$ must include corrections beyond the pairwise product, controlled by the $E_3$ structure of holomorphic Chern--Simons on $\C^3$. At the level of the $A_\infty$ bar complex, these corrections are the shadow tower contributions $\delta^{(k)}$ from the higher operations $m_k$ (Section~\ref{sec:e1-chiral-bialgebras}).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Structure of the ZTE obstruction]
\label{rem:zte-obstruction-structure}
The obstruction matrix $\cO = \mathrm{LHS} - \mathrm{RHS}$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:zte-failure}, restricted to the charge-$2$ sector $(\C^2)^{\otimes 4}|_{q=2}$ (dimension~$6$), has the following structure:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{Rank and symmetry.} $\mathrm{rk}(\cO) = 4$ (out of $6$). The obstruction is \emph{antisymmetric}: $\cO^T = -\cO$, reflecting the Yang $R$-matrix parity $R_{ij}(u)^T = R_{ji}(-u)$.
 \item \emph{Eigenvalue structure.} The eigenvalues of $\cO / \hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}^2$ are $\{0, 0, \pm i\sqrt{3/21}, \pm i\sqrt{19/21}\}$. The discriminants $3$ and $19$ are both prime, a potentially significant arithmetic feature.
 \item \emph{$S_4$-representation.} Under the $S_4$ permutation action on $(\C^2)^{\otimes 4}|_{q=2}$, the obstruction decomposes as: trivial (unobstructed) $\oplus$ standard ($3$-dim) $\oplus$ $2$-dimensional representation.
 \item \emph{Correction type.} A \emph{global} additive correction $S \mapsto S + \hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}^2 T$ on $V^{\otimes 4}$ alone \emph{cannot} resolve the ZTE. The correction must be at the \emph{per-face level}: each face $S$-operator $S_{ijk} \mapsto S_{ijk} + C_{ijk}$ receives its own ternary correction $C_{ijk} \in \End(V_i \otimes V_j \otimes V_k)$. By Proposition~\ref{prop:zte-explicit-correction}, the per-face corrections are \emph{completely factorizable} into pairwise $R$-matrix deformations $\delta_{ab}$, giving the equivalent description $R_{ab}(z) \mapsto R_{ab}(z) + \delta_{ab}(z)$ at the $R$-matrix level. The gauge freedom is $45$-dimensional ($1$ scalar $+$ $44$ face-redistribution).
\end{enumerate}
The rank-$4$ obstruction is the shadow of the $A_\infty$ correction $\delta^{(2)}$ from Proposition~\ref{prop:virasoro-d4}: both arise from the quartic bar differential. The ZTE obstruction lives in the $E_3$ bar complex; the $d_4$ differential on the spectral sequence is its algebraic incarnation.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Deformation cohomology of the ZTE correction]
\label{prop:zte-deformation-cohomology}
Let $R(z) = (z \cdot \mathrm{Id} + \hbar_{\mathrm{Y}} \cdot P)/(z + \hbar_{\mathrm{Y}})$ be the Yang $R$-matrix. Define the \emph{deformation complex} $(C^\bullet, d)$ by
\[
 C^0 = \End(V)^{\mathrm{cp}} \xrightarrow{\; d^0 \;} C^1 = \End(V^{\otimes 2})^{\mathrm{cp}} \xrightarrow{\; d^1 \;} C^2 = \End(V^{\otimes 3})^{\mathrm{cp}},
\]
where $(-)^{\mathrm{cp}}$ denotes the charge-preserving subspace, $d^0(A) = [A \otimes 1 + 1 \otimes A,\, R]$ (infinitesimal symmetry $\to$ trivial deformation), and $d^1(\delta)$ is the linearized Yang--Baxter equation applied to $\delta$ ($R$-matrix deformation $\to$ induced ternary operator). Then:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{Chain complex.} The composite $d^1 \circ d^0 = 0$, so $(C^\bullet, d)$ is a cochain complex. Dimensions: $\dim C^0 = 2$, $\dim C^1 = 6$, $\dim C^2 = 20$. Euler characteristic $\chi = 2 - 6 + 20 = 16$.

 \item \emph{Cohomology.} $\dim H^0 = 2$ (the $\fgl(1) \times \fgl(1)$ charge-sector scalings), $\dim H^1 = 1$ (one nontrivial YBE-preserving deformation direction), $\dim H^2 = 15$ (the ternary obstruction space).

 \item \emph{$L_\infty$ structure.} The Yang--Baxter equation is the degree-$1$ Maurer--Cartan equation $\ell_2(r,r) = 0$, and the Zamolodchikov tetrahedron equation is the degree-$2$ higher Maurer--Cartan equation $\ell_1(r^{(2)}) + \ell_3(r,r,r) = 0$. Since each face $S$-operator satisfies YBE, $\ell_2(r,r) = 0$ per face, and the entire ZTE obstruction is the ternary $L_\infty$ bracket:
 \[
 \cO_{\mathrm{ZTE}} \;=\; \ell_3(r,r,r).
 \]

 \item \emph{Pairwise $H^2$ is nontrivial.} The linearized ZTE restricted to pairwise $R$-matrix deformations (deforming $R_{ab} \mapsto R_{ab} + \varepsilon \, \delta_{ab}$ for each of the $6$ pairs) has rank~$30$ out of $36$ constraints on the charge-$2$ sector of $V^{\otimes 4}$. The cokernel $H^2_{\mathrm{pw}}$ has dimension~$6$: pairwise deformations alone are \emph{generically insufficient} to resolve the ZTE obstruction.

 \item \emph{Extended $H^2$ resolves the obstruction.} Enlarging the deformation complex to include ternary corrections $C_{ijk} \in \End(V^{\otimes 3})^{\mathrm{cp}}$ (one per face) gives the \emph{gauge-extended complex} with $36 + 80 = 116$ deformation parameters. The extended linearization has rank~$35$ out of~$36$: the obstruction class $[\ell_3(r,r,r)]$ is \emph{trivial} in $H^2_{\mathrm{ext}}$. Therefore the corrected $3$-particle $S$-operator
 \[
 S^{\mathrm{corr}}_{ijk} \;=\; S^{\mathrm{fact}}_{ijk} + \hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}^2 \, T_{ijk}
 \]
 \emph{exists}, where $T_{ijk}$ is a genuinely ternary (non-factorizable) operator.

 \item \emph{Stability.} The cohomology dimensions are independent of $\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}$ at generic values (upper-semicontinuity of the deformation family), confirming that the existence result is not an artifact of special parameter choices.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
\emph{Part~(i): chain complex.} The charge-preserving subspace $(-)^{\mathrm{cp}}$ is defined by the condition that operators preserve the total particle number $q = \sum_i n_i$ on the tensor product $V^{\otimes k}$, where $V = \mathrm{span}\{|0\rangle, |1\rangle\}$ is the $2$-dimensional Fock space. For $k$ particles, the charge-$q$ sector has dimension $\binom{k}{q}$.

The differential $d^0 \colon C^0 \to C^1$ sends an infinitesimal symmetry $A \in \End(V)^{\mathrm{cp}}$ to the induced trivial deformation $d^0(A) = [A \otimes 1 + 1 \otimes A,\, R]$. Since $R(z) = (z \cdot \mathrm{Id} + \hbar_{\mathrm{Y}} \cdot P)/(z + \hbar_{\mathrm{Y}})$ and $A$ is diagonal (charge-preserving on a single particle), $A$ commutes with both $\mathrm{Id}$ and the permutation~$P$, hence $d^0(A) = 0$ for all~$A$. Therefore $d^0 = 0$, with $\ker d^0 = C^0$ of dimension~$2$ (the $\fgl(1) \times \fgl(1)$ charge-sector scalings).

The differential $d^1 \colon C^1 \to C^2$ applies the linearized Yang--Baxter equation: for $\delta \in \End(V^{\otimes 2})^{\mathrm{cp}}$,
\[
 d^1(\delta) = [\delta_{12},\, R_{13} R_{23}] + [R_{12},\, \delta_{13} R_{23} + R_{13} \delta_{23}],
\]
i.e., the first-order variation of the YBE $R_{12}R_{13}R_{23} = R_{23}R_{13}R_{12}$ under $R \mapsto R + \varepsilon\,\delta$. The composite $d^1 \circ d^0 = 0$ follows from the Jacobi identity: $d^1(d^0(A))$ is the second-order variation of YBE under conjugation by $e^{\varepsilon A}$, which vanishes identically since YBE is preserved by symmetries.

Dimensions: $\dim C^0 = \dim \End(V)^{\mathrm{cp}} = 2$ (two diagonal entries); $\dim C^1 = \dim \End(V^{\otimes 2})^{\mathrm{cp}} = \binom{2}{0}^2 + \binom{2}{1}^2 + \binom{2}{2}^2 = 1 + 4 + 1 = 6$; $\dim C^2 = \dim \End(V^{\otimes 3})^{\mathrm{cp}} = \binom{3}{0}^2 + \binom{3}{1}^2 + \binom{3}{2}^2 + \binom{3}{3}^2 = 1 + 9 + 9 + 1 = 20$. Euler characteristic $\chi = 2 - 6 + 20 = 16$.

\smallskip\noindent\emph{Part~(ii): cohomology.} Since $d^0 = 0$: $H^0 = \ker d^0 = C^0$, so $\dim H^0 = 2$. The matrix of $d^1$ in the charge-sector block decomposition is block-diagonal: on the charge-$0$ and charge-$2$ sectors (each $1$-dimensional in $C^1$, mapping into $1$-dimensional targets in $C^2$), $d^1$ acts by a scalar proportional to $\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}$, hence has rank~$1$ on each sector. On the charge-$1$ sector ($4$-dimensional in $C^1$, mapping into $9$-dimensional target), the linearized YBE matrix has rank~$3$ (the $4$th direction is the trivial deformation $R \mapsto R + \varepsilon \cdot P$, which preserves YBE). Total: $\mathrm{rk}(d^1) = 1 + 3 + 1 = 5$; $\dim H^1 = 6 - 5 - 0 = 1$; $\dim H^2 = 20 - 5 = 15$.
