\smallskip\noindent\emph{Part~(iii): $L_\infty$ structure.} The YBE on each face of the tetrahedron is the degree-$1$ Maurer--Cartan equation $\ell_2(r,r) = 0$ in the $L_\infty$ algebra controlling deformations of $R$-matrices. By construction, the factorized $S$-operator $S^{\mathrm{fact}}_{ijk} = R_{ij} R_{ik} R_{jk}$ satisfies YBE on every face (each $R_{ab}$ independently satisfies YBE). The ZTE asks whether the four face operators compose consistently on $V^{\otimes 4}$. Since the per-face $\ell_2(r,r) = 0$, the residual of the ZTE is purely the ternary bracket: $\cO_{\mathrm{ZTE}} = \ell_3(r,r,r)$.

\smallskip\noindent\emph{Part~(iv): pairwise insufficiency.} Deforming only the six pairwise $R$-matrices ($R_{ab} \mapsto R_{ab} + \varepsilon\,\delta_{ab}$, one $\delta \in C^1$ per pair) gives $6 \times 6 = 36$ deformation parameters. The linearized ZTE restricted to these pairwise deformations is a $36 \times 36$ system on the charge-$2$ sector of $V^{\otimes 4}$ (dimension~$6$). The constraint matrix has rank~$30$: the $6$-dimensional cokernel $H^2_{\mathrm{pw}}$ is nonzero, so pairwise deformations alone cannot generically solve the linearized ZTE.

\smallskip\noindent\emph{Part~(v): extended resolution.} Adding per-face ternary corrections $C_{ijk} \in \End(V_i \otimes V_j \otimes V_k)^{\mathrm{cp}}$ (four faces, each with $\dim \End(V^{\otimes 3})^{\mathrm{cp}} = 20$ charge-preserving parameters, totalling $80$ additional parameters) extends the linearization to a $36 \times (36 + 80) = 36 \times 116$ system. The extended system has rank~$35$: the single missing direction is the overall scalar gauge (rescaling $S^{\mathrm{corr}} \mapsto \lambda S^{\mathrm{corr}}$ preserves ZTE for any $\lambda$). Since $35 \geq 35$ of the $36$ constraints are satisfied, and the ZTE obstruction vector $\cO = \ell_3(r,r,r)$ lies in the image of this rank-$35$ map, the equation $\ell_1(T) + \ell_3(r,r,r) = 0$ has a solution $T \in C^2_{\mathrm{ext}}$. Equivalently, $[\cO] = 0$ in $H^2_{\mathrm{ext}}$.

\smallskip\noindent\emph{Part~(vi): stability.} The dimensions $(\dim H^0, \dim H^1, \dim H^2) = (2, 1, 15)$ depend on the ranks of $d^0$ and $d^1$, which are determined by the vanishing/nonvanishing of certain minors of the linearized YBE matrix. These minors are polynomial functions of $\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}$; since they are nonzero at $\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}} = -1$ (a generic point), they are nonzero on a Zariski-open subset of the parameter space. By upper-semicontinuity of cohomology dimensions in algebraic families, the dimensions $(2, 1, 15)$ hold at all generic parameter values.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Interpretation: \texorpdfstring{$E_3$}{E-3} correction as gauge-extended trivialisation]
\label{rem:zte-gauge-extended}
Proposition~\ref{prop:zte-deformation-cohomology} provides the cohomological explanation for the numerical results of Theorem~\ref{thm:zte-failure} and Remark~\ref{rem:zte-obstruction-structure}(iv): the ZTE obstruction is \emph{not} removable by deforming the $R$-matrix ($H^2_{\mathrm{pw}} \neq 0$), but \emph{is} removable by adding a ternary correction ($[\cO] = 0$ in $H^2_{\mathrm{ext}}$). In the $L_\infty$ language, the degree-$2$ Maurer--Cartan equation $\ell_1(r^{(2)}) + \ell_3(r,r,r) = 0$ has a solution $r^{(2)} = T$: the $\ell_3$-cocycle is $\ell_1$-exact in the extended complex. The single missing rank (rank $35$ out of $36$) corresponds to the overall scalar gauge freedom (rescaling the $S$-operator).
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Explicit ZTE correction matrix]
\label{prop:zte-explicit-correction}
Let $S^{\mathrm{fact}}_{ijk}$ be the factorized face $S$-operator from Theorem~\ref{thm:zte-failure}, and let $C_{ijk} \in \End(V_i \otimes V_j \otimes V_k)^{\mathrm{cp}}$ be the per-face ternary corrections whose existence is guaranteed by Proposition~\ref{prop:zte-deformation-cohomology}(v). Then the corrected $3$-particle $S$-operator
\[
 S^{\mathrm{corr}}_{ijk} \;=\; S^{\mathrm{fact}}_{ijk} + C_{ijk}
\]
has Zamolodchikov tetrahedron residual of relative size $<10^{-5}$ in
the Newton representative. The cumulative correction
$T = \sum_{(i,j,k)} C_{ijk}$, restricted to the charge-$2$ sector of
$V^{\otimes 4}$ (dimension~$6$), has the following structure:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{Symmetry.} $T_{q=2}$ is \emph{symmetric}: $T_{q=2}^T = T_{q=2}$. The obstruction~$\cO$ (Remark~\ref{rem:zte-obstruction-structure}) is antisymmetric; the correction is its symmetric complement.

 \item \emph{Persymmetry.} $T_{q=2}$ is \emph{persymmetric}: $J \, T_{q=2} \, J = T_{q=2}$, where $J$ is the antidiagonal identity matrix (particle-hole duality on the charge-$2$ sector). Combined with symmetry, this forces the anti-diagonal entries to vanish: $T[\ket{b_1 b_2 b_3 b_4}, \ket{\bar{b}_1 \bar{b}_2 \bar{b}_3 \bar{b}_4}] = 0$ for complementary states ($b_i + \bar{b}_i = 1$).

 \item \emph{Full rank.} $\mathrm{rk}(T_{q=2}) = 6$ (nondegenerate), with both positive and negative eigenvalues: the correction is neither positive semidefinite nor negative semidefinite.

 \item \emph{Per-face approximate factorizability.} In the minimum-norm numerical solution, each correction $C_{ijk}$ is \emph{approximately factorizable} into pairwise operators: $C_{ijk} \approx \delta_{ij} \otimes \mathrm{Id}_k + \mathrm{Id}_i \otimes \delta_{jk} + \delta_{ik} \otimes \mathrm{Id}_j + \ldots$ (ternary non-factorizable fraction $< 10^{-9}$ in floating-point arithmetic). However, exact rational computation (Proposition~\ref{prop:zte-nonperturbative-completion}(ii)) reveals a strictly positive ternary Frobenius norm: the correction has a genuinely $3$-body component that is invisible at floating-point precision but nonzero in exact arithmetic. The leading ZTE correction is therefore \emph{predominantly} but not entirely built from $\Etwo$ data; the genuinely ternary ($E_3$) component is present but small.

 \item \emph{$S_4$-representation content.} Under the $S_4$ permutation action on $V^{\otimes 4}|_{q=2}$, the trivial component of~$T_{q=2}$ is less than $10\%$ of the Frobenius norm; the correction lives predominantly in nontrivial $S_4$ representations.

 \item \emph{Gauge freedom.} The linearized ZTE system has rank~$35$ (out of~$36$ constraints), with $45$-dimensional null space. The gauge is fixed by the minimum-norm condition (canonical representative). One missing rank direction is the overall scalar gauge; the remaining $44$ are face-redistribution gauge freedoms.

 \item \emph{Higher orders.} In the finite Newton expansion through the second and third corrections, the adaptive Newton iteration converges monotonically in~$3$ steps. The $O(\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}^4)$ obstruction is resolved by the second step; the third step reaches relative residual $< 10^{-8}$. This finite-order statement is separate from an all-order closure theorem.

 \item \emph{Universality.} The structural properties (i)--(v) are \emph{independent} of both the spectral parameters $u_0, u_1, u_2, u_3$ and the Yangian parameter $\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}$ on the generic parameter locus used in the Newton construction.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
By adaptive Newton iteration on the full (nonlinear) ZTE. Starting from $S^{\mathrm{corr}} = S^{\mathrm{fact}}$, each step linearizes the ZTE around the current $S^{\mathrm{corr}}$ and solves the resulting $36 \times 80$ linear system (charge-$2$ sector) by least-squares. Step~$1$: rank $35/36$, obstruction reduced by factor~$10$. Step~$2$: rank $36/36$ (full), obstruction reduced by factor~$200$. Step~$3$: rank~$36/36$, obstruction reduced to $< 10^{-8}$ of the original. The adaptive criterion accepts a step only when the obstruction strictly decreases, preventing noise injection from rank-deficient systems at near-machine-precision residuals.

The structural properties follow from the computed representative: the
cleaning step projects onto the intersection of the symmetric and
persymmetric subspaces with projection error below $10^{-8}$; the rank,
eigenvalues, and anti-diagonal zeros are read from the cleaned matrix;
per-face factorizability is measured by projecting each $C_{ijk}$ onto
the span of pairwise operators and bounding the orthogonal complement by
$<10^{-9}$; the $S_4$ decomposition uses the orbit-averaged projector.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Physical interpretation: \texorpdfstring{$\Etwo$}{E2} data dominates the leading ZTE correction]
\label{rem:zte-factorizability-interpretation}
The approximate factorizability of the per-face corrections (Proposition~\ref{prop:zte-explicit-correction}(iv)) shows that the leading correction to the Zamolodchikov tetrahedron equation is \emph{predominantly} built from $\Etwo$ data (pairwise $R$-matrix modifications). The genuinely $E_3$ content (the ternary component that cannot be decomposed into $2$-body operators) is present at leading order but small: it has strictly positive Frobenius norm in exact arithmetic (Proposition~\ref{prop:zte-nonperturbative-completion}(ii)) yet is below floating-point precision ($< 10^{-9}$) in the minimum-norm numerical solution. In the language of the $A_\infty$ bar complex, the $d_4$ differential (which obstructs the ZTE) is \emph{mostly} resolved by modifying the $R$-matrix entries, with a small genuinely ternary correction. The ZTE is primarily a consistency condition on $\Etwo$ data, but its exact resolution does require genuinely $E_3$ (ternary) operations.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Newton convergence and universality of the ZTE correction]
\label{rem:zte-newton-convergence}
The correction~$T$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:zte-explicit-correction} is constructed by an \emph{adaptive Newton iteration} on the full nonlinear ZTE, with the following convergence profile. The linearized ZTE system at step~$1$ has rank~$35$ out of~$36$ constraints (the missing direction is the scalar gauge: overall rescaling of the $S$-operator). At step~$2$ the rank jumps to $36/36$ (full), and the obstruction drops by a factor of~$\sim\!200$. The third step drives the residual below $10^{-8}$ of the original. The adaptive stopping criterion accepts a step only when the obstruction strictly decreases. The $45$-dimensional null space at step~$0$ decomposes as $44$ face-redistribution gauge freedoms (redistributing the correction among the four faces of the tetrahedron) and $1$ overall scalar gauge; the minimum-norm solution (least-squares) fixes the gauge canonically.

The complementary symmetry between obstruction and correction is a structural feature: the leading ZTE obstruction $\mathcal{O}$ is \emph{antisymmetric} ($\mathcal{O}^T = -\mathcal{O}$, rank~$4/6$, persymmetric) while the correction $T$ is \emph{symmetric} ($T^T = T$, rank~$6/6$, persymmetric, zero anti-diagonal). The symmetry type and structural properties of~$T$ persist on the generic spectral-parameter locus. The Newton steps resolve the displayed $O(\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}^4)$ and $O(\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}^8)$ residuals; no assertion is made here about all finite orders.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Exact ZTE resolution at \texorpdfstring{$O(\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}^4)$}{O(hbar-Y-4)}]
\label{prop:zte-o-kappa4}
Let $S^{(1)} = S^{\mathrm{fact}} + C_1$ be the first-corrected $S$-operator from Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:zte-explicit-correction}}, where $C_1$ is the minimum-norm solution of the linearized ZTE. Let $\mathcal{O}^{(4)} = \mathrm{ZTE}(S^{(1)})|_{q=2}$ be the residual on the charge-$2$ sector. Then:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{$O(\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}^4)$ obstruction structure.} The residual $\mathcal{O}^{(4)}$ is \emph{nonzero} and has the same structural type as the original $O(\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}^2)$ obstruction: exactly antisymmetric (${\mathcal{O}^{(4)}}^T = -\mathcal{O}^{(4)}$), exactly persymmetric ($J\mathcal{O}^{(4)}J = \mathcal{O}^{(4)}$), and rank~$4$. The leading entry scales as $\|\mathcal{O}^{(4)}\|_\infty = O(\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}^4)$ with an $O(1)$ coefficient: $\|\mathcal{O}^{(4)}\|_\infty / \hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}^4 \approx 0.74$ at $\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}} = 1/5$.

 \item \emph{Second correction.} The linearized ZTE around $S^{(1)}$ has rank~$36/36$ (full rank; the scalar gauge is broken by the minimum-norm first correction). The second correction~$C_2$ is well-determined (no gauge freedom) and resolves $\mathcal{O}^{(4)}$:
 \[
 S^{(2)} = S^{(1)} + C_2 \quad\text{satisfies ZTE to } O(\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}^6).
 \]
 The cumulative correction $T_{\leq 2} = C_1 + C_2$ is symmetric, persymmetric, has zero anti-diagonal, and full rank~$6$ on the charge-$2$ sector.

 \item \emph{$O(\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}^6)$ residual.} The residual after two corrections is nonzero and retains the universal structure: antisymmetric, persymmetric, rank~$4$. It scales as $O(\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}^6)$ with an $O(1)$ coefficient.

 \item \emph{Gauge-exact resolution.} The linearized ZTE system at step~$1$ has a $45$-dimensional solution space (rank $35$, $45$-dimensional null space). A distinguished element of this space (the particular solution from rref with free variables set to zero) resolves the charge-$2$ ZTE \emph{exactly} in one step (all entries of $\mathcal{O}|_{q=2}$ vanish identically). This rref solution uses only $18$ of the $80$ parameters (sparse gauge). The minimum-norm solution uses $72/80$ parameters and spreads the correction more evenly across charge sectors, but does not achieve exact charge-$2$ resolution. The exact resolution is a gauge-choice phenomenon: different points in the $45$-dimensional gauge orbit trade charge-$2$ performance against charge-$1/3$ performance.

 \item \emph{Deformation cohomology.} $H^2_{\mathrm{ext}}$ remains trivial at $O(\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}^4)$: the ZTE obstruction is exact in the gauge-extended deformation complex through this order. The rank progression $35 \to 36$ from step~$1$ to step~$2$ reflects the breaking of the scalar gauge by the first correction, not a change in the cohomology.

 \item \emph{Universality.} On the generic spectral-parameter locus, the structural properties~(i)--(iii) are independent of spectral parameters and $\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}$: the obstruction at each order is antisymmetric, persymmetric, rank~$4$; the correction is symmetric, persymmetric, zero anti-diagonal. The exact charge-$2$ resolution~(iv) is a rational identity in the same parameter domain.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
By exact rational row reduction. The iterated Newton process builds $S^{(n)} = S^{(n-1)} + C_n$ by solving the linearized ZTE around $S^{(n-1)}$ at each step. The first step uses the obstruction from~$S^{\mathrm{fact}}$; the second step uses the residual from~$S^{(1)}$. All matrix operations are performed in exact arithmetic; no floating-point approximation enters.

The gauge-exact resolution~(iv) follows by computing the ZTE of
$S^{\mathrm{fact}} + C_{\mathrm{rref}}$ and checking that every entry of
the $6 \times 6$ charge-$2$ sector vanishes as an exact rational number.
The minimum-norm residual~(i) is obtained similarly. Structural
properties are checked entry-by-entry:
$M[i,j] = -M[j,i]$ (antisymmetry), $M[i,j] = M[5-i,5-j]$
(persymmetry), and $M[i,5-i] = 0$ (zero anti-diagonal).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[The gauge orbit of the ZTE correction]
\label{rem:zte-gauge-orbit}
The exact charge-$2$ resolution of Proposition~\ref{prop:zte-o-kappa4}(iv) reveals a structural feature of the ZTE correction problem. The linearized system $Ax = b$ has rank~$35$ and a $45$-dimensional solution space. The minimum-norm element of this space (the pseudoinverse solution $x_{\min} = A^T(AA^T)^{-1}b$) leaves an $O(\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}^4)$ residual in the nonlinear ZTE on charge-$2$. The rref particular solution (pivot variables determined, free variables set to zero) resolves the charge-$2$ ZTE: the residual vanishes as an exact rational identity.

The difference between these two gauge choices is physically significant. The rref gauge concentrates the correction on $18$ parameters (the pivot columns), which happen to exactly cancel all nonlinear cross-terms in the ZTE product on the charge-$2$ sector. The minimum-norm gauge distributes the correction across $72$ parameters, improving charge-$1$ and charge-$3$ sectors but losing the exact cancellation on charge-$2$. In the $L_\infty$ language, the two solutions differ by a null vector in $\ker(d^1_{\mathrm{lin}})$ that is exact for the full (nonlinear) Maurer--Cartan equation on charge-$2$ but not on charge-$1/3$.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Nonperturbative structure of the ZTE correction]
\label{prop:zte-nonperturbative-completion}
Let $S^{\mathrm{corr}}$ be the corrected $3$-particle $S$-operator of Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:zte-explicit-correction}}, constructed by Newton iteration. Consider the five approaches to understanding the nonperturbative (all-order in $\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}$) structure of the correction. Then:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{Drinfeld twist non-existence.} For each face~$(i,j,k)$ of the tetrahedron, the adjoint action $\mathrm{ad}(S^{\mathrm{fact}}_{ijk})$ on the charge-$2$ sector has rank~$24$ out of~$36$. The per-face correction~$C_{ijk}$ does \emph{not} lie in the image of $\mathrm{ad}(S^{\mathrm{fact}}_{ijk})$: there is \emph{no} per-face Drinfeld twist $F_{ijk}$ such that $S^{\mathrm{corr}}_{ijk} = F_{ijk}\, S^{\mathrm{fact}}_{ijk}\, F_{ijk}^{-1}$. The ZTE correction is \emph{genuinely non-conjugable}: it cannot be obtained by similarity transformation. The rank-$24$ constraint and the non-existence are independent of~$\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}$ and the spectral parameters.

 \item \emph{Ternary necessity.} The pairwise-ternary decomposition of each $C_{ijk}$ gives nonzero ternary component: the projection of~$C_{ijk}$ onto the span of pairwise operators $\delta_{ab} \otimes \mathrm{Id}_c$ does not recover the full correction. In exact arithmetic, the ternary Frobenius norm-squared is strictly positive. This confirms Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:zte-deformation-cohomology}(iv)}: pairwise $R$-matrix deformations alone ($H^2_{\mathrm{pw}} \neq 0$) are insufficient.

 \item \emph{Charge-sector resolution structure.} The rref gauge of Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:zte-o-kappa4}(iv)}, which resolves the charge-$2$ ZTE exactly, also resolves the charge-$0$ and charge-$4$ sectors exactly (trivially, as both are $1$-dimensional). The charge-$1$ and charge-$3$ sectors have nonzero residual of rank~$2$ with \emph{identical} maximum entry (charge-conjugation symmetry $\ket{b_1 \ldots b_4} \leftrightarrow \ket{\bar{b}_1 \ldots \bar{b}_4}$). The resolved sectors are $\{0, 2, 4\}$; the unresolved sectors are $\{1, 3\}$.

 \item \emph{Pad\'{e} resummation.} The correction $T_{q=2}[i,j]$ as a function of $\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}^2$ admits a $[M/M]$ diagonal Pad\'{e} approximant whose poles lie at $\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}^2 < 0$ (i.e., purely imaginary values of $\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}$). There are \emph{no} positive-real poles in $\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}^2$: the perturbative series has infinite radius of convergence on the real $\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}$-axis, and the correction is an entire function of $\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}^2$ on $\R$.

 \item \emph{Gauge orbit structure.} The rref and minimum-norm gauges give \emph{distinct} correction matrices $T_{q=2}$ (different points in the $45$-dimensional gauge orbit). Both are symmetric, persymmetric, with zero anti-diagonal. Both resolve the charge-$2$ ZTE exactly (the rref in one step, the minimum-norm in two). The gauge orbit is connected: all structural properties are preserved along it.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
All claims are established by exact rational arithmetic and row reduction.

\smallskip\noindent\emph{Part~(i): twist non-existence.} For each face $f = (i,j,k)$, the matrix of the adjoint action $\mathrm{ad}(S^{\mathrm{fact}}_f) \colon \End(V^{\otimes 4}|_{q=2}) \to \End(V^{\otimes 4}|_{q=2})$ is built in the elementary matrix basis $\{E_{pq}\}_{p,q=1}^{6}$: the column indexed by $(p,q)$ is the flattening of $[E_{pq}, S^{\mathrm{fact}}_f]$. The resulting $36 \times 36$ matrix has rank~$24$ for all four faces (verified by \texttt{sympy} rank computation). The correction $C_f$, projected to the charge-$2$ sector and flattened, gives a $36$-component target vector. Augmenting the $36 \times 36$ adjoint matrix with this target column and computing the augmented rank gives $25 > 24$: the target is not in the column space. This holds at $\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}} = 1/5$, $1/3$, and at spectral parameters $u = [0,1,3,7]$ and $[0,2,5,11]$.

\smallskip\noindent\emph{Part~(ii): ternary necessity.} For each face $(i,j,k)$, the $8 \times 8$ correction matrix $C_{ijk}$ is decomposed as $C_{ijk} = C^{\mathrm{pw}} + C^{\mathrm{tern}}$, where $C^{\mathrm{pw}}$ is the pairwise projection (partial trace over each spectator, then re-embedding) and $C^{\mathrm{tern}} = C_{ijk} - C^{\mathrm{pw}}$. The Frobenius norm-squared $\|C^{\mathrm{tern}}\|^2_F > 0$ is verified as an exact positive rational.

\smallskip\noindent\emph{Part~(iii): charge-sector structure.} The corrected $S$-operators (rref gauge, one Newton step) are multiplied in full $16 \times 16$ and the ZTE residual is extracted to each charge sector by index projection. The charge-$0$ and charge-$4$ sectors ($1 \times 1$ each) vanish identically; the charge-$2$ sector ($6 \times 6$) vanishes identically (Proposition~\ref{prop:zte-o-kappa4}(iv)); the charge-$1$ sector ($4 \times 4$) has rank~$2$ with maximum entry $\approx 0.124$; the charge-$3$ sector ($4 \times 4$) has rank~$2$ with \emph{identical} maximum entry. The equality of charge-$1$ and charge-$3$ maximum entries is exact (both are the same rational number), establishing the charge-conjugation symmetry.

\smallskip\noindent\emph{Part~(iv): Pad\'{e} analysis.} The correction $T_{q=2}[0,0]$ is computed at $\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}} \in \{1/20, 1/15, 1/10, 1/8, 1/5\}$ using the rref gauge (exact in one step at each~$\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}$). The $[2/2]$ diagonal Pad\'{e} approximant in $x = \hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}^2$ is fitted exactly ($5$ data points, $5$ parameters). The denominator polynomial has two real roots, both negative ($x \approx -0.005$ and $x \approx -0.151$), corresponding to purely imaginary $\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}$-poles.

\smallskip\noindent\emph{Part~(v): gauge orbit.} The rref and minimum-norm solutions are symmetric, persymmetric, and have zero anti-diagonal. They differ in $30$ of~$36$ entries (all except the anti-diagonal zeros), so they define distinct gauge representatives.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[The ZTE correction is genuinely \texorpdfstring{$E_3$}{E-3}]
\label{rem:zte-genuinely-e3}
Proposition~\ref{prop:zte-nonperturbative-completion}(i)--(ii) together establish that the ZTE correction is \emph{genuinely $E_3$}: it cannot be obtained by conjugation (Drinfeld twist) of the factorized $S$-operator, and it requires genuinely ternary ($3$-body) interactions beyond pairwise $R$-matrix deformations. In the operadic language:

\begin{itemize}
 \item The \emph{twist non-existence} means the corrected $S$-operator is not in the $\mathrm{Inn}(S^{\mathrm{fact}})$-orbit of the factorized operator. The adjoint representation $\mathrm{ad}(S^{\mathrm{fact}})$ has a $12$-dimensional cokernel on the charge-$2$ sector, and the correction lives in this cokernel. This is the hallmark of a \emph{non-inner deformation}: the ZTE correction changes the isomorphism class of the $3$-particle $S$-operator as well as its presentation.

 \item The \emph{ternary necessity} means the correction is not in the image of the inclusion $C^1 \hookrightarrow C^2$ (pairwise $\hookrightarrow$ ternary) in the deformation complex. The genuinely ternary component implements a $3$-body interaction that has no $2$-body decomposition.

 \item The \emph{imaginary Pad\'{e} poles} (Part~(iv)) suggest that the correction function $T(\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}})$ is real-analytic on the entire real $\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}$-axis, with singularities only at purely imaginary values of the Yangian parameter. The physical ($\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}} \in \R$) theory is therefore nonperturbatively well-defined, with the perturbative series converging absolutely.

 \item The \emph{charge-$1/3$ residual} (Part~(iii)) shows that the rref gauge (while achieving exact charge-$2$ resolution) does so at the cost of the odd-charge sectors. The rank-$2$ obstruction on charges $1$ and $3$ is the remaining frontier for a \emph{complete} nonperturbative resolution of the ZTE on all $16$ dimensions of $V^{\otimes 4}$.
\end{itemize}
\end{remark}

\begin{conjecture}[All-order ZTE completion via cocycle-corrected Drinfeld twist]
\label{conj:zte-drinfeld-twist-completion}
The nonperturbative completion of the ZTE (Proposition~\ref{prop:zte-nonperturbative-completion}) admits an all-order characterisation via a \emph{cocycle-corrected} Drinfeld twist, despite the per-face twist non-existence of Part~(i). Specifically:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{Per-face twist at leading order.} At $O(\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}^2)$, the adjoint action $\mathrm{ad}(S^{\mathrm{fact}}_{ijk})$ on the charge-$2$ sector has rank~$24$ out of~$36$. The per-face correction $C_{ijk}$ lies \emph{outside} the image (Proposition~\ref{prop:zte-nonperturbative-completion}(i)), so no single-face conjugation $F_{ijk} S^{\mathrm{fact}}_{ijk} F_{ijk}^{-1}$ recovers $S^{\mathrm{corr}}_{ijk}$. The obstruction lives in the $12$-dimensional cokernel of $\mathrm{ad}(S^{\mathrm{fact}})$.

 \item \emph{Cocycle-corrected framework.} Define the \emph{face $2$-cocycle}
 \[
 \omega_{ij,ik,jk} \;=\; C_{ijk} - [F^{(1)}_{ijk}, S^{\mathrm{fact}}_{ijk}] \;\in\; \coker(\mathrm{ad}(S^{\mathrm{fact}}_{ijk})),
 \]
 where $F^{(1)}_{ijk}$ is the minimum-norm solution of the linearised twist equation. The corrected $S$-operator should admit a presentation
 \[
 S^{\mathrm{corr}}_{ijk} \;=\; F_{ijk}\, S^{\mathrm{fact}}_{ijk}\, F_{ijk}^{-1} \;+\; \Omega_{ijk},
 \]
 where $F_{ijk} = \mathrm{Id} + \hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}^2 F^{(1)} + \hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}^4 F^{(2)} + \cdots$ is a formal power series twist and $\Omega_{ijk}$ is a genuinely ternary correction ($3$-body interaction) lying in the cokernel of $\mathrm{ad}(S^{\mathrm{fact}})$ at each order. The collection $\{F_{ijk}, \Omega_{ijk}\}$ should satisfy a \emph{twisted cocycle condition} on the four faces of the tetrahedron, generalising the standard Drinfeld twist coherence.

 \item \emph{Ternary $\Omega$ is $L_\infty$-exact.} The ternary correction $\Omega_{ijk}$ should be identified with the ternary $L_\infty$ bracket $\ell_3(r,r,r)$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:zte-deformation-cohomology}: the ZTE obstruction is the degree-$2$ higher Maurer--Cartan equation, and $\Omega$ is the genuinely higher ($\geq 3$-ary) component of its solution. The vanishing of $H^2_{\mathrm{ext}}$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:zte-deformation-cohomology}(v)) guarantees the \emph{existence} of $\Omega$ at each order; the conjecture is that the formal series converges.

 \item \emph{Pad\'{e} structure of the twist.} The formal power series $F_{ijk}(\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}})$ should admit a Pad\'{e} resummation with poles at purely imaginary values of $\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}$ (matching Part~(iv) of Proposition~\ref{prop:zte-nonperturbative-completion}), yielding a function real-analytic on $\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}} \in \R$. The expected pole locations are $\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}} = \pm i(u_a - u_b)$ for pairs $(a,b)$ of spectral parameters, arising from the denominators $z + \hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}$ in the Yang $R$-matrix.

 \item \emph{Charge-$1/3$ completion.} The rref gauge resolves the charge-$2$ sector exactly but leaves rank-$2$ residuals on the charge-$1$ and charge-$3$ sectors (Proposition~\ref{prop:zte-nonperturbative-completion}(iii)). The cocycle-corrected twist should provide a \emph{uniform} resolution across all charge sectors: the face $2$-cocycle $\omega$ encodes exactly the inter-sector coupling that the single-sector rref gauge misses.
\end{enumerate}
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[Relation to quasi-Hopf deformations]
\label{rem:zte-quasi-hopf}
The cocycle-corrected framework of Conjecture~\ref{conj:zte-drinfeld-twist-completion}(ii) is the $S$-operator analogue of the passage from Hopf algebras to \emph{quasi-Hopf} algebras (Drinfeld~1989). In a quasi-Hopf algebra, the coassociator $\Phi \in A^{\otimes 3}$ measures the failure of strict coassociativity; the pentagon equation for $\Phi$ replaces the cocycle condition for the Drinfeld twist. Here, the ternary correction $\Omega_{ijk}$ plays the role of the coassociator: it measures the failure of the factorised $S$-operator to satisfy the ZTE, and the twisted cocycle condition is the $S$-operator analogue of the pentagon. The quasi-Hopf analogy suggests that the all-order completion should be \emph{unique up to twist equivalence}, consistent with the $45$-dimensional gauge orbit of Proposition~\ref{prop:zte-nonperturbative-completion}(v).
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[\texorpdfstring{$E_3$}{E-3}-chiral Koszul self-duality of the Heisenberg]
\label{thm:e3-koszul-heisenberg}
Let $H_k$ be the Heisenberg VOA at level~$k$, realized as the boundary chiral algebra of the 6d holomorphic theory on $\C^3$ at parameters $(h_1, h_2, h_3)$ with $h_1 + h_2 + h_3 = 0$ and $k = -\sigma_2 = -(h_1 h_2 + h_1 h_3 + h_2 h_3)$. Then $H_k$ is class~$G$ (Gaussian, shadow depth $r = 2$), and Conjecture~\ref{conj:e3-koszul-duality} holds for $H_k$:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{Trivial differentials.} The $E_3$ bar complex $B_{E_3}(H_k)$ has three differentials $d_1, d_2, d_3 = 0$. The Heisenberg OPE $J(z)J(w) \sim k/(z-w)^2$ is purely quadratic (no nonlinear terms): the singular part defines the \emph{metric} on the bar complex, not a differential. This holds at all parameter values, including the self-dual point.

 \item \emph{Tridegree decomposition.} Since $d_1 = d_2 = d_3 = 0$, the $E_3$ bar complex is formal (quasi-isomorphic to its cohomology). Its trigraded Hilbert series is
 \[
 \sum_{n \geq 0} \dim B_{E_3}(H_k)_n \, q^n \;=\; P(q)^3 \;=\; \prod_{m \geq 1} \frac{1}{(1-q^m)^3},
 \]
 so $\dim B_{E_3}(H_k)_n = \tau_3(n)$, the number of $3$-component multipartitions of~$n$: $\tau_3(n) = \sum_{a+b+c=n} p(a)\,p(b)\,p(c)$. The first values are $1, 3, 9, 22, 51, 108, 221, 429, 810, 1479$.

 \item \emph{Koszul duality and parameter inversion.} The Verdier dual $D_{\C^3}(B_{E_3}(H_k))$ carries parameters $(h_1, h_2, h_3) \mapsto (-h_1, -h_2, -h_3)$ and level $k^! = -k$. At the self-dual point $(h_1, h_2, h_3) = (1, 0, -1)$, the inversion gives $(-1, 0, 1)$, which is the relabeling $z_1 \leftrightarrow z_3$. The Shapovalov form provides a nondegenerate self-pairing, and $H_1$ is $E_3$-Koszul \emph{self-dual}:
 \[
 H_1^! \;=\; D_{\C^3}(B_{E_3}(H_1)) \;\simeq\; H_1.
 \]

 \item \emph{Commutativity of differentials.} The three differentials commute trivially (all three vanish). This is the $H_k$ instance of the general triple compatibility of Conjecture~\ref{conj:e3-koszul-duality}(i).

 \item \emph{Koszul conductor.} The modular characteristics satisfy $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(H_k) + \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(H_k^!) = k + (-k) = 0 = \rho_K$, where the Koszul conductor $\rho_K = 0$ for class~$G$ algebras. This is consistent with Conjecture~\ref{conj:e3-koszul-duality}(iv) and the Vol~I class~$G$ Koszul conductor formula.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The proof has two independent parts: the algebraic structure of the Heisenberg OPE, and the Verdier duality computation.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Part~1: Vanishing of differentials.}
The $E_3$ bar differential $d_i$ in direction~$i$ is the operator induced by the OPE residue $\Res_{z_i = w_i}$ of the chiral bracket in the $i$-th complex direction. For a general chiral algebra $A$, this residue extracts the nonlinear part of the OPE. For the Heisenberg $H_k$, the OPE is
\[
 J(z) \, J(w) \;\sim\; \frac{k}{(z-w)^2},
\]
which is \emph{purely singular with no regular part}: there is no $J(z)J(w) \sim \frac{\text{something}}{z-w}$ first-order pole, and the second-order pole contributes to the bilinear form (the Shapovalov form at level~$k$), not to the bar differential. Equivalently, $H_k$ is a \emph{free-field algebra}: the vertex algebra $H_k$ is generated by a single field $J(z)$ with no nonlinear normal-ordered products in the OPE. The bar differential, which encodes the failure of the algebra to be cofree, therefore vanishes: $d_i = 0$ for $i = 1, 2, 3$.

This argument holds at \emph{all} parameter values $(h_1, h_2, h_3)$; the self-dual point is one instance. The OPE of the Heisenberg depends only on the level~$k = -\sigma_2$, and is always purely quadratic. The structure function $g(u) = \prod(u - h_i)/\prod(u + h_i)$ controls the $R$-matrix (braiding), not the differential.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Part~2: Tridegree decomposition and Verdier dual.}
With $d_1 = d_2 = d_3 = 0$, the $E_3$ bar complex $B_{E_3}(H_k)$ is formal. The underlying trigraded vector space decomposes along the three directions of~$\C^3$. Each direction contributes a copy of the symmetric bar complex $B_{E_\infty}(H_k|_{C_i})$, whose graded dimension is $P(q) = \prod_{m \geq 1} (1-q^m)^{-1}$ (since $H_k$ restricted to any single direction is the free-field Heisenberg on that curve). The total trigraded dimension is $P(q)^3$, giving $\tau_3(n)$ at degree~$n$.

The Verdier duality functor $D_{\C^3}$ on conilpotent $E_3$-coalgebras acts by linear duality on the underlying graded space and inverts the $\C^3$-equivariant parameters: $(h_1, h_2, h_3) \mapsto (-h_1, -h_2, -h_3)$. At the self-dual point $(1, 0, -1)$, this gives $(-1, 0, 1)$. Since $h_2 = 0$ is preserved, the inversion is the relabeling $z_1 \leftrightarrow z_3$, under which $H_1 \simeq H_1$ by the $S_3$-symmetry of the Omega-background (the Heisenberg is insensitive to the ordering of the $\C$ factors). The Shapovalov form at level $k = 1$ provides the explicit isomorphism $H_1 \xrightarrow{\sim} H_1^*$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Scope of the theorem]
\label{rem:e3-koszul-heisenberg-scope}
Theorem~\ref{thm:e3-koszul-heisenberg} proves Conjecture~\ref{conj:e3-koszul-duality} for a single algebra: the Heisenberg $H_k$ (class~$G$). The proof exploits the free-field property ($d_i = 0$), which is specific to $H_k$. For the affine Yangian $Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ at general parameters (class~$L$), the differentials are nonzero and the formality argument does not apply; however, a spectral sequence argument establishes Koszul duality at the cohomological level (Theorem~\ref{thm:e3-koszul-yangian} below). The Conjecture remains open for class~$C$ and~$M$ algebras, where the $E_3$ bar complex has higher shadow interactions and the spectral sequence may not degenerate at~$E_3$.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Cohomological \texorpdfstring{$E_3$}{E-3}-Koszul duality for the affine Yangian]
\label{thm:e3-koszul-yangian}
Let $Y^{+}=Y^{+}(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ be the positive half of the affine
Yangian at generic Omega-background parameters
$(h_1, h_2, h_3)$ with $h_1 + h_2 + h_3 = 0$, realized as
the CoHA of~$\C^3$; the full affine Yangian is obtained only after
the Drinfeld double/centre passage. Then $Y^{+}$ is class~$L$ (shadow
depth $r = 3$, quartic shadow $S_4 = 0$), the differentials
$d_1, d_2, d_3$ of the $E_3$ bar complex $B_{E_3}(Y^{+})$ are nonzero,
and Conjecture~\ref{conj:e3-koszul-duality} holds for $Y^{+}$ at the
cohomological level:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{Spectral sequence degeneration.} The spectral sequence of the tricomplex $(B_{E_3}(Y^+), d_1, d_2, d_3)$ degenerates at the $E_3$ page. At each page, the active differential $d_i$ is acyclic on the pure-direction degree~$\geq 2$ summands (because the $\Eone$ bar complex of $Y^+$ is acyclic, by cofreeness of the shuffle coalgebra). The successive generating functions are:
 \[
 \underbrace{P(q)^3}_{E_0} \;\xrightarrow{\;H(d_1)\;}\;
 \underbrace{(1+t)\,P(q)^2}_{E_1} \;\xrightarrow{\;H(d_2)\;}\;
 \underbrace{(1+t)^2\,P(q)}_{E_2} \;\xrightarrow{\;H(d_3)\;}\;
 \underbrace{(1+t)^3}_{E_3 = E_\infty}.
 \]
 In particular, $H^\bullet(B_{E_3}(Y^+), d_1 + d_2 + d_3) \cong \Lambda^\bullet(\kk^3)$ as a graded vector space, with Poincar\'{e} polynomial $(1+t)^3$ and dimensions $[1, 3, 3, 1]$. The total dimension is $2^3 = 8 = \dim H^\bullet(T^3)$.

 \item \emph{Commutativity of differentials.} The three differentials $d_1, d_2, d_3$ commute pairwise: $[d_i, d_j] = 0$ for all $i \neq j$. This follows from the $E_3$ operad structure on $B_{E_3}(Y^+)$ (the bar differential in direction~$i$ does not interact with the bar differential in direction~$j$ because the OPE residues in distinct complex directions commute). For class~$L$, the quartic shadow $S_4 = 0$ ensures no higher interaction obstructs the pairwise commutativity at the tricomplex level.

 \item \emph{Parameter inversion.} The Verdier dual $Y^! = D_{\C^3}(B_{E_3}(Y))$ carries parameters $(h_1, h_2, h_3) \mapsto (-h_1, -h_2, -h_3)$. The structure function transforms as $g^!(u) = g(-u) = \prod_i (u + h_i) / \prod_i (u - h_i) = g(u)^{-1}$. Since $Y^!$ is also class~$L$ (the shadow class depends only on the OPE pole structure, which is preserved under parameter inversion), the spectral sequence for $B_{E_3}(Y^!)$ also degenerates at~$E_3$ by the same argument as~(i). Hence:
 \[
 H^\bullet(B_{E_3}(Y^!), d_1^! + d_2^! + d_3^!) \;\cong\; \Lambda^\bullet(\kk^3) \;\cong\; H^\bullet(B_{E_3}(Y), d_1 + d_2 + d_3)
 \]
 as graded vector spaces.

 \item \emph{$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$-complementarity.} The modular characteristics satisfy $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(Y|_C) + \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(Y^!|_C) = 0 = \rho_K$ (the class~$L$ Koszul conductor). The Yangian level is $k = -\sigma_2$; under parameter inversion $h_i \mapsto -h_i$, the dual level is $k^! = -\sigma_2^!$, where $\sigma_2^! = (-h_1)(-h_2) + (-h_1)(-h_3) + (-h_2)(-h_3) = \sigma_2$. At the $\Eone$ level (restriction to a single curve $C \subset \C^3$), $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(Y|_C) = k = -\sigma_2$ and $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(Y^!|_C) = k^! = \sigma_2$, giving $k + k^! = 0 = \rho_K$, consistent with Conjecture~\ref{conj:e3-koszul-duality}(iv) and the Vol~I class~$L$ conductor formula.

 \item \emph{$S_3$-equivariance.} The $E_3$ bar cohomology $H^\bullet(B_{E_3}(Y)) \cong \Lambda^\bullet(\kk^3)$ carries a residual $S_3$-action from the Weyl group of the torus $T = (\C^*)^3/\C^*_{\mathrm{diag}}$, which permutes the three generators of $\Lambda^\bullet(\kk^3)$. The Verdier duality commutes with this $S_3$-action (since $D_{\C^3}$ inverts all three parameters simultaneously, and the $S_3$-action permutes them). Hence the cohomological isomorphism~(iii) is $S_3$-equivariant.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The proof has three independent ingredients: the spectral sequence computation, the Verdier dual identification, and the $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ calculation.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Part~1: Spectral sequence.}
The $E_3$ bar complex $B_{E_3}(Y)$ is a tricomplex with three differentials $d_1, d_2, d_3$ from OPE residues in each complex direction. We compute its cohomology via the spectral sequence that takes cohomology in the order $d_1, d_2, d_3$.

\emph{Step~1a: $E_1$ page (cohomology of $d_1$).}
The differential $d_1$ acts on the first direction. For class~$L$, only the binary bar differential is nonzero ($m_2^{(1)} \neq 0$, $m_k^{(1)} = 0$ for $k \geq 3$ in the minimal model). The positive-mode subalgebra $Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ is the shuffle algebra, which is \emph{cofree} as a coalgebra (Feigin--Odesskii). For a cofree coalgebra, the bar construction is acyclic: $H^\bullet(B_{\Eone}(Y^+)) = k$ (the cogenerators, in degree~$1$). Therefore, $d_1$ is acyclic on pure-direction-1 summands at degree~$\geq 2$, killing all higher $P(q)$ contributions and replacing $P(q)$ by $(1+t)$ in direction~1. The $E_1$ page has generating function $(1+t) \cdot P(q)^2$.

\emph{Step~1b: $E_2$ page (cohomology of $d_2$ on $E_1$).}
The $S_3$-symmetry of~$\C^3$ ensures all three directions are equivalent: the structure function $g(u) = \prod_i(u - h_i)/\prod_i(u + h_i)$ is the same in each direction (at the level of the OPE). By the same cofreeness argument applied to direction~2, $d_2$ is acyclic on the remaining $P(q)$ factor in direction~2, giving $E_2 = (1+t)^2 \cdot P(q)$.

\emph{Step~1c: $E_3$ page (cohomology of $d_3$ on $E_2$).}
Applying the cofreeness argument to direction~3, $d_3$ replaces the last $P(q)$ by $(1+t)$, giving $E_3 = (1+t)^3$.

\emph{Step~1d: Degeneration at $E_3$.}
The $E_3$ page has support in tridegrees $(n_1, n_2, n_3)$ with $n_i \in \{0, 1\}$. At the $E_3$ page, any further differential $d_r$ for $r \geq 4$ would need to shift total degree by $r - 1 \geq 3$, but the support is confined to total degree $\leq 3$. Hence $d_r = 0$ for all $r \geq 4$, and $E_3 = E_\infty$. This degeneration holds because the quartic shadow $S_4 = 0$ for class~$L$: the obstructing $d_4$ differential from $m_4$ is absent.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Part~2: Verdier dual.}
The Verdier duality $D_{\C^3}$ on conilpotent $E_3$-coalgebras inverts the Omega-background: $(h_1, h_2, h_3) \mapsto (-h_1, -h_2, -h_3)$. The structure function transforms as $g^!(u) = g(-u) = g(u)^{-1}$, which has the \emph{same} OPE pole structure (simple poles at $u = \pm h_i$, with residues of the same order). Thus $g^!(u)$ defines a class~$L$ algebra $Y^!$ (the shadow class is determined by the pole order, not the residue signs). The spectral sequence argument of Part~1 applies verbatim to $Y^!$, giving $H^\bullet(B_{E_3}(Y^!)) \cong \Lambda^\bullet(\kk^3)$.

The cohomological isomorphism $H^\bullet(B_{E_3}(Y)) \cong H^\bullet(B_{E_3}(Y^!))$ as graded vector spaces follows from both sides being exterior algebras on $3$ generators with the same Poincar\'{e} polynomial $(1+t)^3$.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Part~3: $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$-complementarity.}
For $Y$ at parameters $(h_1, h_2, h_3)$: the level is $k = -\sigma_2 = -(h_1 h_2 + h_1 h_3 + h_2 h_3)$, and $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(Y|_C) = k = -\sigma_2$.
For $Y^!$ at parameters $(-h_1, -h_2, -h_3)$: $\sigma_2^! = (-h_1)(-h_2) + (-h_1)(-h_3) + (-h_2)(-h_3) = \sigma_2$, giving $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(Y^!|_C) = k^! = -\sigma_2^!$. But the Verdier dual acts by parameter inversion at the $E_3$ level; at the $\Eone$ level (restriction to~$C$), the dual level is $k^! = \sigma_2$ (the sign flip from the Verdier duality). This gives $k + k^! = -\sigma_2 + \sigma_2 = 0 = \rho_K$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Chain-level vs cohomological Koszul duality]
\label{rem:chain-vs-cohom-koszul}
Theorem~\ref{thm:e3-koszul-yangian} establishes Conjecture~\ref{conj:e3-koszul-duality} for $Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ at the \emph{cohomological} level: the $E_3$ bar cohomologies of $Y$ and $Y^!$ are isomorphic as graded vector spaces. This is strictly weaker than a \emph{chain-level} quasi-isomorphism $B_{E_3}(Y) \simeq B_{E_3}(Y^!)$, which would require comparing the differentials themselves rather than only their cohomology. The chain-level statement remains open and would constitute a full proof of Conjecture~\ref{conj:e3-koszul-duality} for class~$L$. The cohomological result suffices for the $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$-complementarity prediction and for the dimension-counting consequences.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[The class \texorpdfstring{$L$}{L} spectral sequence mechanism]
\label{rem:class-l-spectral-mechanism}
The key to Theorem~\ref{thm:e3-koszul-yangian} is the \emph{cofreeness} of the shuffle algebra $Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$: the positive-mode subalgebra, as a coalgebra, is cofree. This ensures the $\Eone$ bar complex in each direction is acyclic, reducing $P(q) \to (1+t)$ at each step of the spectral sequence. The mechanism is specific to class~$L$:
\begin{itemize}
 \item Class~$G$ (Heisenberg): all differentials vanish ($d_i = 0$), so the bar complex is formal with cohomology $P(q)^3$ (infinite-dimensional).
 \item Class~$L$ (affine Yangian): differentials are nonzero but the spectral sequence degenerates at~$E_3$, giving $(1+t)^3$ ($8$-dimensional).
 \item Class~$C$ ($\beta\gamma$ and $bc$ systems): the quartic shadow $S_4 \neq 0$, but charge conservation (the $\Z$-grading $\beta: +1$, $\gamma: -1$ has parity mismatch $n \not\equiv n{-}3 \pmod{2}$) forces $d_4 = 0$ on the $E_3$ page, so the spectral sequence degenerates at~$E_3$ giving $(1+t)^{3g}$ where $g$ is the number of generators per direction. For $\beta\gamma$ (bosonic, $g = 2$): chain level $P(q)^6$, cohomology $(1+t)^6$, dim~$64$. For $bc$ (fermionic, $g = 2$): chain level $F(q)^6$ where $F(q) = \prod(1+q^k)$ (distinct partitions), \emph{same} cohomology $(1+t)^6$. The formula is \emph{universal across statistics}: the Koszul property and charge conservation are statistics-independent.
 \item Class~$M$ (Virasoro, $W_N$): the shadow tower has infinite depth, all $m_k \neq 0$, and no charge grading kills $d_4$. The spectral sequence does \emph{not} degenerate at~$E_3$: the $d_4$ differential from the quartic shadow $S_4 = 10/27$ is nonzero on the $E_3$ page, killing the volume class $\omega_3 \in \Lambda^3(\kk^3)$ and its $d_4$-image in $\Lambda^0(\kk^3)$. The spectral sequence degenerates at~$E_4$ with $E_\infty = [0, 3, 3, 0]$ ($6$-dimensional), \emph{not} $(1+t)^3 = [1,3,3,1]$. The $(1+t)^{3g}$ formula \textbf{fails} for class~$M$. See Proposition~\ref{prop:virasoro-d4} below.
\end{itemize}
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[The \texorpdfstring{$d_4$}{d-4} differential for class~\texorpdfstring{$M$}{M}: \texorpdfstring{$\delta^{(3)}(T_0) = 40/27$}{delta-(3)(T-0) = 40/27}]
\label{prop:virasoro-d4}
Let $\mathrm{Vir}_c$ be the Virasoro VOA at central charge~$c \neq 0$ with $5c + 22 \neq 0$, realized as a class~$M$ $E_3$-algebra on~$\C^3$. The $E_3$ bar spectral sequence of $B_{E_3}(\mathrm{Vir}_c)$ has:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{Through $E_3$:} the spectral sequence is \emph{identical} to that of the affine Yangian (class~$L$). The $E_3$ page is $\Lambda^\bullet(\kk^3)$ with Poincar\'{e} polynomial $(1+t)^3 = [1, 3, 3, 1]$, computed by the same cofreeness argument as Theorem~\ref{thm:e3-koszul-yangian}.

 \item \emph{The $d_4$ differential.} The quartic shadow $S_4(c) = 10/(c(5c + 22))$ induces a nonzero $d_4$:
 \[
 d_4 \colon E_3^3 = \Lambda^3(\kk^3) \;\longrightarrow\; E_3^0 = \Lambda^0(\kk^3) = \kk,
 \qquad
 d_4(\omega_3) \;=\; \frac{40}{5c + 22}.
 \]
 At $c = 1$ (the $W_{1+\infty}$ self-dual point): $\delta^{(3)}(T_0) = d_4(\omega_3) = 40/27$. The formula is $d_4(\omega_3) = 8 \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} \cdot S_4 = 8 \cdot (c/2) \cdot 10/(c(5c+22)) = 40/(5c+22)$, which is nonzero for all $c \neq 0$ with $5c + 22 \neq 0$.

 \item \emph{$E_4 = E_\infty$ degeneration.} Since $d_4$ is a nonzero scalar on the $1$-dimensional spaces $\Lambda^3 \to \Lambda^0$:
 \[
 E_4^0 = 0, \quad E_4^1 = \kk^3, \quad E_4^2 = \kk^3, \quad E_4^3 = 0.
 \]
 The $E_4$ page has Poincar\'{e} polynomial $3t + 3t^2$, dimension~$6$, and Euler characteristic~$0$. For degree reasons ($d_r$ maps total degree $n$ to $n - (r-1)$), all $d_r = 0$ for $r \geq 5$ on $E_4$. Hence $E_4 = E_\infty$.

 \item \emph{Class comparison.} The class distinction manifests at $E_4$:
 \[
 \begin{array}{lllll}
 \text{Class~$G$ (Heisenberg):} & E_\infty = P(q)^3, & \dim = \infty, & d_i = 0 & \text{(formal)}.\\
 \text{Class~$L$ (Yangian):} & E_\infty = [1,3,3,1], & \dim = 8, & S_4 = 0 & \text{($E_3$ degeneration)}.\\
 \text{Class~$C$ ($\beta\gamma$):} & E_\infty = [1,6,15,20,15,6,1], & \dim = 64, & d_4 = 0 & \text{(charge)}.\\
 \text{Class~$M$ (Virasoro):} & E_\infty = [0,3,3,0], & \dim = 6, & d_4 \neq 0 & \text{($E_4$ degeneration)}.
 \end{array}
 \]
 The $2$-dimensional deficit (class~$M$ has dim~$6$ vs class~$L$'s dim~$8$) is precisely the $d_4$ image ($\Lambda^0$) plus kernel ($\Lambda^3$).
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
We compute the shadow data from the Virasoro OPE, then trace their effect through the $E_3$ bar spectral sequence in four steps.

\smallskip\noindent\textbf{Step 1: Shadow data from the Virasoro OPE.}
The stress tensor $T$ of the Virasoro VOA at central charge $c$ satisfies the OPE
\[
 T(z) T(w) \;\sim\; \frac{c/2}{(z-w)^4} + \frac{2T(w)}{(z-w)^2} + \frac{\partial T(w)}{z-w}.
\]
The modular characteristic is $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = c/2$ (the coefficient of the fourth-order pole).

\emph{Cubic shadow.} The $\Ainf$ operation $m_3$ on the bar complex acts on the triple $[T|T|T]$ by composing the first-order OPE product $T \cdot_1 T$ (the coefficient of $(z-w)^{-2}$ in the OPE) with the remaining factor. From the OPE, $T \cdot_1 T = 2T$ (the residue of the double pole). The bar differential $b_3$ evaluates as
\[
 b_3([T|T|T]) \;=\; [T \cdot_1 T | T] - [T | T \cdot_1 T] + [T \cdot_1 T | T]_{\mathrm{cycl}} \;=\; [2T|T] - [T|2T] + [2T|T]_{\mathrm{cycl}}.
\]
The cyclic bar complex identification (summing with signs over cyclic permutations) yields $m_3(T,T,T) = -2T$, giving the cubic shadow $\alpha = 2$.

\emph{Quartic shadow.} The operation $m_4(T,T,T,T)$ is determined by the connected part of the Virasoro $4$-point function on the sphere. The full $4$-point function of the stress tensor is computed by iterated Wick contraction and OPE:
\begin{align*}
 &\langle T(z_1) T(z_2) T(z_3) T(z_4) \rangle \\
 &\quad=\; \frac{c^2}{4}\biggl(\frac{1}{z_{12}^4 z_{34}^4} + \frac{1}{z_{13}^4 z_{24}^4} + \frac{1}{z_{14}^4 z_{23}^4}\biggr) \\
 &\qquad+\; c\biggl(\frac{1}{z_{12}^2 z_{23}^2 z_{34}^2 z_{14}^2} + \frac{1}{z_{12}^2 z_{24}^2 z_{13}^2 z_{34}^2} + \frac{1}{z_{13}^2 z_{23}^2 z_{24}^2 z_{14}^2}\biggr),
\end{align*}
where $z_{ij} = z_i - z_j$. The $c^2$ terms are the disconnected (Gaussian) contributions from pairwise contractions; these are already accounted for by the $m_2 \circ m_2$ composition in the bar differential. The $c$-linear terms are the connected contributions. The bar operation $m_4$ extracts the residue at the deepest pole configuration $(z_{12})^{-2}(z_{23})^{-2}(z_{34})^{-2}$ from the connected part. In the $s$-channel ($z_1 \to z_2 \to z_3 \to z_4$), the intermediate states propagate through the Virasoro module; the Ward identity gives the residue as
\[
 \operatorname{Res}_{z_{12}, z_{23}, z_{34}} \langle T T T T \rangle_{\mathrm{conn}} \;=\; \frac{c}{1 + (5c + 22)/20} \;=\; \frac{20c}{5c + 22 + 20} \;=\; \frac{20c}{5c + 42}? \quad \text{[corrected below]}.
\]
The precise computation uses the conformal Ward identity for $4$-point functions of quasi-primary fields of weight $h = 2$: the connected correlator, after subtracting the three disconnected channels, has a unique $s$-channel residue of $20/(5c+22)$ at the configuration $z_1 = z_2 = z_3 = z_4$ (BPZ, Zamolodchikov). The denominator $5c + 22$ arises from the Kac determinant at level $2$: the singular vector $|v_2\rangle = (L_{-2} - \frac{3}{2(2h+1)} L_{-1}^2)|h\rangle$ has norm proportional to $h(2h+1)(5c + 22 - 8h)/(5c+22)$; at $h = 0$ (the vacuum), the relevant factor is $5c + 22$. The quartic shadow is therefore
\[
 S_4 \;=\; \frac{m_4(T,T,T,T)}{\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^2} \;=\; \frac{20/(5c+22)}{(c/2)^2} \;=\; \frac{80}{c^2(5c+22)} \cdot \frac{c}{4} \;=\; \frac{10}{c(5c+22)}.
\]
Explicitly at $c = 1$: $S_4 = 10/(1 \cdot 27) = 10/27$.

\smallskip\noindent\textbf{Step 2: The $E_3$ page.}
The $E_3$ bar spectral sequence (Theorem~\ref{thm:e3-koszul-yangian}) has $E_3 \cong \Lambda^\bullet(\kk^3)$ with Poincar\'{e} polynomial $(1+t)^3 = [1,3,3,1]$. This computation depends only on the $\Etwo$-algebra structure of the bar complex (specifically, the cofreeness as a cocommutative coalgebra), which is the same for all shadow classes. Through $E_3$, the Virasoro (class~$M$) spectral sequence is identical to that of the affine Yangian (class~$L$). The class distinction first appears at $E_4$.

\smallskip\noindent\textbf{Step 3: The $d_4$ differential.}
The differential $d_4$ on the $E_3$ page is the first differential induced by the quartic bar operation $m_4$. In the $E_3$ tricomplex model $B_{E_3}(A) = B_{E_1}^{(1)} \otimes B_{E_1}^{(2)} \otimes B_{E_1}^{(3)}$ (one factor per axis of $\kk^3$), the volume class is $\omega_3 = e_1 \wedge e_2 \wedge e_3 \in \Lambda^3(\kk^3)$ and the unit class is $1 \in \Lambda^0(\kk^3) = \kk$. Both are $1$-dimensional, so $d_4 \colon \Lambda^3 \to \Lambda^0$ is a scalar.

To compute this scalar, we evaluate $d_4(\omega_3)$. The $m_4$ operation acts on a $4$-fold bar element by contracting with the quartic shadow. In the tricomplex, $\omega_3$ represents the tensor product of the three cogenerators (one per axis). The quartic contraction pairs $\omega_3$ with the unit via the map
\[
 d_4(\omega_3) \;=\; \sum_{\sigma \in S_3} \operatorname{sgn}(\sigma) \cdot 2^3 \cdot \frac{\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} \cdot S_4}{3!} \;=\; 8\,\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} \cdot S_4,
\]
where the factor $2^3 = 8$ arises because each of the three axes contributes a factor of $2$ from the binary contraction $m_2$ that connects the quartic $m_4$ to the tricomplex structure, and the sum over $S_3$ permutations with the alternating sign is accounted for by the exterior algebra structure ($3!/3! = 1$ for the normalized volume form). Substituting:
\[
 d_4(\omega_3) \;=\; 8 \cdot \frac{c}{2} \cdot \frac{10}{c(5c+22)} \;=\; \frac{40}{5c+22}.
\]
At $c = 1$: $\delta^{(3)}(T_0) = d_4(\omega_3) = 40/27$.

Since $d_4(\omega_3) \neq 0$ for $c \neq 0$ and $5c + 22 \neq 0$, the map $d_4 \colon \Lambda^3 \to \Lambda^0$ is an isomorphism (between $1$-dimensional spaces). On the $E_4$ page:
\[
 E_4^0 = \Lambda^0 / \operatorname{im}(d_4) = 0, \qquad E_4^3 = \ker(d_4|_{\Lambda^3}) = 0,
\]
while $E_4^1 = \kk^3$ and $E_4^2 = \kk^3$ are untouched ($d_4$ neither originates from nor lands in these degrees, since $d_4 \colon E_3^3 \to E_3^0$ is the only nontrivial component).

\smallskip\noindent\textbf{Step 4: Degeneration at $E_4$.}
On $E_4 = [0, 3, 3, 0]$, the nonzero groups occupy degrees~$1$ and~$2$. The differential $d_r$ on $E_r$ maps total degree~$n$ to~$n - (r-1)$. For $d_5$: source degree $n \in \{1, 2\}$ gives target degree $n - 4 \in \{-3, -2\}$, which is outside the support of $E_4$. For any $r \geq 5$: the target degree $n - (r-1) \leq 2 - 4 = -2 < 0$, hence all higher differentials vanish for degree reasons. Therefore $E_4 = E_\infty$, with Poincar\'{e} polynomial $3t + 3t^2$, total dimension~$6$, and Euler characteristic $3 - 3 = 0$.

The $2$-dimensional deficit from class~$L$ is precisely the $d_4$ image: $\dim E_\infty^{\text{class }L} - \dim E_\infty^{\text{class }M} = 8 - 6 = 2 = \dim(\Lambda^0) + \dim(\Lambda^3)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Higher-genus class~\texorpdfstring{$M$}{M} \texorpdfstring{$E_3$}{E-3} bar cohomology: \texorpdfstring{$\dim E_\infty = 6^g$}{dim E-inf = 6-g}]
\label{cor:class-m-higher-genus}
Let $\Sigma_g$ be a closed oriented surface of genus~$g \geq 1$, and let $A = \mathrm{Vir}_c^{\oplus g}$ be $g$ independent copies of the Virasoro VOA at central charge~$c \neq 0$ with $5c + 22 \neq 0$. The $E_3$ bar spectral sequence of $B_{E_3}(A)$ satisfies:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{$E_3$ page:} $\Lambda^\bullet(\kk^{3g})$ with Poincar\'{e} polynomial $(1+t)^{3g}$, total dimension~$2^{3g} = 8^g$.

 \item \emph{$E_4$ page (K\"{u}nneth decomposition).} The $d_4$ differential decomposes as a sum of independent per-copy contractions: $d_4 = \sum_{i=1}^g d_4^{(i)}$, where each $d_4^{(i)}$ acts only on the $i$-th tensor factor of $\Lambda^\bullet(\kk^{3g}) = \bigotimes_{i=1}^g \Lambda^\bullet(\kk^3_i)$. By the K\"{u}nneth theorem for tensor products of chain complexes over a field,
 \[
 E_4 \;=\; H^\bullet\bigl(\Lambda^\bullet(\kk^{3g}),\, d_4\bigr) \;=\; \bigotimes_{i=1}^g H^\bullet\bigl(\Lambda^\bullet(\kk^3_i),\, d_4^{(i)}\bigr) \;=\; \bigl[0,\, 3,\, 3,\, 0\bigr]^{\otimes g},
 \]
 with Poincar\'{e} polynomial $(3t + 3t^2)^g = (3t(1+t))^g = 3^g t^g (1+t)^g$. Degree by degree:
 \[
 \dim E_4^n \;=\; \begin{cases} 3^g \binom{g}{n-g} & \text{if } g \leq n \leq 2g, \\ 0 & \text{otherwise}.\end{cases}
 \]
 Total dimension: $3^g \cdot 2^g = 6^g$. Euler characteristic: $\chi(E_4) = 0$.

 \item \emph{Degeneration for $g \leq 3$.} The $E_4$ page is supported in degrees~$[g,\, 2g]$, a band of width~$g+1$. The differential $d_r$ maps degree~$n$ to~$n - (r-1)$, so for $d_r$ to act nontrivially on~$E_4$, both $n$ and $n - (r-1)$ must lie in $[g,\, 2g]$, requiring $r \leq g + 1$. For $g \leq 3$: $r \leq 4$, so $d_r = 0$ for all $r \geq 5$ on~$E_4$. Hence $E_4 = E_\infty$, and $\dim E_\infty = 6^g$. This is independent of~$S_5, S_6, \ldots$

 \item \emph{Explicit values.}
 \[
 \begin{array}{lcll}
 g = 1: & E_\infty = [0,\, 3,\, 3,\, 0], & \dim = 6 & \text{(Proposition~\ref{prop:virasoro-d4})}. \\
 g = 2: & E_\infty = [0,\, 0,\, 9,\, 18,\, 9,\, 0,\, 0], & \dim = 36 & \text{(deficit } 28 \text{ from class } L\text{)}.\\
 g = 3: & E_\infty = [0,\, 0,\, 0,\, 27,\, 81,\, 81,\, 27,\, 0,\, 0,\, 0], & \dim = 216 & \text{(deficit } 296\text{)}.
 \end{array}
 \]

 \item \emph{Deficit from class~$L$.} The class~$M$ deficit is $8^g - 6^g = (8^g - 6^g)$, with ratio $(4/3)^g \to \infty$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The key input is that $d_4$ on $g$ independent copies acts as a sum of per-copy contractions, each of which is the genus-$1$ differential from Proposition~\ref{prop:virasoro-d4}. On the tensor product $\Lambda^\bullet(\kk^3_i)$ of the $i$-th copy, $d_4^{(i)} \colon \Lambda^3(\kk^3_i) \to \Lambda^0(\kk^3_i)$ is multiplication by $\Delta = 40/(5c+22)$, a nonzero scalar. The cross-terms $m_4(T_i, T_j, T_k, T_l)$ for $i \neq j$ vanish because the copies are independent.

Since $d_4 = \sum_i d_4^{(i)}$ is a sum of differentials acting on independent tensor factors, the K\"{u}nneth theorem (valid over any field) gives $H^\bullet(d_4) = \bigotimes_i H^\bullet(d_4^{(i)})$. Each factor has cohomology $[0,3,3,0]$ by Proposition~\ref{prop:virasoro-d4}. The Poincar\'{e} polynomial of the tensor product is the product $(3t + 3t^2)^g$.

The degeneration argument for $g \leq 3$: on $E_4$ supported in degrees $[g, 2g]$, the differential $d_5$ would require a source-target pair $(n, n-4)$ with both in $[g, 2g]$, i.e.\ $g + 4 \leq 2g$, hence $g \geq 4$. For $g \leq 3$ this is impossible, so $d_5 = 0$ on $E_4$ for degree reasons. All $d_r$ for $r \geq 5$ likewise vanish.

The K\"{u}nneth closed form agrees with explicit $d_4$ matrix rank
computations at $g = 1, 2, 3$: the matrix is built in the multi-degree
basis with Koszul signs $(-1)^{\sum_{j < i} a_j}$, and its rank matches
the closed-form prediction at every degree.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Class \texorpdfstring{$M$}{M} at genus \texorpdfstring{$g \geq 4$}{g >= 4}]
\label{rem:class-m-genus-geq-4}
For $g \geq 4$, the spectral sequence need not degenerate at $E_4$: the differential $d_5$ (controlled by the quintic shadow $S_5 = -16/9$ at $c = 1$) can potentially act. The first instance is $g = 4$, where $d_5 \colon E_4^8 \to E_4^4$ maps between $81$-dimensional spaces. The spectral sequence terminates at $E_{g+2}$ at the latest (all $d_r = 0$ for $r \geq g + 2$ by degree reasons). The remaining proof obligation is to compute the induced action of $m_5$ on $\operatorname{tensor}^g [0,3,3,0]$ and decide whether $d_5$ acts nontrivially on the $E_4$ cohomology.
\end{remark}

\subsection{The Miki automorphism from the CY torus Weyl group on \texorpdfstring{$\C^3$}{C-3}}
\label{subsec:miki-from-e3}
\index{Miki automorphism!CY torus derivation@CY torus derivation}

The Miki automorphism of the quantum toroidal algebra $U_{q,t}(\widehat{\widehat{\fgl}}_1)$ is an $\SL_2(\Z)$ symmetry whose generator $S$ acts by cyclic permutation $(q_1,q_2,q_3) \mapsto (q_2,q_3,q_1)$ (Remark~\ref{rem:miki-automorphism}). Previous treatments observe this symmetry algebraically. We derive it from the Weyl group of the CY torus acting on the $E_3$-chiral structure on $\C^3$, specific to $U_{q,t}(\widehat{\widehat{\fgl}}_1)$ (Miki is algebra-specific, not a consequence of the $E_3$ operad alone; a general $E_3$-algebra such as $k[x]/(x^2)$ admits no Miki automorphism).

\subsubsection{The \texorpdfstring{$S_3$}{S-3}-embedding in \texorpdfstring{$\SO(3)$}{SO(3)}}

The $E_3$ operad $E_3(n) = \Conf_n(D^3)$ carries an action of $\SO(3)$ by rotations of the ambient $3$-disk. The coordinate axes $e_1, e_2, e_3$ of $\R^3$ (underlying $\C^3$ via the holomorphic projection) define a preferred frame, and the symmetric group $S_3$ embeds in $\SO(3)$ as the \emph{rotation symmetry group of the cube} restricted to coordinate permutations: each $\sigma \in S_3$ acts by $e_i \mapsto e_{\sigma(i)}$, realized as a signed permutation matrix with determinant $+1$. Concretely, the cyclic permutation $(123) \in S_3$ is the rotation by $2\pi/3$ around the axis $\ell = \R \cdot (1,1,1)$:
\[
 R_{(123)} = \begin{pmatrix} 0 & 0 & 1 \\ 1 & 0 & 0 \\ 0 & 1 & 0 \end{pmatrix} \in \SO(3),
\]
satisfying $R_{(123)} e_i = e_{i+1 \bmod 3}$, $R_{(123)}^3 = I$, and $R_{(123)}(1,1,1)^T = (1,1,1)^T$ (fixed axis). The transposition $(12) \in S_3$ is the $\pi$-rotation around $\R \cdot (1,1,0)$, exchanging $e_1 \leftrightarrow e_2$. The subgroup $S_3 \hookrightarrow \SO(3)$ is the rotation group of the regular triangular prism with axis~$\ell$, a chiral subgroup of order~$6$.

\subsubsection{From holomorphic torus action to Miki triality}

The holomorphic refinement replaces the $\SO(3)$-action on $E_3$ by the action of the algebraic torus $T = (\C^*)^3/\C^*_{\mathrm{diag}}$ on $\C^3$, where $\C^*_{\mathrm{diag}}$ acts by the CY constraint $q_1 q_2 q_3 = 1$. The Omega-background parameters $(h_1, h_2, h_3)$ with $h_1 + h_2 + h_3 = 0$ are the Lie algebra coordinates on $\mathrm{Lie}(T)$, and the multiplicative parameters $q_i = e^{h_i}$ are the characters. The Weyl group of $T$ in $\GL_3(\C)$ is precisely $S_3$ acting by coordinate permutation. Hence $S_3$ persists from the topological $\SO(3)$-action on $E_3$ to the holomorphic torus action on $\C^3$:
\[
 S_3 \;\hookrightarrow\; \SO(3) \;\curvearrowright\; E_3 \qquad \leadsto \qquad
 S_3 \;=\; W(T) \;\curvearrowright\; T\text{-equivariant } E_3\text{-chiral on } \C^3.
\]
The cyclic subgroup $\Z/3\Z \subset S_3$ generated by $(123)$ acts on the $E_3$-chiral factorization algebra $\cF$ on $\C^3$ by permuting the three equivariant parameters: $(q_1, q_2, q_3) \mapsto (q_2, q_3, q_1)$. Since the factorization homology $\int_{\C^3} \cF$ recovers $U_{q,t}(\widehat{\widehat{\fgl}}_1)$ (Conjecture~\ref{conj:topological-e3-comparison}), this $\Z/3\Z$-action descends to an automorphism $S$ of $U_{q,t}$ with $S^3 = \mathrm{id}$ on parameters. This is the Miki automorphism.

\begin{conjecture}[CY torus derivation of the Miki automorphism for \texorpdfstring{$U_{q,t}(\widehat{\widehat{\fgl}}_1)$}{U-q,t(widehatwidehatfgl-1)}]
\label{conj:miki-from-e3}
Let $\cF$ be the $T$-equivariant $E_3$-chiral factorization algebra on $\C^3$ from Conjecture~\ref{conj:topological-e3-comparison}, with $T = (\C^*)^3/\C^*_{\mathrm{diag}}$ acting by coordinate scaling. Then:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item The Weyl group element $(123) \in W(T) = S_3$ acts on $\cF$ as an $E_3$-algebra automorphism, permuting the three OPE residue differentials $(d_1, d_2, d_3)$ and coproducts $(\Delta_1, \Delta_2, \Delta_3)$ of Conjecture~\ref{conj:e3-koszul-duality}(i) cyclically.
 \item Under factorization homology, this $\Z/3\Z$-action on $\cF$ descends to the Miki automorphism $S$ of $U_{q,t}(\widehat{\widehat{\fgl}}_1)$, acting on parameters by $S \colon (q_1,q_2,q_3) \mapsto (q_2,q_3,q_1)$ and on generators by $S \colon E(z) \leftrightarrow K^+(z)$, $F(z) \leftrightarrow K^-(z)$.
 \item The transposition $(12) \in S_3$ gives a second automorphism $\tau \colon (q_1,q_2,q_3) \mapsto (q_2,q_1,q_3)$, i.e.\ $\tau \colon (q,t) \mapsto (1/t, 1/q)$, with $\tau^2 = \mathrm{id}$. Together, $S$ and $\tau$ generate the full $S_3$-action on parameters.
 \item The $\SL_2(\Z)$-extension: the shift generator $T$ of $\SL_2(\Z)$ is the \emph{Dehn twist} of the factorization torus. In the $E_3$ picture, $T$ arises not from coordinate permutation but from the $E_3$-monodromy: the generator $T \colon E_n \mapsto E_{n+1}$ is the spectral flow around the compactified loop, corresponding to the action of $\pi_1(T) \cong \Z$ on the factorization algebra restricted to the torus $T^2 \subset \C^3$. Equivalently, $T$ is the automorphism of $\cF|_{C \times C}$ induced by the Dehn twist along one cycle of the double-loop base.
 \item The relation $S$ and $T$ satisfy $(ST)^3 = S^2 = Z$ (central), matching the standard $\SL_2(\Z)$ presentation, because: $S^2$ is the half-turn $(q_1,q_2,q_3) \mapsto (q_3,q_1,q_2) \mapsto (q_1,q_2,q_3)$ after applying the CY constraint $q_1 q_2 q_3 = 1$ to reorder, which acts as the central involution on generators; and $(ST)^3$ composes the triality rotation with three Dehn twists, producing the same central element by the lantern relation on the torus.
\end{enumerate}
\emph{Dependency chain}: (i)--(v) are conditional on Conjecture~\ref{conj:topological-e3-comparison} (the $E_3$-chiral structure on $\C^3$). The parameter-level statements (i), (iii) are unconditional consequences of the $S_3$ Weyl group action on $T$; only the descent to algebra automorphisms (ii), (iv), (v) requires the full $E_3$-chiral framework.
\end{conjecture}

\subsubsection{Rational degeneration and \texorpdfstring{$S_3$}{S-3}-breaking}
\label{subsubsec:miki-degeneration}

The affine Yangian $Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ is the rational limit $q_i \to 1$, equivalently $h_i \to 0$ with ratios $\epsilon_i = h_i/\hbar$ fixed. In this limit, the torus $T = (\C^*)^2$ degenerates to the additive group $\C^2$, and the $S_3$-action on $(\epsilon_1, \epsilon_2, \epsilon_3)$ persists as a \emph{parameter symmetry} but ceases to lift to an algebra automorphism. The $E_3$-operadic explanation is:
\begin{itemize}
 \item The $E_3$-chiral structure on $\C^3$ requires all three complex directions on equal footing. In the rational limit, the geometry degenerates to $\C^2 \times \R$ (the 5d holomorphic CS of row~2 in the table of \S\ref{subsec:en-theory-dimension}), which carries only $\Etwo$-chiral structure. The third direction collapses from $\C^*$ to $\R$, breaking the three-fold symmetry.
 \item At the $\Etwo$ level, the Weyl group of $(\C^*)^2$ is $S_2$, not $S_3$: only the transposition $(12)$ survives as an algebra automorphism (the exchange $\epsilon_1 \leftrightarrow \epsilon_2$). The cyclic permutation $(123)$ maps $\epsilon_3 = -\epsilon_1 - \epsilon_2$ into the $\epsilon_1$ slot, but this no longer lifts from the parameter level to an automorphism of $Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$, because the third direction is geometrically distinguished (real, not complex).
 \item The Miki automorphism is therefore a \emph{genuinely $E_3$ phenomenon}: it requires the full holomorphic three-fold symmetry of $\C^3$, and its absence for the affine Yangian is the shadow of the $E_3 \to \Etwo$ degeneration.
\end{itemize}

\begin{remark}[The \texorpdfstring{$T$}{T}-generator as Dehn twist vs.\ spectral flow]
\label{rem:T-generator-dehn}
The shift generator $T \colon E_n \mapsto E_{n+1}$ of the $\SL_2(\Z)$ action on $U_{q,t}$ has two equivalent descriptions in the $E_3$ framework: (a)~the Dehn twist along one cycle of the double-loop base $T^2$, which acts on the factorization algebra by monodromy; (b)~the spectral flow automorphism in the vertex-algebraic language, shifting the mode index by one unit. In the rational degeneration, the Dehn twist degenerates to a translation on $\C$, and the spectral flow degenerates to the shift $J^a_{m,n} \mapsto J^a_{m+1,n}$ (or $J^a_{m,n+1}$) on DDCA generators. The $T$-automorphism survives the rational limit (it generates the infinite cyclic group $\Z \subset \SL_2(\Z)$), but the relation $(ST)^3 = S^2$ breaks because $S$ degenerates.
\end{remark}

\subsection{The chiral \texorpdfstring{$R$}{R}-matrix from \texorpdfstring{$E_3$}{E-3} holomorphic braiding on \texorpdfstring{$\C^3$}{C-3}}
\label{subsec:chiral-rmatrix-e3-braiding}
\index{R-matrix!chiral from E3 braiding@chiral from $E_3$ braiding}

The classical derivation of the quantum $R$-matrix from Chern--Simons theory on $\R^3$ (Costello 2013; Costello--Francis--Gwilliam 2025) extracts the $R$-matrix as the \emph{holonomy} of the CS flat connection on the configuration space $\Conf_2(\R^3)$. Two points in $\R^3$ can be exchanged via a path in $\Conf_2(\R^3) \simeq \R^3 \times S^2$; the holonomy along this path is the $R$-matrix. Since $\pi_1(S^2) = 0$, the braiding is \emph{not} topological (there is no braid group action); rather, the $R$-matrix arises from the holomorphic structure of the Chern--Simons connection. The result is a $z$-independent $R$-matrix $R(q) \in U_q(\frakg) \otimes U_q(\frakg)$ (quantum group type), where $q = e^{2\pi i/(k + h^\vee)}$ encodes the quantization of the CS level.

For the 6d holomorphic theory on $\C^3$, the construction extends to produce a $z$-\emph{dependent} $R$-matrix $R_{\mathrm{ch}}(z)$ from the holomorphic braiding on $\Conf_2(\C^3)$. The key topological and holomorphic data are:

\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{Configuration space.} $\Conf_2(\C^3) = \{(z_1, z_2) \in (\C^3)^2 : z_1 \neq z_2\} \simeq \C^3 \times (\C^3 \setminus \{0\})$. As a real manifold, $\C^3 \setminus \{0\}$ deformation-retracts onto $S^5$, so $\pi_1(\Conf_2(\C^3)) = \pi_1(S^5) = 0$: no topological braiding, no braid group.

 \item \emph{Holomorphic braiding.} The holomorphic $E_3$ structure assigns to each holomorphic separation $z = z_1 - z_2 \in \C^3 \setminus \{0\}$ a braiding operator $R_{\mathrm{ch}}(z)$. The spectral parameter $z$ is a \emph{Yangian} spectral parameter (holomorphic separation), not a worldsheet insertion coordinate.

 \item \emph{Rational vs topological.} The 3d theory on $\R^3$ produces a $z$-independent $R$-matrix (quantum group, topological). The 6d theory on $\C^3$ produces a $z$-dependent $R$-matrix (Yangian/rational type). This dichotomy is the geometric origin of the quantum group vs Yangian distinction: \emph{compactification} of the spectral parameter ($\C \to \C^* \to \C/\Lambda$) recovers the trigonometric and elliptic levels.

 \item \emph{Two-parameter extension.} Choosing a Wilson line along one direction of $\C^3$ leaves two transverse directions with independent spectral parameters $(u, v)$. The full $E_3$ $R$-matrix is $R_{\mathrm{ch}}(u, v)$.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{conjecture}[Chiral \texorpdfstring{$R$}{R}-matrix from \texorpdfstring{$E_3$}{E-3} holomorphic braiding]
\label{conj:chiral-rmatrix-e3-braiding}
Let $\cF$ be the $E_3$-chiral factorization algebra on $\C^3$ from Conjecture~\ref{conj:topological-e3-comparison}, with Omega-background parameters $(h_1, h_2, h_3)$, $h_1 + h_2 + h_3 = 0$, and let $g(z) = \prod_{i=1}^{3} (z - h_i)/(z + h_i)$ be the Yangian structure function~\eqref{eq:yangian-structure-function}. The $R$-matrix from the holomorphic braiding on $\Conf_2(\C^3)$ has the following structure:

\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
 \item \emph{Charge~$1$ (Zamolodchikov factorization).} The charge-$1$ $R$-matrix is the scalar
 \[
 R_{\mathrm{ch}}^{(1)}(u, v) \;=\; g(u) \cdot g(v) \cdot g(u - v),
 \]
 with three factors: direction-$1$ braiding, direction-$2$ braiding, and the crossing factor (Miki automorphism kernel). This is exact at charge~$1$ (scalars commute).

 \item \emph{CY inversion.} The identity $g(z)\,g(-z) = 1$ (equation~\eqref{eq:cy-condition-rmatrix}) implies:
 \[
 R_{\mathrm{ch}}^{(1)}(u, v) \cdot R_{\mathrm{ch}}^{(1)}(-u, -v) \;=\; 1.
 \]

 \item \emph{Miki exchange phase.} The ratio $R_{\mathrm{ch}}^{(1)}(u,v) / R_{\mathrm{ch}}^{(1)}(v,u) = g(u-v)^2$ measures the asymmetry between the two transverse directions under the Miki exchange of Conjecture~\ref{conj:miki-from-e3}.

 \item \emph{Parametric tetrahedron (charge~$1$).} The parametric tetrahedron equation on the constraint surface is automatically satisfied at charge~$1$ (commutativity of scalar multiplication).

 \item \emph{Charge~$2$ (Zamolodchikov leading term).} On the charge-$2$ Fock space with diagonal $R$-matrix $R_\lambda(z)$ (equation~\eqref{eq:r-matrix-partitions}), the $E_3$ $R$-matrix is approximated by
 \[
 R_{\mathrm{ch}}^{(2)}(u,v) \;\approx\; R^{(2)}(u) \cdot R^{(2)}(v) \cdot R^{(2)}(u-v)
 \]
 with corrections of order $O(\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}^2)$ from the ZTE obstruction (Theorem~\ref{thm:zte-failure}).

 \item \emph{$K3$ restriction.} For $K3 \times C$ with Mukai lattice $\Lambda$ of rank~$24$, the structure function restricts to $g_{K3}(z) = \prod_{a=1}^{24} (z - h_a)/(z + h_a)$ (degree $(24,24)$, satisfying $g_{K3}(z)\,g_{K3}(-z) = 1$ and $g_{K3}(0) = +1$ from the even rank). The two-parameter $K3$ $R$-matrix is $R_{K3,\mathrm{ch}}(u,v) = g_{K3}(u) \cdot g_{K3}(v) \cdot g_{K3}(u-v)$.
\end{enumerate}
\emph{Dependency chain}: conditional on Conjecture~\ref{conj:topological-e3-comparison} ($E_3$-chiral structure on $\C^3$) and on a witnessed CY-A$_3$ Stage-$2$ restriction datum for the $K3$ fibre. The algebraic identities (b), (c), (d) are unconditional.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[Dimensional hierarchy of holomorphic holonomy]
\label{rem:dimensional-hierarchy-holonomy}
The $\En$ level determines the type of $R$-matrix:
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{ccccc}
 \toprule
 $\dim_{\C}$ & Theory & $\pi_1(\Conf_2)$ & $R$-matrix type & Algebra \\
 \midrule
 $1$ & 3d hol CS on $C \times \R$ & $\Z$ & $z$-independent & $U_q(\frakg)$ \\
 $2$ & 5d hol CS on $\C^2 \times \R$ & $0$ & $R(z)$, $1$ param & $Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ \\
 $3$ & 6d hol theory on $\C^3$ & $0$ & $R_{\mathrm{ch}}(u,v)$, $2$ params & $U_{q,t}(\widehat{\widehat{\fgl}}_1)$ \\
 \bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Higher dimension gives more spectral parameters (richer $R$-matrix data) at the cost of trivializing $\pi_1$ (holomorphic braiding rather than topological). The dimensional projection $E_3 \to \Etwo$ at $v = 0$ recovers $R_{\mathrm{ch}}(u, 0) = g(u) \cdot g(0) \cdot g(u) = -g(u)^2$ (noting $g(0) = -1$), which is the \emph{square} of the $\Etwo$ $R$-matrix up to the CY sign. The trigonometric structure function $G(x) = \prod_i (1 - q_i x)/(1 - q_i^{-1} x)$ satisfies $G(x; q_i) \to 1/g(z; h_i)$ in the rational limit $x = e^{\varepsilon z}$, $q_i = e^{\varepsilon h_i}$, $\varepsilon \to 0$ (the numerator/denominator swap between the two conventions).
\end{remark}

\subsection{Factorization homology on \texorpdfstring{$\C^3$}{C-3} and the chiral quantum group}
\label{subsec:fact-hom-c3}
\label{subsec:qg-from-fh}

The factorization homology framework of Costello--Francis--Gwilliam computes the global observables of the 6d theory on a $3$-dimensional complex manifold $M$ as the factorization homology integral
\[
 \int_M \cF \;=\; \text{(global observables of the 6d theory on $M$)},
\]
where $\cF$ is the local $E_3$-algebra of observables. For $M = \C^3$, the integral is expected to recover the quantum toroidal algebra $U_{q,t}(\widehat{\widehat{\fgl}}_1)$ (conditional on Conjecture~\ref{conj:topological-e3-comparison}). For $M = K3 \times E$ the same sentence is not a consequence of local $\C^3$ theory or CY-A$_3$ alone: the BKM-related chiral algebra requires a compact hCS factorisation algebra, an oriented compact critical Hall/CoHA realisation, PBW/no-extra-relations, a non-degenerate Hall pairing, and the Borcherds comparison data.

\begin{conjecture}[Factorization homology of \texorpdfstring{$E_3$}{E-3}-chiral on \texorpdfstring{$K3 \times E$}{K3 x E}]
\label{conj:fact-hom-k3xe}
Assume a compact $E_3$ hCS factorization algebra $\cF$ on $K3 \times E$ and the compact Hall/CoHA, PBW, pairing, and Borcherds comparison data above. Then the factorization homology $\int_{K3 \times E} \cF$ is expected to compute:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item The K3 integral $\int_{K3} \cF|_{K3}$ should produce a chiral algebra on $E$ whose genus-two character is compared with the unnormalised Igusa square $\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}=\Delta_5^2$.
 \item The $E$ integral $\int_E \left(\int_{K3} \cF|_{K3}\right)$ should produce the genus-$1$ partition function of the 6d theory on $K3 \times E$, after the OP normalisation $\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{OP}}=4096^{-1}\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}$ is fixed.
 \item The intermediate integral $\int_{K3} \cF|_{K3}$ should be the \emph{toroidal Kac--Moody enveloping chiral algebra} on $E$: a vertex algebra on the elliptic curve $E$ whose representation category is compared with the braided monoidal category of BPS states of $K3 \times E$ only after the compact Hall--Drinfeld double data have been supplied.
\end{enumerate}
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[What factorization homology adds]
\label{rem:fact-hom-adds}
Conjecture~\ref{conj:fact-hom-k3xe}(iii) is the proposed identity between the ``toroidal Kac--Moody enveloping chiral algebra on $E$'' (the target of the Costello programme) and the factorization homology of the compact $E_3$ hCS algebra over K3. The K3 surface is the \emph{integration domain} rather than an auxiliary CY parameter: the K3 geometry is ``integrated out,'' and the result is expected to be a chiral algebra on $E$ that remembers the K3 data through the root datum and the \emph{expected} $\kappa_\bullet$-spectrum. The total-space manifold invariants $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(K3 \times E) = \chi(\cO_{K3}) \cdot \chi(\cO_E) = 2 \cdot 0 = 0$ and $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_{K3\times E})=0$ by Hodge supertrace (the K3-fibre value $\chi(\cO_{K3}) = 2$ is $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(K3)$, not the total-space invariant) and $\kappa_{\mathrm{fiber}} = 24$ are unconditional topological/Hodge data. The algebraization invariants $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}} = 3$ and $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}} = c_1(0)/2 = 5$ require the witnessed CY-A$_3$ Stage-$2$ datum together with the compact algebraization and Borcherds comparison data; they are not consequences of compact total-space Hodge theory.
\end{remark}


\subsection{The Nekrasov partition function as \texorpdfstring{$E_3$}{E-3} factorization homology}
\label{subsec:nekrasov-e3}

The Nekrasov partition function of 4d $\cN = 2$ gauge theory in the $\Omega$-background provides the principal computational bridge between the $E_3$-chiral factorization algebra on $\C^3$ and the quantum toroidal algebra of \S\ref{subsec:fact-hom-c3}. The connection passes through the 6d $(2,0)$ theory compactified on $\C^2 \times C$ with $\Omega$-background parameters $(\varepsilon_1, \varepsilon_2)$ on $\C^2$ and a holomorphic direction along $C$: the resulting 5d holomorphic Chern--Simons theory on $\C^2 \times C$ carries the $E_3$-structure of Conjecture~\ref{conj:topological-e3-comparison}.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{(1) Nekrasov partition function and the $E_3$ factorization integral.}
The Nekrasov partition function for $U(1)$ gauge theory is the $T$-equivariant volume of the instanton moduli space:
\begin{equation}
 Z_{\mathrm{Nek}}(\varepsilon_1, \varepsilon_2, a; q) \;=\; \sum_{n \geq 0} q^n \int_{\mathrm{Hilb}^n(\C^2)} \mathbf{1}^{T}
 \;=\; \prod_{n=1}^{\infty} \frac{1}{1 - q^n},
 \label{eq:nekrasov-u1}
\end{equation}
where $T = (\C^*)^2$ acts on $\C^2$ with weights $(\varepsilon_1, \varepsilon_2)$ and the $U(1)$ result is the generating function for $\chi_T(\mathrm{Hilb}^n(\C^2)) = p(n)$ (G\"ottsche's formula). The plethystic logarithm $\mathrm{PLog}(Z_{\mathrm{Nek}}) = q/(1-q)$ gives the single-particle BPS spectrum: one bosonic creation mode at each instanton number, matching the positive generator $E_0$ of $U_{q,t}(\widehat{\widehat{\fgl}}_1)$ (the annihilation operator $F_0$ acts in the opposite direction).

In the $E_3$ framework, the identification is:
\begin{equation}
 Z_{\mathrm{Nek}}(\varepsilon_1, \varepsilon_2, a; q) \;=\; \int_{\C^2} \cF\big|_{\C^2},
 \label{eq:nekrasov-fact-hom}
\end{equation}
