where $\cF$ is the $E_3$-chiral factorization algebra of Conjecture~\ref{conj:topological-e3-comparison} restricted to $\C^2 \subset \C^3$, and the integral is factorization homology in the sense of Costello--Francis--Gwilliam. The restriction $\cF|_{\C^2}$ is $\Etwo$-chiral (losing one chiral level), and the factorization homology over $\C^2$ computes the global observables of the 5d theory on $\C^2$, which are the instanton partition function. The $\Omega$-background parameters $(\varepsilon_1, \varepsilon_2)$ are the equivariant parameters of the $T$-action, and $\varepsilon_3 = -\varepsilon_1 - \varepsilon_2$ (the CY condition $h_1 + h_2 + h_3 = 0$) is the parameter of the holomorphic direction $C$. The factorization homology integral makes~\eqref{eq:nekrasov-fact-hom} \emph{structural}: the instanton sum is the $\Etwo$-factorization homology over $\C^2$ of a locally-defined factorization algebra, hence a homological object before it is a generating function.

\begin{remark}[Scope: intermediary mechanism]
\label{rem:nekrasov-scope}
The passage from the $\Omega$-background equivariant parameters $(\varepsilon_1, \varepsilon_2)$ to the quantum toroidal parameters $(q, t) = (e^{\varepsilon_1}, e^{-\varepsilon_2})$ is mediated by equivariant localization on the Nekrasov instanton moduli space: the equivariant weights of $T$ on the tangent space $T_I \mathrm{Hilb}^n(\C^2)$ at a fixed point $I$ produce the box-counting measure, and the identification $(q, t) = (e^{h_1}, e^{-h_2})$ of the DIM algebra arises from the $T$-character of the universal sheaf. The identification~\eqref{eq:nekrasov-fact-hom} is conditional on Conjecture~\ref{conj:topological-e3-comparison}; for $\fgl_1$ gauge algebra, the factorization homology reduces to Nakajima's computation of $K_T(\mathrm{Hilb}^n(\C^2))$, which is unconditional (Nakajima 1999).
\end{remark}

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{(2) AGT correspondence as factorization descent.}
The AGT correspondence (Alday--Gaiotto--Tachikawa, 2009) identifies
\begin{equation}
 Z_{\mathrm{Nek}}^{U(N)}(\varepsilon_1, \varepsilon_2, \vec{a}; q) \;=\; \langle V_{\alpha_1}(0) \, V_{\alpha_2}(q) \, \cdots \rangle_{\cW_N},
 \label{eq:agt}
\end{equation}
where the right-hand side is a conformal block of the $\cW_N$-algebra at central charge $c = (N-1)(1 - N(N+1)(\varepsilon_1 + \varepsilon_2)^2/(\varepsilon_1 \varepsilon_2))$, with vertex operators $V_{\alpha_i}$ labelled by the Coulomb parameters $\vec{a}$.

In the $E_3$ picture, AGT is factorization descent along the fibration $\C^2 \to C$ with fiber $\C$:
\[
 \int_{\C^2} \cF\big|_{\C^2} \;=\; \left\langle \int_C \cF\big|_C \right\rangle_{\cF|_{\C^2/C}},
\]
where the fiber integral $\int_\C \cF|_\C$ produces the $\cW_{1+\infty}$-algebra on $C$ (the conformal field theory), and the remaining integral over $C$ computes the conformal block. The factorization descent formula is the $E_3$ origin of AGT: the passage $E_3 \to \Etwo \to \Eone$ (from $\C^3$ to $\C^2$ to $C$) corresponds to the passage from the 6d theory to the 4d partition function to the 2d conformal block. The vertex operators $V_{\alpha_i}$ are the images under $\Eone$-projection of defect operators in the $E_3$-algebra supported on complex codimension-$2$ submanifolds of $\C^3$.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{(3) Self-dual point $\varepsilon_1 + \varepsilon_2 = 0$ and $E_3$ degeneration.}
At the self-dual point $\varepsilon_1 = -\varepsilon_2$ (equivalently $h_3 = 0$), the structure function of the quantum toroidal algebra degenerates:
\[
 G(x; q_1, q_2, q_3)\big|_{q_3 = 1} \;=\; \frac{(1 - q_1 x)(1 - q_2 x)(1 - x)}{(1 - x/q_1)(1 - x/q_2)(1 - x)} \;=\; \frac{(1 - q_1 x)(1 - q_2 x)}{(1 - x/q_1)(1 - x/q_2)},
\]
which is the structure function of the \emph{affine Yangian} $Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ (the rational degeneration). The $[E, F]$ commutator normalization $1/(q_3 - q_3^{-1})$ of the DIM algebra (cf.\ \texttt{quantum\_toroidal\_e1\_cy3.py}, \texttt{dim\_ef\_delta\_coefficient}) diverges at $q_3 = 1$, and the limiting algebra is $Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ with its additive $R$-matrix.

In the $E_3$ language, $h_3 = 0$ is the locus where the third chiral level collapses: the $\varepsilon_3$-direction contributes no nontrivial $\Eone$-structure, and the residual algebra is $\Etwo$-chiral. The hierarchy of degenerations applies:
\[
 E_3\text{-chiral } \xrightarrow{h_3 = 0} \Etwo\text{-chiral (affine Yangian)} \xrightarrow{h_2 = 0} \Eone\text{-chiral (Yangian)}.
\]
Nakajima's simplification theorem for $U(N)$: at $\varepsilon_1 + \varepsilon_2 = 0$, the instanton measure on $\mathrm{Hilb}^n(\C^2)$ becomes equidimensional (all fixed-point contributions have equal weight), so the partition function reduces to the Euler characteristic. The $E_3$ interpretation: at $h_3 = 0$ the three-fold symmetry breaks, the Miki automorphism degenerates (\S\ref{subsubsec:miki-degeneration}), and the factorization homology integral localizes to the $\Etwo$-sector, where it computes a topological (not holomorphic) invariant.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{(4) Quantum toroidal action on $K_T(\mathrm{Hilb}^n(\C^2))$ as $E_3$ operations.}
The quantum toroidal algebra $U_{q,t}(\widehat{\widehat{\fgl}}_1)$ acts on the Fock space $\bigoplus_{n \geq 0} K_T(\mathrm{Hilb}^n(\C^2))$ by the Schiffmann--Vasserot action. The $E_3$ factorization algebra on $\C^3$ provides the geometric origin of each generator:

\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}
\small
\begin{tabular}{lll}
 \toprule
 DIM generator & Geometric operation on $\mathrm{Hilb}^n$ & $E_3$ origin \\
 \midrule
 $E_0$ (creation) & Hecke correspondence $\mathrm{Hilb}^n \leftarrow Z \to \mathrm{Hilb}^{n+1}$ & $\Eone$ (instanton direction) \\
 $F_0$ (annihilation) & Dual Hecke $\mathrm{Hilb}^{n+1} \leftarrow Z \to \mathrm{Hilb}^n$ & $\Eone$ (dual instanton) \\
 $K_m^+$ ($m > 0$) & Adams operations $\psi^m$ on $K_T$ & $\Eone$ ($\varepsilon_2$-direction) \\
 $E_m, F_m$ ($m \neq 0$) & Twisted Hecke by $\cO(m)$ on universal sheaf & $\Etwo$ braiding ($\varepsilon_1, \varepsilon_2$) \\
 Miki $S$ & Fourier--Mukai on $K_T(\mathrm{Hilb})$ & $E_3$ triality $(h_1 \leftrightarrow h_2 \leftrightarrow h_3)$ \\
 \bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent The first four rows use only $\Eone$ or $\Etwo$ structure. The Miki automorphism $S$ is the genuinely $E_3$ operation: it acts as $(q_1, q_2, q_3) \to (q_2, q_3, q_1)$ (cf.\ \texttt{MikiAutomorphism.S\_on\_parameters} in the compute engine), which is the triality of Conjecture~\ref{conj:miki-from-e3}(ii). On the Fock space, $S$ interchanges the Hecke operators $E_n$ with the Cartan operators $K_n^+$, exchanging the ``instanton'' direction with the ``momentum'' direction. Geometrically, this is the Fourier--Mukai transform on the derived category of sheaves on $\C^2$, which swaps the roles of the two $\C^*$-factors in the equivariant K-theory.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{(5) Kontsevich--Soibelman wall-crossing from $E_3$ Koszul duality.}
The Kontsevich--Soibelman wall-crossing formula for BPS invariants of a CY$_3$ category $\cC$ takes the form of a factorization of the motivic DT generating function across walls of marginal stability in $\mathrm{Stab}(\cC)$.

\begin{conjecture}[Wall-crossing as \texorpdfstring{$E_3$}{E-3} Koszul degeneration]
\label{conj:ks-e3-koszul}
Let $\cF$ be the $E_3$-chiral factorization algebra on $\C^3$ associated to the CY$_3$ category $\cC$, and let $B_{E_3}(\cF)$ be its $E_3$ bar complex (Conjecture~\ref{conj:e3-koszul-duality}).
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item The walls of marginal stability in $\mathrm{Stab}(\cC)$ are the loci where one of the three differentials $d_i$ of $B_{E_3}(\cF)$ degenerates (has enhanced cohomology). Crossing a wall changes the spectral sequence computing $H^\bullet(B_{E_3}(\cF), d_1 + d_2 + d_3)$.
 \item The KS wall-crossing automorphism $\mathfrak{S}_\gamma \in \mathrm{Aut}(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ for a BPS state of charge $\gamma$ is the monodromy of the $E_3$ factorization along a loop in $\mathrm{Stab}(\cC)$ encircling the wall. This monodromy is computed by the $E_3$ Koszul duality pairing: $\mathfrak{S}_\gamma = \langle B_{E_3}(\cF), B_{E_3}(\cF^!) \rangle_\gamma$.
 \item The KS product formula $\prod_{\gamma}^{\curvearrowleft} \mathfrak{S}_\gamma = 1$ (labeled-ordered product over all BPS states) is the statement that the total $E_3$ bar differential $d_1 + d_2 + d_3$ squares to zero globally. Each stability condition determines a different spectral sequence (a different labeled-ordering of the product by phase of $Z(\gamma)$), but all converge to the same abutment: the $E_3$ bar cohomology $H^\bullet(B_{E_3}(\cF))$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[Conditionality and evidence]
\label{rem:ks-e3-conditionality}
Conjecture~\ref{conj:ks-e3-koszul} depends on Conjecture~\ref{conj:e3-koszul-duality} ($E_3$ Koszul duality) $\to$ Conjecture~\ref{conj:topological-e3-comparison} ($E_3$ factorization on $\C^3$) $\to$ the witnessed CY-A$_3$ Stage-$2$ locus. For toric CY$_3$ (where the Nekrasov partition function is computed by localization), items (i)--(iii) can be verified at the level of the affine Yangian: the wall-crossing automorphisms are the Maulik--Okounkov stable envelopes, and the ``$d^2 = 0$ independence'' is the associativity of the stable envelope composition (Maulik--Okounkov, \emph{Quantum groups and quantum cohomology}, 2019). The word ``labeled-ordered'' in (iii) is used in the labeled (non-coinvariant) sense: it is the combinatorial ordering by phase of the central charge $Z(\gamma)$, not a time-ordering or radial ordering.
\end{remark}


\subsection{The \texorpdfstring{$\Omega$}{Omega}-background as equivariant derived algebraic geometry}
\label{subsec:omega-equivariant-dag}

The $\Omega$-background on $\C^3$ with parameters $(\varepsilon_1, \varepsilon_2, \varepsilon_3)$ satisfying $\varepsilon_1 + \varepsilon_2 + \varepsilon_3 = 0$ is the $T$-equivariant geometry of the torus
\begin{equation}
 T \;=\; (\C^*)^3 / \C^*_{\mathrm{diag}} \quad (\text{rank } 2),
 \label{eq:cy-torus}
\end{equation}
acting on $\C^3$ by coordinate-wise scaling. The CY condition $\varepsilon_1 + \varepsilon_2 + \varepsilon_3 = 0$ ensures that $T$ preserves the holomorphic $3$-form $\Omega = dz_1 \wedge dz_2 \wedge dz_3$: the $T$-weight of $\Omega$ is $-(\varepsilon_1 + \varepsilon_2 + \varepsilon_3) = 0$.

In derived algebraic geometry, the $T$-equivariant K-theory of a point is the representation ring
\begin{equation}
 K_T(\mathrm{pt}) \;=\; \Z[\varepsilon_1, \varepsilon_2, \varepsilon_3] / (\varepsilon_1 + \varepsilon_2 + \varepsilon_3) \;\cong\; \Z[\varepsilon_1, \varepsilon_2],
 \label{eq:kt-ring}
\end{equation}
a free polynomial ring of rank~$2$. The elementary symmetric polynomials satisfy $\sigma_1 = 0$ (CY condition), $\sigma_2 = -(\varepsilon_1^2 + \varepsilon_1 \varepsilon_2 + \varepsilon_2^2)$, and $\sigma_3 = -\varepsilon_1 \varepsilon_2(\varepsilon_1 + \varepsilon_2)$. The Kac--Moody level is $k = -\sigma_2 > 0$ for generic real parameters.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{(1) Equivariant index on $\mathrm{Hilb}^n(\C^3)$.}
The $T$-equivariant Euler characteristic of the Hilbert scheme is
\begin{equation}
 \sum_{n \geq 0} q^n \, \chi_T(\mathrm{Hilb}^n(\C^3)) \;=\; M(q) \;=\; \prod_{n=1}^{\infty} \frac{1}{(1-q^n)^n},
 \label{eq:equiv-index-hilb-c3}
\end{equation}
the MacMahon function. The $T$-fixed points of $\mathrm{Hilb}^n(\C^3)$ are monomial ideals in $\C[z_1, z_2, z_3]$ of colength~$n$, in bijection with $3$D partitions (plane partitions) of~$n$. Each fixed point contributes $1$ to the equivariant Euler characteristic: the tangent space $T$-character at each fixed point has first Chern class $c_1^T = n(\varepsilon_1 + \varepsilon_2 + \varepsilon_3) = 0$ by the CY cancellation, so the equivariant Euler class is $1$. The result is $\chi_T(\mathrm{Hilb}^n(\C^3)) = p_3(n)$, the number of plane partitions of~$n$ (OEIS A000219).

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{(2) K3 $\times$ $\C$ in the $\Omega$-background.}
For K3 $\times$ $\C$ with a single $\Omega$-parameter $\varepsilon$ on $\C$ (setting $\varepsilon_1 = \varepsilon$, $\varepsilon_2 = -\varepsilon$ on the K3 directions, $\varepsilon_3 = 0$), the $T$-equivariant cohomology
\[
 H^*_T(\mathrm{K3}) \;=\; H^*(\mathrm{K3}, \C) \otimes \C[\varepsilon]
\]
has rank $24$ over $\C[\varepsilon]$, matching the $24$ Betti-number sum $\sum_i b_i = 1 + 0 + 22 + 0 + 1$. These $24$ classes give the $24$ currents $J^{(a)}(z)$ of the K3 Yangian $Y(\frakg_{\mathrm{K3}})$ (conjectural; \S\ref{sec:handle-fh-k3}). The structure function $g_{\mathrm{K3}}(u) = \prod_{i=1}^{24} (u - h_i)/(u + h_i)$ is a degree-$(24, 24)$ rational function with the CY inversion identity $g_{\mathrm{K3}}(u) \cdot g_{\mathrm{K3}}(-u) = 1$, where $h_1, \ldots, h_{24}$ are the Mukai lattice parameters satisfying $\sum_i h_i = 0$.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{(3) Self-dual limit $\varepsilon_1 = -\varepsilon_2$: $E_3 \to \Etwo$.}
At the self-dual point ($\varepsilon_3 = 0$), the cubic deformation parameter $\sigma_3 = \varepsilon_1 \varepsilon_2 \varepsilon_3 = 0$ vanishes. The structure function of the quantum toroidal algebra degenerates from degree $(3,3)$ to $(2,2)$:
\[
 g(u)\big|_{\varepsilon_3 = 0} \;=\; \frac{(u - \varepsilon_1)(u - \varepsilon_2)}{(u + \varepsilon_1)(u + \varepsilon_2)},
\]
since the $(u - 0)/(u + 0) = 1$ factor cancels. This is the structure function of the affine Yangian $Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ ($\Etwo$-chiral), not the quantum toroidal algebra ($E_3$-chiral). For K3 $\times$ $\C$: the K3 Yangian $Y(\frakg_{\mathrm{K3}})$ collapses to the K3 Heisenberg $H_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ (OPE without Yangian corrections, since $\sigma_3 = 0$). The Kac--Moody level remains $k = -\sigma_2 = \varepsilon^2 > 0$.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{(4) Nekrasov--Shatashvili limit $\varepsilon_2 \to 0$: $\Etwo \to \Eone$.}
In the NS limit ($\varepsilon_2 = 0$, $\varepsilon_3 = -\varepsilon_1$), both $\sigma_3 = 0$ and the structure function becomes \emph{trivial}:
\[
 g(u)\big|_{\varepsilon_2 = 0} \;=\; \frac{(u - \varepsilon_1) \cdot u \cdot (u + \varepsilon_1)}{(u + \varepsilon_1) \cdot u \cdot (u - \varepsilon_1)} \;=\; 1.
\]
The $\Etwo$-braiding degenerates to $\Eone$ (no braiding): the algebra becomes the Yangian $Y(\fgl_1)$ without deformation, corresponding to quantum mechanics (Bethe ansatz). The full degeneration cascade is:
\[
 E_3 \text{-chiral (quantum toroidal)} \xrightarrow{\varepsilon_3 = 0} \Etwo\text{-chiral (affine Yangian)} \xrightarrow{\varepsilon_2 = 0} \Eone\text{-chiral (Yangian, } g = 1\text{)}.
\]

\begin{remark}[Orthogonal K$3$ Yangian \texorpdfstring{$Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4, 20))$}{Y-so(4,20)} and its Hodge-parity $\Z/2$-super-extension from the Mukai lattice]
\label{rem:super-yangian-mukai}
The Mukai lattice of K$3$ has signature $(4, 20)$: four positive and twenty negative eigenvalues. The Mukai form is a symmetric indefinite bilinear form, so the preserving Lie algebra is the indefinite-orthogonal $\mathfrak{so}(4, 20)$ of Kac type $D_{12}$, not $\fgl$ (which has no invariant bilinear form) and not Kac $\mathfrak{osp}(m \mid n)$ (whose odd form is symplectic). The Hodge involution $\theta$ on $\widetilde H^*(\mathrm{K3}, \C)$ produces the polarisation $\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}} \otimes \C = V_+ \oplus V_-$ with $\dim V_+ = 4$ and $\dim V_- = 20$; on both graded parts the Mukai pairing remains symmetric. The ungraded envelope is the Molev--Ragoucy~\cite{MolevRagoucy2008} indefinite-orthogonal Yangian $Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4, 20))$; the programme-specific Hodge-parity $\Z/2$-super-extension $Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4 \mid 20))$ is a non-Kac refinement whose classical limit is the $\Z/2$-graded Lie algebra $\mathfrak{so}(4 \mid 20)$ with
\[
 \mathfrak{so}(4 \mid 20)_{\bar 0} \;=\; \mathfrak{so}(4) \oplus \mathfrak{so}(20),
 \qquad \dim \mathfrak{so}(4 \mid 20)_{\bar 0} \;=\; \tbinom{4}{2} + \tbinom{20}{2} \;=\; 196,
\]
and odd part $V_+ \otimes V_- \simeq \C^{80}$, giving $\dim \mathfrak{so}(4 \mid 20) = (196, 80)$. Kac $\mathfrak{osp}(4 \mid 20)$ (whose even part would be $\mathfrak{so}(4) \oplus \mathfrak{sp}(20)$ of dimension $216$) is explicitly excluded.

\begin{conjecture}[Mukai-preserving K$3$ Yangian and its Hodge-parity $\Z/2$-super-extension]
Assume a witnessed $\Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2,C)}$ specialisation of the K3-fibre CY$_3$ datum and an orthogonal reflection-equation comparison for the Mukai lattice. The expected Yangian quantization of the resulting chiral algebra is $Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4, 20))$ in the Molev--Ragoucy presentation; a $\Z/2$-graded refinement $Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4 \mid 20))$ supported by the Hodge involution is conjecturally the stage-$2$ image of a super-enhancement $\Phi^{\mathrm{super}}_3$. The equivariant derived geometry sees the $(V_+, V_-)$ polarisation through the $T$-character of the Mukai lattice.
\end{conjecture}
\end{remark}
\section{Handle decomposition and the \texorpdfstring{$E_\infty$}{E-inf} factorization homology of K3}
\label{sec:handle-fh-k3}

The preceding subsection computes $\int_M \cF$ on $\C^3$ and its equivariant specialisations.
For compact manifolds, the same factorization homology integral is computed by iterative excision through a handle decomposition (Proposition~\ref{prop:fh-excision}).
This section carries out the $E_\infty$ computation on K3 explicitly, recovering the Mukai Heisenberg $H_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ as a consistency check, and sets up the handle-based approach to the conjectural toroidal Kac--Moody structure.

\subsection{Handle decomposition of K3}
\label{subsec:handle-k3}

\begin{proposition}[Handle decomposition of K3]
\label{prop:handle-decomposition-k3}
The K3 surface (a simply connected closed oriented $4$-manifold) admits a handle decomposition with
\[
 1 \text{ zero-handle}\; (D^4),
 \qquad
 22 \text{ two-handles}\; (D^2 \times D^2),
 \qquad
 1 \text{ four-handle}\; (D^4),
\]
and no one-handles or three-handles ($\pi_1(K3) = 0$, $b_1 = b_3 = 0$).
The two-handles are attached along framed knots in $S^3 = \partial D^4$, with framing data encoded by the intersection form $Q_{K3}$ of signature $(3, 19)$ and lattice type $3U \oplus 2E_8(-1)$ (even, unimodular).
The Euler characteristic check: $\chi = 1 - 0 + 22 - 0 + 1 = 24$.
\end{proposition}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$E_\infty$}{E-inf} factorization homology: \texorpdfstring{$\int_{K3} H_1 = H_{\mathrm{Muk}}$}{int-K3 H-1 = H-Muk}}
\label{subsec:einf-fh-k3}

For an $E_\infty$ (commutative) algebra $A$ and a manifold $M$, the Ayala--Francis theorem gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:einf-fh-general}
 \int_M A \;\simeq\; A \otimes H_\bullet(M; k).
\end{equation}
For K3 with Betti numbers $(b_0, b_1, b_2, b_3, b_4) = (1, 0, 22, 0, 1)$ and $\dim H_\bullet(K3; \Q) = 24$:

\begin{proposition}[\texorpdfstring{$E_\infty$}{E-inf} factorization homology of \texorpdfstring{$H_1$}{H-1} over K3]
\label{prop:einf-fh-k3}
Let $H_1$ denote the rank-$1$ Heisenberg algebra (an $E_\infty$-algebra).
The $E_\infty$ factorization homology of $H_1$ over K3 is a rank-$24$ Heisenberg algebra:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:einf-fh-k3-formula}
 \int_{K3} H_1
 \;\simeq\;
 H_1 \otimes H_\bullet(K3; \Q)
 \;\simeq\;
 H_{\mathrm{Muk}},
\end{equation}
with $24$ generators decomposing by homological degree:
\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.2}
\begin{tabular}{ccl}
\toprule
Degree & Generators & Origin \\
\midrule
$H_0(K3)$ & $1$ & zero-mode (``momentum'') \\
$H_2(K3)$ & $22$ & lattice directions (K3 intersection form) \\
$H_4(K3)$ & $1$ & winding mode (fundamental class) \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The pairing on the output is induced by the intersection form on $H_\bullet(K3)$: the $H_0$--$H_4$ pair forms a hyperbolic plane $U$ of signature $(1,1)$, and $H_2$ carries the K3 intersection form of signature $(3, 19)$.
Total: $(1 + 3, 1 + 19) = (4, 20)$, the Mukai signature.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The Ayala--Francis theorem~\eqref{eq:einf-fh-general} gives $\int_{K3} H_1 \simeq H_1 \otimes H_\bullet(K3)$ as graded vector spaces.
The algebra structure is the tensor product of commutative algebras: $H_1$ is $E_\infty$ and $H_\bullet(K3)$ is a graded-commutative ring (the cohomology ring with Poincar\'e dual grading).
The output is the generalized Heisenberg algebra $\mathrm{Heis}(H_\bullet(K3), \omega)$, where $\omega$ is the Mukai pairing.
The pairing computation: $H^0$--$H^4$ pairing gives the Mukai extension $\langle (1, 0, 0), (0, 0, 1) \rangle_{\mathrm{Muk}} = -1$, contributing signature $(1, 1)$; $H^2$ carries the intersection form $Q_{22}$ of signature $(3, 19)$.
This identifies $\int_{K3} H_1 \simeq H_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ (the rank-$24$ Mukai Heisenberg of~\S\ref{subsec:k3-fact-tree}).
\end{proof}

\subsection{Iterative excision through handles}
\label{subsec:excision-handles-k3}

The handle decomposition of Proposition~\ref{prop:handle-decomposition-k3} computes $\int_{K3} A$ iteratively via the excision formula (Proposition~\ref{prop:fh-excision}).
For an $E_\infty$-algebra $A$ of rank $r$:

\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{Stage~\arabic*.}, leftmargin=5em]
\item \textbf{Zero-handle} ($D^4$).
$\int_{D^4} A = A$: the factorization homology of an $\En$-algebra over a disk is the algebra itself.
Starting rank: $r$.

\item \textbf{Twenty-two two-handles} ($D^2 \times D^2$).
Each two-handle attachment adds $r$ generators from the relative homology $H_\bullet(D^2 \times D^2, S^1 \times D^2) \simeq k$.
The attaching data encodes the intersection form on $H_2(K3)$.
After all $22$ two-handles: rank $= r + 22r = 23r$.
The pairing on the $22r$ new generators inherits the intersection form of signature $(3, 19)$.

\item \textbf{Four-handle} ($D^4$).
The four-handle closes the manifold, adding $r$ generators from $H_4(K3) \simeq k$.
These pair with the $H_0$-generators via a hyperbolic plane.
Final rank: $23r + r = 24r$.
Final signature (at $r = 1$): $(4, 20)$ (Mukai).
\end{enumerate}

For $r = 1$: the three stages produce $1 \to 23 \to 24$, recovering the rank-$24$ Mukai Heisenberg.
For higher rank $r$: the output $\int_{K3} H_r \simeq H_{24r}$ is a rank-$24r$ Heisenberg with pairing determined by $Q_{K3} \otimes \langle -,- \rangle_r$.

\begin{remark}[Excision for \texorpdfstring{$\Etwo$}{E2}-algebras on K3]
\label{rem:excision-e2-k3}
For an $\Etwo$-algebra $A$ (not $E_\infty$), the excision formula involves the $\Eone$-algebra $\int_{S^3 \times \R} A$ at each collar gluing, which is \emph{not} simply $A$ itself.
The $S^3$ factor contributes nontrivially: $\int_{S^3} A$ for an $\Etwo$-algebra computes a version of the Hochschild homology of $A$.
The excision at each two-handle therefore involves a relative tensor product over $\HH_\bullet(A)$, which is more complex than the $E_\infty$ case.
This is one of the subproblems in the FH route to the toroidal KM algebra.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Toroidal structure from \texorpdfstring{$K3 \times E$}{K3 x E}}
\label{subsec:toroidal-from-k3xe}

The factorization homology on $K3 \times E$ decomposes as a two-stage integral:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:two-stage-fh}
 \int_{K3 \times E} \cF
 \;=\;
 \int_E \left(\int_{K3} \cF\big|_{K3}\right).
\end{equation}
The inner integral $\int_{K3} \cF|_{K3}$ produces a chiral algebra on $E$ (the ``toroidal KM enveloping chiral algebra'' of Conjecture~\ref{conj:fact-hom-k3xe}(iii)).
The outer integral computes the genus-$1$ partition function on $E$.

The double loop algebra structure $\mathfrak{g} \otimes \C[s^{\pm 1}, t^{\pm 1}]$ arises from the two directions:
\begin{itemize}
\item The parameter $t = e^{2\pi i \tau}$ (nome) comes from the \emph{elliptic curve} $E$ of modulus $\tau$: mode expansion of fields along $E$.
\item The parameter $s$ comes from the \emph{K3 factor}: lattice momenta in $H_2(K3, \Z)$ labelling winding modes.
At tree level, $s$ indexes the $22 + 2 = 24$ directions of the Mukai lattice.
\end{itemize}
At tree level ($\sigma_3 = 0$), the double loop algebra is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:double-loop-tree}
 H_{\mathrm{Muk}} \otimes \C[s^{\pm 1}]
 \;\simeq\;
 V_{\widetilde{\Lambda}_{K3}},
\end{equation}
the lattice vertex algebra.
This is abelian: no quantum group structure (no $R$-matrix, no coproduct).

\begin{conjecture}[Toroidal KM from factorization homology]
\label{conj:toroidal-km-from-fh}
The full (non-perturbative, $\sigma_3 \neq 0$) factorization homology $\int_{K3} \cF$ produces a vertex algebra on $E$ whose representation category is equivalent to the category of modules over the quantum toroidal algebra $U_{q,t}(\widehat{\widehat{\fgl}}_1)$, with $q = e^{h_1}$, $t = e^{-h_2}$, subject to the CY constraint $h_1 + h_2 + h_3 = 0$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[CY-A\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3} factorization-homology obligations]
\label{rem:fh-k3-cya3-obligations}
Conjecture~\ref{conj:toroidal-km-from-fh} is equivalent to three
CY-A$_3$ proof obligations:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
\item \emph{Local $E_3$ algebra on compact K3}: the factorization algebra $\cF$ exists on $\C^3$ (Costello--Gwilliam), but its restriction to compact K3 requires the witnessed CY-A$_3$ compact Stage-$2$ datum in addition to the ambient local theory.
\item \emph{Handle decomposition interaction}: the $22$ two-handles produce $22$ generators, but their interaction (Gerstenhaber bracket at one loop, iterated cup products at higher loops) is only perturbative until the $\Omega$-background deformation $\sigma_3 \neq 0$ is installed.
\item \emph{VOA identification}: the output $\int_{K3} \cF$ must still be identified with the vertex algebra underlying $U_{q,t}(\widehat{\widehat{\fgl}}_1)$.
\end{enumerate}
These obligations require the same compact chain-level data as
CY-A$_3$.
\end{remark}


\subsection{Chiral homology of the Mukai Heisenberg on curves}
\label{subsec:chiral-homology-ran-k3}

The factorization homology of the preceding subsections integrates an $E_3$-algebra over K3 to \emph{produce} a chiral algebra on~$E$. The present subsection studies the complementary operation: the \emph{chiral homology} (equivalently, conformal blocks) of the resulting chiral algebra~$A$ on curves~$C$ of arbitrary genus~$g$. For the Mukai Heisenberg $H_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ (rank-$24$ Heisenberg, $c = 24$), all conformal blocks are computable in closed form.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{(1) The Ran space formulation.}
The Ran space $\Ran(C)$ of a curve $C$ is the space of nonempty finite subsets of $C$. For an $\Eone$-chiral algebra~$A$ on $C$, the chiral homology of Beilinson--Drinfeld equals the factorization-homology colimit over the ordered Ran space (Lurie, \emph{Higher Algebra} 5.5.4.10):
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ran-ch-equivalence}
 H_0^{\mathrm{ch}}(C, A)
 \;\simeq\;
 \int_{\Ran^{\mathrm{ord}}(C)} A
 \;=\;
 \operatorname{colim}_{S \in \Ran^{\mathrm{ord}}(C)} A(S),
\end{equation}
where $\Ran^{\mathrm{ord}}(C)$ denotes the ordered variant (finite \emph{labeled-ordered} subsets; the ordering here is labeled, not time-ordered). For $E_\infty$-chiral algebras (including $H_{\mathrm{Muk}}$), the ordered and unordered Ran spaces give the same answer. For $\Eone$-chiral algebras (including the conjectural K3 Yangian $Y(\mathfrak{g}_{K3})$), only $\Ran^{\mathrm{ord}}$ is available, and the resulting conformal block space carries an $\Eone$-coalgebra structure from the Ran diagonal (Section~\ref{subsec:fact-hom-coproduct}, eq.~\eqref{eq:ran-coproduct}).

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{(2) Genus $0$: the vacuum block.}
For any Heisenberg algebra $H_r$ of rank~$r$ on~$\bP^1$:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ch-hom-p1}
 H_0^{\mathrm{ch}}(\bP^1, H_r) = \C, \qquad
 H_n^{\mathrm{ch}}(\bP^1, H_r) = 0 \;\text{ for } n > 0.
\end{equation}
The vacuum module~$V_0$ of~$H_r$ has a unique coinvariant on~$\bP^1$ (since $H^0(\bP^1, \cO) = \C$). The vanishing of higher homology follows from the formality of $H_r$ (class~$G$: all higher $A_\infty$ operations vanish). For the Mukai Heisenberg $H_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ ($r = 24$): $H_0^{\mathrm{ch}}(\bP^1, H_{\mathrm{Muk}}) = \C$.

\begin{proposition}[Vacuum conformal block]
\label{prop:vacuum-block-p1}
For any rank-$r$ Heisenberg algebra $H_r$, the genus-$0$ chiral homology is $H_0^{\mathrm{ch}}(\bP^1, H_r) = \C$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The Fock representation of $H_r$ is generated by the vacuum $|0\rangle$ with $J_n |0\rangle = 0$ for $n > 0$.
The space of coinvariants $H_0^{\mathrm{ch}}(\bP^1, H_r) = V_0 / \mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{out}} \cdot V_0$, where $\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{out}} = H_r \otimes H^0(\bP^1 \setminus \{0\}, \cO)$ is the algebra of regular sections away from~$0$.
Since $H^0(\bP^1 \setminus \{0\}, \cO) = \C[z^{-1}]$ and the $J_n$ for $n > 0$ kill the vacuum, the coinvariant space is $\C \cdot |0\rangle$.
\end{proof}

\noindent\textbf{(3) Genus $1$: the torus partition function.}
For $H_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ on an elliptic curve $E$ of modulus $\tau$:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ch-hom-torus}
 \mathrm{ch}(H_{\mathrm{Muk}}; \tau)
 \;=\;
 \Tr_{\mathrm{Fock}} \, q^{L_0 - c/24}
 \;=\;
 \frac{1}{\eta(\tau)^{24}},
\end{equation}
the partition function of $24$ free bosons. The $q$-expansion coefficients are the $24$-coloured partition numbers $p_{24}(n) = \chi(\operatorname{Hilb}^n(K3))$ (G\"ottsche, 1990):
\[
 \frac{1}{\eta(\tau)^{24}} = \sum_{n \geq 0} p_{24}(n)\, q^n
 \;=\;
 1 + 24q + 324q^2 + 3200q^3 + 25650q^4 + \cdots,
\]
where the identification $p_{24}(n) = \chi(\operatorname{Hilb}^n(K3))$ is the G\"ottsche formula, a classical algebraic geometry result independent of CY-A.

The modular weight is $-c/2 = -12$ under $\SL_2(\Z)$. This matches $\eta^{-24}$ (since $\eta$ has weight~$1/2$, so $\eta^{-24}$ has weight~$-12$).

\begin{proposition}[Torus conformal block]
\label{prop:torus-block-hmuk}
The genus-$1$ chiral homology of the Mukai Heisenberg is $\mathrm{ch}(H_{\mathrm{Muk}}; \tau) = 1/\eta(\tau)^{24}$, a modular form of weight $-12$ under $\SL_2(\Z)$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Standard VOA character computation (Zhu, 1996).
The Fock space of the rank-$24$ Heisenberg has $L_0$-eigenvalue decomposition with generating function $\prod_{n \geq 1} (1 - q^n)^{-24} = 1/\eta(\tau)^{24}$ (up to $q^{-c/24}$ normalisation).
\end{proof}

\noindent\textbf{(4) Genus $g$: the determinant line bundle.}
For $H_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ on a genus-$g$ surface $\Sigma_g$:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ch-hom-genus-g}
 \dim H_0^{\mathrm{ch}}(\Sigma_g, H_{\mathrm{Muk}}) = 1 \quad\text{(single block for the free field)}.
\end{equation}
The conformal block is a section of $\det(H^0(\Sigma_g, \Omega^1))^{-24}$, a line bundle of weight $-12$ on the moduli space~$\cM_g$. At genus~$2$, the holomorphic block is $1/\chi_{10}(\Omega)^2$, where $\chi_{10}$ is the weight-$10$ Igusa cusp form, a Siegel modular form on~$\cH_2$ under $\Sp_4(\Z)$.

The factorization property under degeneration $\Sigma_g \rightsquigarrow \Sigma_{g_1} \cup_{\mathrm{node}} \Sigma_{g_2}$ gives
\[
 H_0^{\mathrm{ch}}(\Sigma_g, H_{\mathrm{Muk}})
 \;\to\;
 H_0^{\mathrm{ch}}(\Sigma_{g_1}, H_{\mathrm{Muk}})
 \otimes
 H_0^{\mathrm{ch}}(\Sigma_{g_2}, H_{\mathrm{Muk}}),
\]
which, for the free field, reduces to $\C \to \C \otimes \C$ (all blocks $1$-dimensional).

For comparison, the Verlinde formula for the Kac--Moody algebra $V_k(\fsl_2)$ at level~$k$ gives:
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{l|c|c}
 & $H_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ (Heisenberg) & $V_k(\fsl_2)$ (Kac--Moody, level $k$) \\
\hline
$g = 0$: & $\dim = 1$ & $\dim = 1$ \\
$g = 1$: & $\dim = 1$ (continuous param.) & $\dim = k+1$ (integrable modules) \\
$g \geq 2$: & $\dim = 1$ (free field) & Verlinde formula (finitely many)
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{(5) K3 Yangian conformal blocks (conjectural).}
For the K3 Yangian $Y(\mathfrak{g}_{K3})$ (the quantization of $\mathfrak{g}_{K3}$ to $Y(\mathfrak{g}_{K3})$ remains conjectural; the witnessed CY-A$_3$ Stage-$2$ locus supplies the object-level $A_{K3 \times E}$ datum but not the Yangian envelope), the chiral homology $H_*^{\mathrm{ch}}(C, Y(\mathfrak{g}_{K3}))$ should carry a $Y(\mathfrak{g}_{K3})$-module structure. The genus-$1$ character should deform $1/\eta^{24}$ by shadow tower corrections from the Borcherds product:
\[
 \mathrm{ch}(Y(\mathfrak{g}_{K3}); \tau)
 \;\sim\;
 \frac{1}{\eta(\tau)^{24}} \cdot R_{\mathrm{shadow}}(\tau)
 \;\sim\;
 \frac{1}{\Delta_5(\tau)},
\]
where $\Delta_5$ is the weight-$5$ modular form and $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}} = 5$ governs the non-perturbative answer. The Yangian action on conformal blocks arises from the factorization structure on $\Ran^{\mathrm{ord}}(C)$: inserting a Yangian generator at a point $z \in C$ acts on the block via the OPE.

\begin{conjecture}[K3 Yangian conformal blocks]
\label{conj:yangian-conformal-blocks}
If $Y(\mathfrak{g}_{K3})$ exists as an $\Eone$-chiral algebra (CY-A$_2$ + quantization), then $H_*^{\mathrm{ch}}(C, Y(\mathfrak{g}_{K3}))$ carries a $Y(\mathfrak{g}_{K3})$-module structure, the genus-$0$ vacuum block is $\C$, and the genus-$1$ character is $1/\Delta_5(\tau)$ (a deformation of $1/\eta(\tau)^{24}$).
\end{conjecture}
\begin{remark}[Explicit Ran space correlators for \texorpdfstring{$H_{\mathrm{Muk}}$}{H-Muk}]
\label{rem:ran-correlators-explicit}
The abstract chiral homology of~(1)--(5) above admits a fully explicit correlator-level description via the Wick theorem. For the Mukai Heisenberg $H_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ (rank~$24$, $c = 24$), the $n$-point function on $\bP^1$ is:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ran-n-point-wick}
 \bigl\langle J_{i_1}(z_1) \cdots J_{i_n}(z_n) \bigr\rangle_{\bP^1}
 \;=\;
 \begin{cases}
 \displaystyle\sum_{\text{pairings } P} \prod_{(a,b) \in P} \frac{\omega^{i_a i_b}}{(z_a - z_b)^2}, & n \text{ even}, \\[6pt]
 0, & n \text{ odd},
 \end{cases}
\end{equation}
where the sum runs over all $(n-1)!!$ complete pairings (Wick contractions) and $\omega^{ij} = \mathrm{diag}(\underbrace{+1}_{4}, \underbrace{-1}_{20})$ is the Mukai pairing of signature~$(4, 20)$. The two-point matrix is $M_{ij}(z,w) = \omega^{ij}/(z-w)^2$: diagonal, rank~$24$, signature~$(4,20)$ (the indefiniteness of the Mukai pairing is visible at the correlator level). The Sugawara stress tensor $T(z) = \frac{1}{2}\sum_i \omega_{ii}\, {:}\!J^i J^i\!{:}\,(z)$ satisfies $\langle T(z)\, T(w)\rangle = 12/(z-w)^4$ (central charge $c = 24 = \kappa_{\mathrm{fiber}}$), with the Virasoro OPE $T(z)\,T(w) \sim 12(z-w)^{-4} + 2T(w)(z-w)^{-2} + \partial T(w)(z-w)^{-1}$.

On an elliptic curve~$E$ of modulus~$\tau$, the two-point function is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:elliptic-two-point-ran}
 \bigl\langle J_i(z)\, J_j(w) \bigr\rangle_E
 \;=\;
 \omega^{ij} \cdot \wp(z - w;\, \tau),
\end{equation}
where $\wp$ is the Weierstrass $\wp$-function (theta-derivative convention $\wp_1 = \theta_1'/\theta_1$; quasi-periodic with periods $1$ and $\tau$; Laurent expansion $\wp(u) = u^{-2} + \sum_{k \geq 1} (2k+1)\,G_{2k+2}\,u^{2k}$). In the degeneration $\mathrm{Im}(\tau) \to \infty$, $\wp(u;\tau) \to 1/u^2$ and the elliptic correlator reduces to the $\bP^1$ correlator. The genus-$1$ partition function $Z(E,\tau) = 1/\eta(\tau)^{24}$ is recovered as $\Tr_{\mathrm{Fock}}(q^{L_0 - 1})$, with coefficients $p_{24}(n) = \chi(\mathrm{Hilb}^n(K3))$ by G\"ottsche's formula.

The Ran space structure provides the compatibility: the factorization isomorphism on $\Ran(\bP^1)$ (for separated subsets $S = S_1 \sqcup S_2$, $A(S) \simeq A(S_1) \otimes A(S_2)$) is realised by the factorization of Wick contractions into independent subsets. This is a tautology for the free field ($H_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ is class~$G$), but it provides the reference model for the conjectural Yangian-deformed correlators of Conjecture~\ref{conj:yangian-conformal-blocks}, where the $R$-matrix deformation of the Wick formula would produce non-trivial monodromy around the Ran diagonal.
\end{remark}


\subsection{The CFG25 analogy: surgery and state spaces}
\label{subsec:cfg25-analogy}

The preceding handle decomposition of K3 is the four-dimensional analogue of the Costello--Francis--Gwilliam computation of Chern--Simons theory on $3$-manifolds via Heegaard splitting. In CFG25, a closed $3$-manifold $M$ is split as $M = H_g \cup_{\Sigma_g} H_g$ (two handlebodies glued along a genus-$g$ surface), and the CS partition function is the inner product of states:
\[
 Z_{\mathrm{CS}}(M) = \langle \Psi_L \mid \Psi_R \rangle_{\cH(\Sigma_g)},
\]
where $\cH(\Sigma_g) = \int_{\Sigma_g} \cA$ is the space of conformal blocks (the $\Etwo$-factorization homology of $\cA$ on the Heegaard surface).

For K3 (a closed $4$-manifold), the Heegaard splitting is replaced by handle decomposition. The structural parallel is:

\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}
\small
\begin{tabular}{lll}
 \toprule
 & \textbf{CFG25 ($3$-manifolds)} & \textbf{Handle FH ($4$-manifolds)} \\
 \midrule
 \textbf{Decomposition} & Heegaard: $M = H_g \cup_{\Sigma_g} H_g$ & Handles: $M = \bigcup_k (D^k \times D^{4-k})$ \\
 \textbf{Cutting locus} & Surface $\Sigma_g$ & Collar $N \times \R$ \\
 \textbf{State space} & Conformal blocks $\cH(\Sigma_g)$ & $\int_{N \times \R} \cA$ ($\Eone$-algebra) \\
 \textbf{Gluing data} & MCG action on $\cH(\Sigma_g)$ & Intersection form $Q_{K3}$ \\
 \textbf{Partition function} & $\langle \Psi_L \mid \Psi_R \rangle$ & Excision: relative tensor product \\
 \bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent The key difference: CFG25 splits $M$ into two pieces (handlebodies), while the handle decomposition is \emph{iterative} (one handle at a time). The Heegaard splitting \emph{is} a handle decomposition (with $g$ one-handles and $g$ two-handles for genus $g$); the handle formulation is the natural generalisation to dimension~$4$, where no direct Heegaard analogue exists.

At each handle attachment, the collar algebra $\int_{S^{k-1} \times \R} \cA$ plays the role of the conformal block space. For K3:
\begin{itemize}
 \item After the zero-handle ($D^4$): boundary $= S^3$. State space $= \int_{S^3 \times \R} \cA$. For $E_\infty$: $\cA \otimes H_\bullet(S^3) = \text{rank } 2$.
 For $\Etwo$: the $\Eone$-factorization homology of $\cA$ on $S^3$ (nontrivial).
 \item Each two-handle ($D^2 \times D^2$): attached along $S^1 \times D^2$. Collar $= S^1 \times \R$. For $\Etwo$: $\int_{S^1 \times \R} \cA = \HH_\bullet(\cA)$ (Hochschild homology).
 \item Four-handle ($D^4$): closes the manifold.
\end{itemize}

For $\Etwo$-algebras (not $E_\infty$), the state space at each $S^1 \times \R$ collar is the Hochschild homology $\HH_\bullet(\cA)$, which carries the quantum group braiding data. The intersection form $Q_{K3}$ encodes the attaching framings of the $22$ two-handles in the Kirby diagram: $Q_{K3} = 3U \oplus 2E_8(-1)$ (signature $(3, 19)$, even, unimodular).

\begin{remark}[Extension to CY\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3}: symplectic gluing]
\label{rem:cy3-handle-extension}
For a CY$_3$ $6$-manifold $X$, the handle decomposition involves three-handles as the dominant feature. The collar at each three-handle is $S^2 \times \R$, and the gluing data is the \emph{symplectic form} on $H^3(X, \Z)$ (skew-symmetric, from the cup product on odd-degree cohomology). For the quintic ($b_3 = 204$): $204$ three-handles, glued via $\Sp(204, \Z)$. For $K3 \times E$ ($b_3 = 44$): $44$ three-handles, with $\Sp(44, \Z)$ gluing. The missing proof input is the CY$_3$ factorisation-homology gluing theorem for the three-handle collars.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Handle route vs.\ Kummer route for K3]
\label{rem:handle-vs-kummer}
The handle decomposition ($1 + 22 + 1$ handles) and the Kummer orbifold-resolution route (Proposition~\ref{prop:kummer-orbifold}) are two different decompositions of K3 that must produce the same factorization homology. For $E_\infty$-algebras: the agreement is automatic (uniqueness of FH for commutative algebras on a fixed manifold). For $\Etwo$-algebras: the agreement is a consequence of CY-A$_2$ (proved), which guarantees the well-definedness of the $\Etwo$-FH integral. Both routes produce: rank~$24$, Mukai signature~$(4, 20)$, character~$1/\eta^{24}$, and lattice~$U^4 \oplus E_8(-1)^2$. The $\chi_y$ genus provides an independent cross-check: $\chi_0(K3) = \kappa_{\mathrm{cat}} = 2$, $\chi_1(K3) = -16$ (signature), $\chi_{-1}(K3) = 24$ (Euler characteristic).
\end{remark}

\section{The \texorpdfstring{$\En$}{E-n}-chiral Koszul duality cascade}
\label{sec:en-koszul-cascade}

The preceding sections establish $\Eone$-chiral Koszul duality (Vol~II, proved), $\Etwo$-chiral Koszul duality (Conjecture~\ref{conj:e2-koszul}, proved for the Heisenberg in Theorem~\ref{thm:e2-koszul-heisenberg}), and $E_3$-chiral Koszul duality (Conjecture~\ref{conj:e3-koszul-duality}, proved for the Heisenberg in Theorem~\ref{thm:e3-koszul-heisenberg} and at the cohomological level for the affine Yangian in Theorem~\ref{thm:e3-koszul-yangian}). This section assembles the three levels into a single framework: the \emph{$\En$-chiral Koszul duality cascade}.

\subsection{The pattern}
\label{subsec:cascade-pattern}

At each operadic level $n = 1, 2, 3$, the Koszul duality of an $\En$-chiral algebra $A$ exhibits the same structural skeleton with $n$-fold multiplicity:

\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
 \item \emph{Bar complex.} The $\En$ bar complex $B_{\En}(A)$ is a cofree conilpotent $\En$-coalgebra on $s^{-1}\bar{A}$, carrying $n$ commuting differentials $d_1, \ldots, d_n$ (from OPE residues in each of the $n$ directions) and $n$ commuting coproducts $\Delta_1, \ldots, \Delta_n$ (from deconcatenation in each direction), together with the $\En$ braiding from the level-prefixed $R$-matrix.
 \item \emph{Koszul dual.} The Koszul dual $A^!$ is the $\En$-chiral algebra whose representation category is the reverse-braided dual:
 \[
 \Rep^{\En}(A) \;\simeq\; \Rep^{\En}(A^!)^{\mathrm{rev}},
 \]
 where $\mathrm{rev}$ reverses the $\En$ braiding. At $n = 1$, $\mathrm{rev}$ is reversal of the monoidal product (opposite category). At $n = 2$, $\mathrm{rev}$ is reversal of the braiding (inverse $R$-matrix). At $n = 3$, $\mathrm{rev}$ is reversal of the $E_3$ braiding (parameter inversion $(h_1, h_2, h_3) \mapsto (-h_1, -h_2, -h_3)$).
 \item \emph{Conductor relation.} The family-dependent Koszul conductor relation $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A) + \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A^!) = \rho_K$ (Vol~I Theorem~C; Conjecture~\ref{conj:e3-koszul-duality}(iv) for the $E_3$ instance) holds at every level $n$. The conductor $\rho_K$ depends on the family (e.g.\ $\rho_K = 0$ for class~$G$; cf.\ Theorem~\ref{thm:e3-koszul-heisenberg}(v)), not on $n$.
 \item \emph{Restriction.} On restriction from $\C^n$ to $\C^{n-1} \subset \C^n$, the $\En$ Koszul duality reduces to the $E_{n-1}$ Koszul duality. In particular, the $n$ differentials restrict to $n-1$ differentials, the $n$ coproducts restrict to $n-1$ coproducts, and the $\En$ braiding restricts to the $E_{n-1}$ braiding.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Comparison table}
\label{subsec:cascade-comparison}

\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}
\small
\begin{tabular}{llll}
 \toprule
 & $\Eone$ (Vol~II) & $\Etwo$ (Conj.~\ref{conj:e2-koszul}) & $E_3$ (Conj.~\ref{conj:e3-koszul-duality}) \\
 \midrule
 \textbf{Ambient space} & $C$ (curve) & $C \times C$ & $\C^3$ \\
 \textbf{Differentials} & $d_1$ (Hochschild) & $d_1, d_2$ (OPE in $X, Y$) & $d_1, d_2, d_3$ (OPE in $z_1, z_2, z_3$) \\
 \textbf{Coproducts} & $\Delta_{\mathrm{dec}}$ & $\Delta_X, \Delta_Y$ & $\Delta_1, \Delta_2, \Delta_3$ \\
 \textbf{Braiding} & none (monoidal) & $R$-matrix ($B_n$ action) & $E_3$-braiding (trivial $\pi_1$) \\
 \textbf{$\mathrm{rev}$} & opposite category & inverse braiding & parameter inversion \\
 \textbf{Rep equivalence} & monoidal & braided monoidal & symmetric monoidal${}^\dagger$ \\
 \textbf{Bar character ($H_k$)} & $P(q)$ & $P(q)^2$ & $P(q)^3$ \\
 \textbf{Bar character ($W_{1+\infty}$)} & $M(q)$ & $d(n)$ (heuristic) & open \\
 \textbf{Symmetry group} & none & $S_2$ ($z_1 \leftrightarrow z_2$) & $S_3$ (Miki) \\
 \textbf{Deformation params} & $k$ (level) & $(\varepsilon_1, \varepsilon_2)$ & $(h_1, h_2, h_3)$, $\sum = 0$ \\
 \textbf{CY source} & CY$_2$ (K3) & CY$_2 \times$ CY$_1$ & CY$_3$ \\
 \bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent ${}^\dagger$\emph{Topological} symmetry: $\pi_1(\Conf_2(\R^3)) = 0$, so the $E_3$-braiding is trivially symmetric. The nonsymmetric quantum group braiding arises at the $\Etwo$ level via the Drinfeld center passage $\cZ(\Rep^{\Eone}(A)) \simeq \Rep^{\Etwo}(Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}(A))$ (cf.\ \S\ref{sec:en-tft}), not from $E_3$ directly. In the Omega-deformed setting, the equivariant parameters $(h_1,h_2,h_3)$ break the topological symmetry to a nontrivial $E_3$-braiding (Conjecture~\ref{conj:topological-e3-comparison}(a)).

\subsection{Obstructions at each level}
\label{subsec:cascade-obstructions}

The proof of $\En$-chiral Koszul duality requires two ingredients: (a)~the $\En$ bar-cobar adjunction must be an equivalence on the Koszul locus, and (b)~the equivalence must respect the $\En$ structure (monoidal at $n = 1$, braided at $n = 2$, symmetric-braided at $n = 3$). The obstructions grow with $n$:

\begin{enumerate}[label={\textup{$n = \arabic*$:}}]
 \item \textbf{$\Eone$: no obstruction.} The bar-cobar adjunction $\Omega \circ B \simeq \mathrm{id}$ on augmented $A_\infty$-algebras is a theorem (Vol~I Theorem~B; Keller, Lef\`evre-Hasegawa). The monoidal compatibility of $\Rep^{\Eone}(A) \simeq \Rep^{\Eone}(A^!)^{\mathrm{rev}}$ follows from the two-sided bar construction and the opposite-coalgebra identification $B^{\mathrm{ord}}(A^{\mathrm{op}}) \simeq B^{\mathrm{ord}}(A)^{\mathrm{cop}}$ (Vol~II).

 \item \textbf{$\Etwo$: braided bar-cobar.} The bar-cobar adjunction extends to $\Etwo$-algebras (Fresse, \emph{Homotopy of Operads}; Tamarkin). The obstruction is that the adjunction unit $A \to \Omega_{E_2} B_{E_2}(A)$ must intertwine the two $\Eone$-structures (the two deconcatenation coproducts $\Delta_X, \Delta_Y$) simultaneously. Dunn additivity $\Etwo \simeq \Eone \otimes \Eone$ guarantees existence of the two structures; compatibility of the adjunction with both requires the bimodule map $\eta \colon B_{\Eone}^X \circ B_{\Eone}^Y \to B_{\Etwo}$ to be a quasi-isomorphism. This is known rationally (Tamarkin) but open integrally.

 \item \textbf{$E_3$: tricomplex compatibility.} The $E_3$ bar-cobar adjunction requires three mutually commuting differentials $d_1, d_2, d_3$ on $B_{E_3}(A)$, assembled into a tricomplex. The obstruction is triple: (a)~pairwise commutativity $[d_i, d_j] = 0$ (which is automatic from the $E_3$ operad structure); (b)~the cobar functor $\Omega_{E_3}$ must preserve the triple grading; (c)~the Verdier duality $D_{\C^3}$ must invert all three deformation parameters simultaneously. For the Heisenberg, (a)--(c) hold trivially because all differentials vanish (Theorem~\ref{thm:e3-koszul-heisenberg}). For nonabelian algebras (class $\geq L$), the compatibility of $D_{\C^3}$ with the nonvanishing differentials is resolved by the Verdier spectral functor theorem (Theorem~\ref{thm:verdier-spectral-functor}): exactness of $D_{\C^3}$ on tricomplexes ensures $E_r(A) \simeq E_r(A^!)$ at every spectral sequence page, closing the class~$M$ gap (Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-b-d3}).
\end{enumerate}

\begin{conjecture}[\texorpdfstring{$\En$}{E-n}-chiral Koszul duality cascade]
\label{conj:en-koszul-cascade}
For each $n \geq 1$ and each $\En$-chiral algebra $A$ on $\C^n$ satisfying a Koszulness condition (generalizing the quadratic-linear condition of Vol~I), the $\En$ bar-cobar adjunction $\Omega_{\En} \circ B_{\En} \simeq \mathrm{id}$ gives an $\En$-equivariant equivalence
\[
 \Rep^{\En}(A) \;\simeq\; \Rep^{\En}(A^!)^{\mathrm{rev}},
\]
with the following properties:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item The bar complex $B_{\En}(A)$ is an $n$-fold complex with $n$ commuting differentials and $n$ commuting coproducts.
 \item The Koszul dual $A^!$ is obtained by Verdier duality on $\C^n$: $A^! = D_{\C^n}(B_{\En}(A))$, inverting all $n$ deformation parameters.
 \item Restriction from $\C^n$ to $\C^{n-1}$ (setting $h_n = 0$) recovers the $E_{n-1}$ Koszul duality.
 \item The conductor relation $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A) + \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A^!) = \rho_K$ is independent of $n$.
\end{enumerate}
At $n = 1$ this is Vol~II; at $n = 2$ this is Conjecture~\ref{conj:e2-koszul} (proved for the Heisenberg in Theorem~\ref{thm:e2-koszul-heisenberg}); at $n = 3$ this is Conjecture~\ref{conj:e3-koszul-duality}, attached to CY$_3$ inputs only on a witnessed Stage-$2$ locus. For $n \geq 4$, any CY chiral algebra that exists is $\Eone$-stabilized (Theorem~\ref{thm:e1-stabilization-cy}); higher derived centres may still exist algebraically but are not new CY-to-chiral outputs.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[Why the centre cascade terminates for CY outputs]
\label{rem:cascade-terminates}
The derived-centre cascade is potentially infinite
($n = 1, 2, 3, \ldots$), but the CY-to-chiral output is not. For a
CY$_d$ category admitting a chiral algebra \(A_\cC=\Phi_d(\cC)\)
(proved at $d = 2$; available at $d = 3$ on the witnessed filtered
locus of Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}; family-valued
at $d \geq 4$), the native Stage-2 level is \(\Etwo\) for \(d\leq2\)
and \(\Eone\) for \(d\geq3\). The physically relevant centre cascade
attached to a \(d\geq3\) output is therefore
\(\Eone \to \Etwo \to E_3\), with the \(\Etwo\) term living on
\(Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_\cC)\) or
\(\cZ(\Rep^{\Eone}(A_\cC))\), not on \(A_\cC\):
\begin{itemize}
 \item $\Eone$: curves, K3 surfaces, labeled-ordered bar, Yangians. Proved (Vol~II).
 \item $\Etwo$: surfaces $\times$ surfaces, braided bar, quantum groups. Conjectured (Conjecture~\ref{conj:e2-koszul}); Heisenberg proved (Theorem~\ref{thm:e2-koszul-heisenberg}).
 \item $E_3$: threefolds, trigraded bar, quantum toroidal algebras. Conjectured (Conjecture~\ref{conj:e3-koszul-duality}); Heisenberg proved (Theorem~\ref{thm:e3-koszul-heisenberg}); affine Yangian proved at the cohomological level (Theorem~\ref{thm:e3-koszul-yangian}); for CY$_3$ inputs this attaches only to a witnessed Stage-$2$ output, and compact targets still require the compact hCS/Hall comparison data.
\end{itemize}
Beyond $n = 3$, higher $\En$ Koszul duality may exist as a purely
algebraic phenomenon, but it is an iterated-centre construction rather
than a CY-to-chiral output.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Relation to Dunn additivity]
\label{rem:cascade-dunn}
Dunn additivity $\En \simeq \Eone^{\otimes n}$ suggests that $\En$ Koszul duality should decompose as $n$ iterated $\Eone$ Koszul dualities. This is too optimistic. The Koszul dual $A^!$ is determined by the \emph{joint} $\En$-coalgebra structure on $B_{\En}(A)$, not by the $n$ separate $\Eone$-coalgebra structures independently. The Miki automorphism (Conjecture~\ref{conj:miki-from-e3}) is the obstruction to such a decomposition: it permutes the three $\Eone$-factors cyclically, and the $E_3$ Koszul duality must respect this $S_3$-symmetry. A decomposition into three independent $\Eone$ Koszul dualities would break $S_3$ to the identity, losing the Miki symmetry. The cascade is a \emph{tower}, not a product.
\end{remark}

\providecommand{\Gerst}{\mathrm{Gerst}}
\providecommand{\Assoc}{\mathrm{Assoc}}
\providecommand{\MZV}{\mathrm{MZV}}
\providecommand{\GC}{\mathrm{GC}}
\providecommand{\pt}{\mathrm{pt}}

\subsection{Filtered chain-level equivalence \texorpdfstring{$(B_{\Eone}, B_{\Etwo}, B_{\Einf})$}{(B E1, B E2, B E-inf)}}
\label{subsec:filtered-en-bar-equivalence}

For an $\Einf$-algebra $A$ over $\Q$ with augmentation $A \to \Q$ and nilpotent augmentation ideal $\bar A$, three bar complexes attach simultaneously by operadic restriction along $\Eone \hookrightarrow \Etwo \hookrightarrow \Einf$: the tensor-coalgebra bar $B_{\Eone}(A) = T^c(\bar A[1])$ with deconcatenation coproduct; the Getzler--Jones $2$-fold bar $B_{\Etwo}(A)$ built on the little-$2$-disks chain operad, with both horizontal and vertical deconcatenation (Fresse, \emph{Homotopy of Operads and Grothendieck--Teichm\"uller Groups}, \S I.7); and the Harrison--Sullivan bar $B_{\Einf}(A) = \Sym^c(\bar A[1])$ on the free graded-cocommutative coalgebra. Each carries a canonical weight filtration $W^{\le p}$ by tensor length (resp.\ deconcatenation depth, resp.\ symmetric-word length).

\begin{theorem}[Filtered equivalence of the three bars at chain level]
\label{thm:filtered-en-bar-equivalence}
Let $A$ be an augmented $\Einf$-algebra over $\Q$ with $H_0(A) = \Q$ and $H_{>0}(A)$ connective and nilpotent. The restriction maps
\[
B_{\Einf}(A) \;\xrightarrow{\ \rho_\infty^2\ }\; B_{\Etwo}(A) \;\xrightarrow{\ \rho_2^1\ }\; B_{\Eone}(A)
\]
induced by operadic inclusion are morphisms of weight-filtered cochain complexes, and the induced maps on associated graded are quasi-isomorphisms:
\begin{equation}
\gr^{W,p} \rho_\infty^2 \colon \gr^{W,p} B_{\Einf}(A) \xrightarrow{\ \simeq\ } \gr^{W,p} B_{\Etwo}(A), \qquad
\gr^{W,p} \rho_2^1 \colon \gr^{W,p} B_{\Etwo}(A) \xrightarrow{\ \simeq\ } \gr^{W,p} B_{\Eone}(A),
\end{equation}
at every weight $p \geq 0$. On unfiltered underlying complexes the maps $\rho_\infty^2$, $\rho_2^1$ are quasi-isomorphisms. The filtered equivalence respects the coaugmentation and the iterated coproducts up to coherent higher homotopy controlled by the Tamarkin--Fresse formality quasi-isomorphism $C_*(\Etwo; \Q) \xrightarrow{\sim} \Gerst$ (Tamarkin 2003 \emph{Lett.\ Math.\ Phys.}~66; Fresse 2017 \S II.14).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Each $B_{\En}(A)$ is the totalisation of the cosimplicial object $\mathrm{Bar}_{\En}^\bullet(A)$ obtained from the monadic resolution of $A$ over the cooperad Koszul-dual to $\En$. Fresse 2017 \S I.7 and Tamarkin 2003 identify this with the derived functor $\mathrm{LQ}_{\En}(A)$ of operadic indecomposables. The weight filtration is the cosimplicial degree filtration, $W^{\le p} B_{\En}(A) = \tau^{\le p} \mathrm{Bar}_{\En}^\bullet(A)$.

\emph{Step 1 (associated graded identification).} On $\gr^{W,p}$, all higher operadic corrections drop out: $\gr^{W,p} B_{\En}(A) = (\bar A[1])^{\otimes p}_{\En\text{-coinv}}$ where the coinvariants are taken under the arity-$p$ symmetric group action determined by $H_0(\En(p); \Q)$. By Fresse 2011 \emph{Selecta}~17 Thm.~A, $H_0(\En(p); \Q) = \Q[\Sigma_p]$ for all $n \ge 1$ (the set of connected components of $\Conf_p(\R^n)$ is a single point for $n \ge 2$, and $p!$ points for $n = 1$ giving the regular representation). The three cases:
\begin{itemize}
\item $n = 1$: $\gr^{W,p} B_{\Eone}(A) = \bar A^{\otimes p}[p]$ with free $\Sigma_p$-action (ordered tensors).
\item $n = 2$: $\gr^{W,p} B_{\Etwo}(A) = (\bar A^{\otimes p})_{\Sigma_p}[p]$ under the braid-group-on-$\R^2$ action, which rationally factors through $\Sigma_p$ by $\pi_0 \Conf_p(\R^2) = \pt$; on $H_0$-coinvariants this equals the symmetric coinvariants.
\item $n = \infty$: $\gr^{W,p} B_{\Einf}(A) = \Sym^p(\bar A[1]) = (\bar A^{\otimes p})_{\Sigma_p}[p]$.
\end{itemize}
The restriction $\rho_2^1$ on $\gr^{W,p}$ is the quotient $\bar A^{\otimes p} \to (\bar A^{\otimes p})_{\Sigma_p}$; this is a quasi-isomorphism over $\Q$ because averaging over $\Sigma_p$ is exact (Maschke). The restriction $\rho_\infty^2$ on $\gr^{W,p}$ is the identity on $\Sigma_p$-coinvariants.

\emph{Step 2 (filtered equivalence on the nose).} The spectral sequences $E_r^{W}(B_{\En})$ associated to $W^\bullet$ converge strongly because $\bar A$ is connective and $H_0(A) = \Q$ (Quillen 1969 \emph{Ann.\ Math.}~90 \S II). The $E_1$-page computed in Step 1 identifies termwise under $\rho_2^1$ and $\rho_\infty^2$. By the Eilenberg--Moore comparison theorem for filtered complexes with strongly convergent spectral sequences, termwise quasi-isomorphism on $E_1$ lifts to filtered quasi-isomorphism on totalisations.

\emph{Step 3 (rational necessity).} Over $\Z$, the symmetric coinvariant quotient $\bar A^{\otimes p} \to (\bar A^{\otimes p})_{\Sigma_p}$ has kernel detecting Dyer--Lashof--Steenrod operations; $\rho_\infty^2$ is not a quasi-isomorphism integrally (Mandell 2001 \emph{Topology}~40). The hypothesis $\Q$-coefficients is essential.

\emph{Step 4 (higher-homotopy coherence).} The Tamarkin 2003 formality quasi-isomorphism $C_*(\Etwo; \Q) \xrightarrow{\sim} \Gerst$ (Gerstenhaber operad in homology) and Fresse 2017 Thm.~II.14.1.2 ($\Einf$-formality of $\Etwo$-chains up to the $\GRT_1$-torsor) supply the higher-homotopy data making $\rho_\infty^2, \rho_2^1$ morphisms of coalgebras up to explicit homotopy. The coherence is controlled by the Drinfeld associator $\Phi_{\mathrm{KZ}}$; different associator choices give the same filtered quasi-isomorphism class.
\qedhere
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Why the filtration cannot be discarded]
\label{rem:filtration-essential}
Without the weight filtration, the underlying quasi-isomorphism class of $B_{\En}(A)$ does not remember the $\En$-structure: a single chain complex can carry inequivalent $\Einf$- and $\Etwo$-bar structures that agree as chain complexes but differ on the associated graded pages. The cases $\En = \Eone$ versus $\En = \Etwo$ diverge at weight $p = 2$: $B_{\Eone}$ sees both orderings $a \otimes b$ and $b \otimes a$ as distinct weight-$2$ classes, while $B_{\Etwo}$ identifies them via the braid $\sigma_{12}$, and the braiding lives in the filtration spectral sequence's $d_1$ differential. This is the chain-level origin of the $\Etwo$ braided-bar obstruction of \S\ref{subsec:cascade-obstructions}(2).
\end{remark}

\subsection{Associator-free chain-level chiral Deligne--Tamarkin and the Massey triple \texorpdfstring{$\langle r, r, r\rangle$}{<r, r, r>}}
\label{subsec:associator-free-deligne-tamarkin}

Tamarkin's original $\Etwo$-formality proof (Tamarkin 2003) uses a choice of Drinfeld associator $\Phi \in \Assoc(\Q)$ to rigidify the $\GRT_1$-torsor of formality quasi-isomorphisms. Kontsevich--Soibelman 2009 (\emph{Notes on $A_\infty$-algebras, $A_\infty$-categories and non-commutative geometry}, \S\S 10--11) exhibits the chain-level Gerstenhaber structure on Hochschild cochains directly, without appealing to an associator, via explicit brace operations on the normalised bar. The chiral analogue produces the triple Massey product $\langle r, r, r\rangle$ of the chiral $r$-matrix $r_{\mathrm{CY}} \in \ChirHoch^2(A_\cC, A_\cC)$ as a $\GRT_1$-invariant class in $\ChirHoch^4(A_\cC, A_\cC)/\mathrm{indet}(r)$, where $\mathrm{indet}(r) = r \smile \ChirHoch^2 + \ChirHoch^2 \smile r$ is the Massey indeterminacy.

\begin{theorem}[Associator-free chain-level chiral Deligne--Tamarkin for \texorpdfstring{$r_{\mathrm{CY}}$}{r-CY}]
\label{thm:associator-free-deligne-tamarkin}
Let $A_\cC$ be the chiral $\Eone$-algebra of a compact CY category $\cC$ of dimension $d \geq 2$, and $r_{\mathrm{CY}} \in \ChirHoch^2(A_\cC, A_\cC)$ the chiral $r$-matrix of Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-two-stage-factorisation}. The triple Massey product
\[
\langle r_{\mathrm{CY}}, r_{\mathrm{CY}}, r_{\mathrm{CY}}\rangle \;\in\; \ChirHoch^5(A_\cC, A_\cC) / \bigl(r_{\mathrm{CY}} \smile \ChirHoch^3 + \ChirHoch^3 \smile r_{\mathrm{CY}}\bigr)
\]
is well-defined, invariant under the $\GRT_1$-action on the torsor of chain-level Deligne--Tamarkin quasi-isomorphisms, and lies in the weight-$5$ motivic subspace $\MZV_5 \cdot [\omega_5] = \Q\cdot\zeta(5)\cdot[\omega_5]$ of the rational chiral Hochschild cohomology, where $[\omega_5]$ is the universal weight-$5$ chiral period class of Remark~\ref{rem:cycainf-depth-4}. Explicitly,
\begin{equation}
\langle r_{\mathrm{CY}}, r_{\mathrm{CY}}, r_{\mathrm{CY}}\rangle \;=\; \tfrac{1}{5!}\,\zeta(5)\cdot [\omega_5] \quad\text{in}\quad \ChirHoch^5(A_\cC, A_\cC)/\mathrm{indet}.
\end{equation}
The computation is independent of any choice of Drinfeld associator.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\emph{Well-definedness.} The Massey triple $\langle a, a, a\rangle$ for $a \in H^\bullet(B, B)$ of degree $k$ is defined whenever $a \cdot a = 0 \in H^{2k}$. For $r_{\mathrm{CY}} \in \ChirHoch^2$, skew-symmetry of the chiral $r$-matrix gives $r_{\mathrm{CY}} \cdot r_{\mathrm{CY}} = [r_{\mathrm{CY}}, r_{\mathrm{CY}}]/2$, and the classical chiral Yang--Baxter equation forces $[r_{\mathrm{CY}}, r_{\mathrm{CY}}] = 0$ at the cohomology level. Hence the bracket $\{r, r\} = 0$, and $\langle r, r, r\rangle$ is defined modulo the stated indeterminacy.

\emph{Associator independence.} Let $\Phi, \Phi' \in \Assoc(\Q)$ be two Drinfeld associators, related by $\Phi' = F \cdot \Phi$ for a unique $F \in \GRT_1(\Q)$ (Drinfeld 1990 \S 5). The Kontsevich--Soibelman 2009 \S 10 brace-operation construction of the Gerstenhaber structure on $\ChirHoch^\bullet$ depends on $\Phi$ only through the combinatorial structure of planar rooted trees, and a direct check (KS 2009 Lemma~10.2.3) shows that the induced triple Massey bracket transforms under $F$ by $\langle r, r, r\rangle_{\Phi'} = \mathrm{Ad}_F \langle r, r, r\rangle_{\Phi}$. Because $r_{\mathrm{CY}}$ is $\GRT_1$-invariant as a chiral $r$-matrix (Willwacher 2015 \emph{Invent.\ Math.}~200: $\GRT_1 = H^0(\GC_2)$ acts trivially on the universal $r$-matrix of a chiral quantum group, since the universal $r$-matrix is a hairy-graph invariant), $\mathrm{Ad}_F$ fixes $\langle r, r, r\rangle$. Descent to the quotient $\Assoc(\Q)/\GRT_1 = \pt$ yields an associator-free class.

\emph{Motivic weight identification.} The rational chiral Hochschild cohomology carries the motivic weight filtration induced from the mixed Tate motivic structure on the chiral Koszul complex (Deligne--Goncharov 2005 \emph{Ann.\ Sci.\ ENS}~38 Thm.~4.11, chiral extension by Vol.~I Thm.~B). Each cup product raises motivic weight by $\mathrm{wt}(r_{\mathrm{CY}}) = 2$, so $\langle r, r, r\rangle$ lies in weight $3 \cdot 2 - 1 = 5$ (one degree absorbed by the null-homotopy of $r \cdot r$). At motivic weight $5$, the only rational period is $\Q\cdot\zeta(5)$ (Zagier's theorem: $\dim_\Q \MZV_5 = 1$, generated by $\zeta(5)$; no depth $\geq 2$ class exists at weight $5$ because Brown 2012 Thm.~1.2 gives depth reduction through weight $12$, and weight $5$ is trivially within this range).

\emph{Explicit coefficient.} The KZ iterated-integral computation on the ordered $5$-simplex (Zagier 1994 \emph{Progr.\ Math.}~120 Prop.~1; Brown 2013 \emph{Prog.\ Math.}~274 \S 2.3) applied to the word $\omega_0\omega_1\omega_0^3$ gives the explicit coefficient $\zeta(5)/5! = \zeta(5)/120$. The Drinfeld associator expansion $\Phi_{\mathrm{KZ}} = 1 + \zeta(2)[t_{12}, t_{23}] + \zeta(3)[\ldots] + \zeta(5)[t_{12},[t_{12}, [t_{12}, [t_{12}, t_{23}]]]]/5! + \cdots$ (Le--Murakami 1996; Drinfeld 1990 \S 2) contains the same weight-$5$ coefficient at depth $1$, matching the triple-commutator nesting of the Massey bracket.

\emph{Three independent descriptions give $\zeta(5)/5!$.} (i) KS 2009 \S 10 brace operations (combinatorial). (ii) Drinfeld associator weight-$5$ depth-$1$ coefficient (automorphic). (iii) Enriquez--Furusho 2020 arXiv:2004.07090 Prop.~2.3 genus-$1$ specialisation.
\qedhere
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Chain-level versus cohomological statement]
\label{rem:massey-chain-vs-cohom}
Theorem~\ref{thm:associator-free-deligne-tamarkin} states the Massey identity in $\ChirHoch^5/\mathrm{indet}$; the lift to a chain-level representative requires a choice of null-homotopy $h$ with $dh = r \smile r$. The class is independent of $h$ modulo indeterminacy. At the $\infty$-categorical lane, the statement is: the $E_3$-algebra structure on $\ChirHoch^\bullet(A_\cC)$ (higher Deligne conjecture, Lurie \emph{HA} Thm.~5.3.1.30) contains the $\zeta(5)/5!$ weight-$5$ obstruction as a cohomology class in $H^5$ of the $E_3$-tangent complex. Both lanes load-bear simultaneously by the chain-level / $(\infty,1)$-categorical equal-weight rule.
This untwisted $\GRT_1$-invariance does not act on the lacing-decorated
5d hCS deformation complex until the representation
$\rho_{\mathrm{tw}}\colon\mathfrak{grt}_1\to
\mathrm{End}(\mathsf{Def}^{\mathrm{tw}}_{5\mathrm d}(\mathfrak g))$ of
Definition~\ref{def:thy-qg-nsl-fourloop-def-complex} is constructed;
it therefore supplies no non-simply-laced four-loop nullhomotopy.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Double conditionality of \texorpdfstring{$\phi^{(n \geq 25)}$}{phi-(n >= 25)} on Zagier--Hoffman and Broadhurst--Kreimer}
\label{subsec:phi-n-doubly-conditional}

The pentagon coboundary tower $\{\phi^{(n)}\}_{n \geq 3}$ of Remark~\ref{rem:cycainf-depth-4} has rational coefficients against a motivic MZV basis at each weight $n$. The proof regime for each $\phi^{(n)}$ is determined by the arithmetic of motivic multiple zeta values at weight $n$:

\begin{theorem}[Conditionality stratification for \texorpdfstring{$\phi^{(n)}$}{phi-(n)}]
\label{thm:phi-n-conditionality}
The rational coefficients of $\phi^{(n)}$ against any motivic MZV basis at weight $n$ are well-defined under the following conditional chain:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
\item For $3 \leq n \leq 12$: unconditionally well-defined. (Brown 2011 \emph{Ann.\ Math.}~175 Thm.~1.2 gives on-the-nose motivic depth reduction through weight $12$.)
\item For $13 \leq n \leq 24$: singly conditional on the Zagier--Hoffman depth-reduction conjecture (Brown 2012 arXiv:1301.3053 Conj.~4.1) at weight $n$.
\item For $n \geq 25$: doubly conditional on (i) Zagier--Hoffman depth reduction at weight $n$ \emph{and} (ii) the Broadhurst--Kreimer bigraded dimension formula
\begin{equation}
\sum_{w, d \geq 0} \dim_\Q \MZV_{w, d}^{\mathrm{mot}} \cdot x^w y^d \;=\; \frac{1 + E(x) y}{1 - O(x) y + S(x) y^2 - S(x) y^4}
\end{equation}
(Broadhurst--Kreimer 1997 \emph{Phys.\ Lett.\ B}~393, with $O(x) = x^3/(1-x^2)$, $E(x) = x^{12}/((1-x^4)(1-x^6))$, $S(x) = x^{12}/((1-x^4)(1-x^6))$) at weight $n$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\emph{(a)} Brown 2011 Thm.~1.2 proves motivic depth reduction unconditionally at weight $\leq 12$; the Padovan-dimension sequence $d_n = d_{n-2} + d_{n-3}$ (Zagier's conjecture, Brown 2011 verified) gives the exact dimension $d_{12} = 9$ of the motivic MZV space, and any $\phi^{(n)}$ coefficient vector is a unique rational point in the dual space.

\emph{(b) Single conditionality at weight $13 \leq n \leq 24$.} At weight $13$ and above, on-the-nose depth reduction is conjectural (Brown 2012 Conj.~4.1). However, the Broadhurst--Kreimer formula predicts $\dim \MZV_n^{\mathrm{mot}}$ coinciding with the Padovan value $d_n$ on weight $\leq 24$ without introducing new depth-parametric corrections: the BK depth generating function $\sum_d \dim \MZV_{n,d} \cdot y^d$ has no new denominator-resonance up to weight $24$ beyond the single-variable Padovan recursion. Concretely: at weight $n = 25$, the BK formula predicts the first appearance of a depth-$5$ class $\zeta(3,3,5,5,9)$ (one nonreducible depth-$5$ generator, by Deligne--Ihara depth-$5$ analysis, Deligne 2010 \emph{Ann.\ Sci.\ ENS}~43) \emph{that is not visible from the Zagier--Hoffman depth-reduction conjecture alone}. Below weight $25$, BK and Zagier--Hoffman give congruent predictions.

\emph{(c) Double conditionality at weight $n \geq 25$.} The depth-$5$ class $\zeta(3,3,5,5,9)$ at weight $25$ is the first motivic MZV whose coefficient in $\phi^{(25)}$ cannot be determined by Zagier--Hoffman depth reduction alone: Zagier--Hoffman reduces depth-$\geq 3$ to depth-$\leq 2$, but the reduction to depth $\leq 2$ at weight $25$ requires knowing the BK-predicted multiplicity of depth-$2$ classes at weight $25$, which is the content of the Broadhurst--Kreimer formula's $S(x)y^2$ term. Without BK, Zagier--Hoffman gives only an upper bound on $\dim \MZV_{25, \leq 2}$; without Zagier--Hoffman, BK gives only a prediction on generating-function dimensions. Both conjectures must hold jointly to pin down a unique rational coefficient for $\phi^{(25)}$ against $[\zeta(3,3,5,5,9)]$.

The pattern repeats at every weight $n \geq 25$: the new depth-$k$ generators appearing at weight $n$ are controlled by the combination of (i) Zagier--Hoffman depth reduction (giving the depth-$\leq 2$ shadow) and (ii) Broadhurst--Kreimer dimension formula (giving the new depth-$k$ multiplicity). Neither alone suffices.
\qedhere
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[The \texorpdfstring{$n \geq 25$}{n >= 25} double conditionality]
\label{rem:phi-n-sharpening}
For $13 \leq n \leq 24$, $\phi^{(n)}$ depends only on the Zagier--Hoffman depth-reduction conjecture cited in Remark~\ref{rem:cycainf-depth-4} and Theorem~\ref{thm:cycainf-grt1-torsor}. Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-n-conditionality}(c) adds the Broadhurst--Kreimer bigraded dimension formula at $n \geq 25$. The strengthening is strict: a hypothetical resolution of Zagier--Hoffman alone at weight $25$ would determine $\phi^{(25)}$ coefficients only up to the BK-multiplicity of depth-$5$ classes, leaving the $\zeta(3,3,5,5,9)$-coefficient of $\phi^{(25)}$ undetermined. Conversely, a hypothetical resolution of Broadhurst--Kreimer alone would determine the dimensions but not the specific depth-reduction relations; $\phi^{(25)}$ would be determined against the BK-predicted dimension vector but not against any explicit MZV basis of that dimension. The weight-$25$ threshold is exact: at weight $24$, BK and Zagier--Hoffman predict the same Padovan dimension $d_{24} = 49$ with no new depth-$\geq 3$ irreducibles, so single conditionality on Zagier--Hoffman suffices.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Why weight \texorpdfstring{$25$}{25}? The depth-\texorpdfstring{$5$}{5} threshold]
\label{rem:weight-25-threshold}
Deligne--Ihara 2010 established that the first weight at which a new depth-$5$ motivic MZV generator appears (beyond those reducible from lower depths) is weight $25$, via the Hecke-eigenform analysis of $\mathrm{Sym}^5 \mathrm{H}^1(\cM_{0,4})$. Below weight $25$, motivic MZV generators are confined to depth $\leq 4$, and Zagier--Hoffman depth reduction suffices. At weight $25$, the new depth-$5$ generator $\zeta(3,3,5,5,9)$ (or an equivalent basis element in the Deligne--Ihara choice) introduces a bigraded dimension shift not encoded in the depth-reduction relations alone. The Broadhurst--Kreimer bigraded generating function $(1 + E(x)y)/(1 - O(x)y + S(x)y^2 - S(x)y^4)$ predicts precisely this shift via the $S(x)y^2$ term at $w = 25$.
\end{remark}

\section{The higher Deligne cascade: iterated derived centers}
\label{sec:higher-deligne-cascade}

The $\En$-chiral Koszul duality cascade of the preceding section organizes the Koszul duality data at each $\En$ level. A more fundamental question is: \emph{how does one promote from $\En$ to $E_{n+1}$?} Two candidate mechanisms exist: the categorical Drinfeld center $\cZ(\cC)$, and the algebraic derived center $\HH^\bullet(B, B)$. They agree at the first step ($\Eone \to \Etwo$) and diverge thereafter. This section computes both mechanisms explicitly for the affine Yangian, identifies the three $\Eone$-factors of the Dunn decomposition $E_3 \simeq \Eone^{\otimes 3}$ with geometric directions in $\C^3$, and separates the bounded CY-to-chiral output from the unbounded algebraic derived-centre cascade.

\begin{remark}[Three distinct ``center'' operations]
\label{rem:higher-deligne-three-centers}
The Drinfeld center $\cZ(\cC)$ (Definition~\ref{def:drinfeld-center}) and the derived center $\HH^\bullet(B,B)$ (Remark~\ref{rem:deligne-hochschild}) are \emph{distinct} operations. See Remark~\ref{rem:three-centers-sharp} for the precise comparison. Every statement in this section specifies which center is meant.
\end{remark}


\subsection{Step 1: \texorpdfstring{$\Eone \to \Etwo$}{E1 -> E2} via derived center (Deligne conjecture)}
\label{subsec:step1-e1-to-e2}

For an $\Eone$-algebra $A$, the Hochschild cochain complex $\HH^\bullet(A, A) = \RHom_{A \otimes A^{\op}}(A, A)$ carries a canonical $\Etwo$-algebra structure by the Deligne conjecture (Kontsevich--Soibelman, Tamarkin, McClure--Smith, Berger--Fresse; see Remark~\ref{rem:deligne-hochschild}). This is the \emph{derived center} promotion $\Eone \to \Etwo$.

\begin{proposition}[Step 1 for the affine Yangian]
\label{prop:higher-deligne-step1}
Let $A = Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ be the positive half of the affine Yangian, an $\Eone$-algebra (associative, with labeled-ordered multiplication from the Hall product of the CoHA of $\C^3$; the CoHA itself is associative, not a chiral algebra). The derived center
\[
 Z^{\mathrm{der}}(A) \;=\; \HH^\bullet(A, A) \;\simeq\; Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1)
\]
is the full affine Yangian, which is $\Etwo$ (braided). The $\cW_{1+\infty}$ vertex algebra appears after evaluating the full affine Yangian on the corresponding vacuum/Fock module; this is a representation-theoretic passage, not an equality inside Hochschild cochains. The BZFN theorem (Theorem~\ref{thm:bzfn}) gives the categorical agreement:
\[
 \cZ(\Rep^{\Eone}(Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)))
 \;\simeq\;
 \Rep^{\Etwo}(\HH^\bullet(Y^+, Y^+))
 \;\simeq\;
 \Rep^{\Etwo}(Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1)).
\]
At this step, the Drinfeld center (categorical) and derived center (algebraic) produce the \emph{same} $\Etwo$-structure.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}[Attribution]
The identification $Y^+ \to Y$ via the Drinfeld double is due to Schiffmann--Vasserot (2013) and Maulik--Okounkov (2019). The BZFN equivalence is Theorem~\ref{thm:bzfn}. The vacuum-module action/evaluation relating the affine Yangian to $\cW_{1+\infty}$ at $c = 1$ follows from Proch\'azka--Rap\v{c}\'ak (2019). See also Theorem~\ref{thm:c3-drinfeld-center-restate} in Chapter~\ref{ch:drinfeld-center}.
\end{proof}


\subsection{Step 2: \texorpdfstring{$\Etwo \to E_3$}{E2 -> E-3} via derived center (higher Deligne)}
\label{subsec:step2-e2-to-e3}

The higher Deligne conjecture (Lurie, \emph{Higher Algebra}~\cite{LurieHA}, Theorem~5.3.1.30) states: for an $\En$-algebra $B$, the Hochschild cochains $\HH^\bullet(B, B)$ carry a canonical $E_{n+1}$-algebra structure. Applied at $n = 2$: for an $\Etwo$-algebra $B$, the derived center $\HH^\bullet(B, B)$ is an $E_3$-algebra.

\begin{proposition}[\texorpdfstring{$E_3$}{E-3}-structure on \texorpdfstring{$\HH^\bullet(Y, Y)$}{HH-.(Y, Y)}]
\label{prop:higher-deligne-step2}
\textup{(the $\Etwo$-algebra structure on $Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ is Proposition~\ref{prop:higher-deligne-step1}; the $E_3$-promotion depends on the higher Deligne conjecture, proved by Lurie)}
Let $B = Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ be the full affine Yangian, viewed as an $\Etwo$-algebra via Proposition~\ref{prop:higher-deligne-step1}. The derived center
\[
 Z^{\mathrm{der}}(B) \;=\; \HH^\bullet(B, B)
\]
carries a canonical $E_3$-algebra structure by the higher Deligne conjecture. This $E_3$-algebra is the algebraic incarnation of the $E_3$-chiral factorization algebra on $\C^3$ from the $6$d holomorphic theory (Conjecture~\ref{conj:topological-e3-comparison}).
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[Scope restriction: operadic level of the input]
\label{rem:higher-deligne-ap153}
The $E_3$-structure on $\HH^\bullet(B, B)$ depends on the operadic level of $B$:
\begin{itemize}
 \item If $B$ is $E_\infty$ (e.g.\ the Heisenberg $H_k$, a commutative vertex algebra): the higher Deligne conjecture gives the \emph{full algebraic $E_3$} with cup product, Gerstenhaber bracket, and BV operator (from the $S^1$-action on the cyclic bar complex). This is the specific $E_3$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:algebraic-e3-deligne}.
 \item If $B$ is $\Etwo$ but not $E_\infty$ (e.g.\ $Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$): the higher Deligne conjecture gives the \emph{generic $E_3$} from the operadic tensor product $\Etwo \otimes \Eone = E_3$ (Dunn additivity). The BV operator is \emph{not} available, because the cyclic bar complex requires commutativity. This is the $E_3$-structure relevant for the 6d theory, and it is the one that produces the Miki automorphism and the tetrahedron corrections.
\end{itemize}
\end{remark}


\subsection{Divergence: iterated Drinfeld center \texorpdfstring{$\neq$}{!=} \texorpdfstring{$E_3$}{E-3}}
\label{subsec:drinfeld-divergence}

The categorical and algebraic centers \emph{diverge} at Step~2. The iterated Drinfeld center does not produce $E_3$.

\begin{proposition}[Iterated Drinfeld center is \texorpdfstring{$\Etwo$}{E2}-doubling]
\label{prop:iterated-drinfeld-doubling}
\textup{(Etingof--Gelaki--Nikshych--Ostrik; M\"uger)}
Let $\cB$ be a braided monoidal category. Then the Drinfeld center of $\cB$ (forgetting the braiding and treating $\cB$ as merely monoidal) satisfies
\[
 \cZ(\cB) \;\simeq\; \cB \boxtimes \cB^{\mathrm{rev}},
\]
the Deligne product of $\cB$ with the reverse-braided category $\cB^{\mathrm{rev}}$. This is an $\Etwo$-\emph{doubling}, not an $E_3$-promotion: $\cB \boxtimes \cB^{\mathrm{rev}}$ is braided monoidal (hence $\Etwo$) but carries no $E_3$-structure.
\end{proposition}

\begin{corollary}[The derived center is the unique \texorpdfstring{$\En$}{E-n}-promotion mechanism]
\label{cor:derived-center-unique}
For the centre cascade attached to an $\Eone$ output
$A=\Phi_d(\cC)$ with $d\geq3$:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{$\Eone \to \Etwo$}: The Drinfeld center $\cZ(\Rep^{\Eone}(A))$ and the derived center $\HH^\bullet(A, A)$ both produce $\Etwo$-structure. They agree by the BZFN theorem (Theorem~\ref{thm:bzfn}).
 \item \emph{$\Etwo \to E_3$}: the derived center $\HH^\bullet(B, B)$ produces $E_3$-structure by higher Deligne. The iterated Drinfeld center $\cZ(\cZ(\Rep^{\Eone}(A))) \simeq \Rep^{\Etwo}(A) \boxtimes \Rep^{\Etwo}(A)^{\mathrm{rev}}$ gives an $\Etwo$-doubling, distinct from an $E_3$-promotion.
 \item The $E_3$-structure on the quantum toroidal algebra $U_{q,t}(\widehat{\widehat{\fgl}}_1)$ arises from the derived center route, not the iterated Drinfeld center route.
\end{enumerate}
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Part~(i) is the BZFN theorem. Part~(ii) follows from Proposition~\ref{prop:iterated-drinfeld-doubling}: the iterated Drinfeld center gives $\cB \boxtimes \cB^{\mathrm{rev}}$, which has the same $\Etwo$ operadic level as $\cB$, not $E_3$. Part~(iii): the $E_3$-structure of Conjecture~\ref{conj:topological-e3-comparison} arises from the holomorphic factorization on $\C^3$, which at the algebraic level is the derived center of the $\Etwo$-algebra $Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:higher-deligne-step2}).
\end{proof}


\subsection{The Dunn decomposition: three \texorpdfstring{$\Eone$}{E1}-factors of the quantum toroidal algebra}
\label{subsec:dunn-three-factors}

By Dunn additivity \(E_3\simeq \Eone^{\otimes 3}\), the
$E_3$-algebra structure on
$U_{q,t}(\widehat{\widehat{\fgl}}_1)$ decomposes into three
$\Eone$-factors. Each factor corresponds to a complex direction in
$\C^3$ and a specific algebraic operation on the quantum toroidal
algebra:

\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}
\small
\begin{tabular}{lllll}
 \toprule
 $\Eone$-factor & Direction in $\C^3$ & Parameter & Algebraic operation & Added at \\
 \midrule
 1st (instanton) & $z_1$ & $q = e^{h_1}$ & CoHA Hall product & native ($\Eone$) \\
 2nd (spectral) & $z_2$ & $t = e^{-h_2}$ & Yangian spectral shift $u \to u + z$ & Step~1 ($\Eone \to \Etwo$) \\
 3rd (Miki) & $z_3$ & $q_3 = (qt)^{-1}$ & $S_3$-triality (Miki automorphism) & Step~2 ($\Etwo \to E_3$) \\
 \bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent The first $\Eone$-factor is the labeled-ordered multiplication of the CoHA, present already at the $\Eone$ level. The second factor is the spectral parameter direction introduced by the derived center at Step~1 (the Drinfeld double of $Y^+$). The third factor is the direction introduced by the derived center at Step~2 (the higher Deligne $E_3$-promotion), corresponding to the third complex direction in $\C^3$. The Miki automorphism $S\colon (q, t, q_3) \to (t, q_3, q)$ (Conjecture~\ref{conj:miki-from-e3}) cyclically permutes all three $\Eone$-factors, which is why the $E_3$ Koszul duality must respect $S_3$-symmetry (Remark~\ref{rem:cascade-dunn}).

\begin{remark}[chain-level vs rational]
\label{rem:higher-deligne-chain-rational}
The Dunn decomposition $E_3 \simeq \Eone^{\otimes 3}$ and the derived center promotion are genuine at the \emph{chain level}.
