Under Kontsevich formality ($C_*(E_n; \Q) \xrightarrow{\sim} H_*(E_n; \Q)$), the $E_3$-structure collapses to $\Etwo$ rationally: the third $\Eone$-factor becomes trivial after passing to homology. The physical content (the Miki automorphism, the tetrahedron corrections (Theorem~\ref{thm:zte-failure}), the factorization homology on $\C^3$) lives at the chain level and is destroyed by formality.
\end{remark}


\subsection{Cascade termination at \texorpdfstring{$E_3$}{E-3}}
\label{subsec:cascade-termination-proof}

The algebraic derived centre cascade is unbounded: for any $\En$-algebra $C$, the higher Deligne conjecture produces an $E_{n+1}$-algebra $\HH^\bullet(C, C)$. The CY-to-chiral output is bounded by the Stage-2 rule \(d\leq2\Rightarrow \Etwo\), \(d\geq3\Rightarrow \Eone\); the \(\Etwo\) and \(E_3\) terms attached to \(d\geq3\) are centre objects.

\begin{proposition}[Termination of the derived centre cascade for CY outputs]
\label{prop:cascade-termination}
\noindent\emph{Hypotheses.} The obstruction computation is unconditional. Its application to $A_\cC$ is unconditional at $d = 2$; at $d = 3$ it applies on the witnessed filtered locus of Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}; at $d \geq 4$ it assumes CY-A$_d$.
For a CY$_d$ category $\cC$ admitting a chiral algebra $A_\cC = \Phi_d(\cC)$ (the CY-to-chiral correspondence is $d$-indexed, not a unified functor; see \texttt{rem:phi-not-unified-functor} and \texttt{conj:phi-d-functoriality}):
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item The Stage-2 output is $\Etwo$ for \(d\leq2\) and $\Eone$ for \(d\geq3\).
 \item For \(d\geq3\), the first braided object attached to \(A_\cC\) is the derived centre \(Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_\cC)=\HH^\bullet(A_\cC,A_\cC)\), equivalently the Drinfeld centre of \(\Rep^{\Eone}(A_\cC)\) under BZFN hypotheses.
 \item A further higher-Deligne step produces an \(E_3\)-algebra from the \(\Etwo\)-algebra \(Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_\cC)\). The next \(E_4\)-term is an algebraic iterated-centre deformation object; it is not a new value of \(\Phi_d\).
 \item Thus
 \[
  \Phi_d(\cC)\in
  \begin{cases}
   \Etwo\text{-}\mathrm{ChAlg}, & d\leq2,\\
   \Eone\text{-}\mathrm{ChAlg}, & d\geq3,
  \end{cases}
 \]
 and the sequence \(\Eone\to\Etwo\to E_3\) at \(d\geq3\) is a centre cascade on \(\Phi_d(\cC)\), not the native operadic level of \(\Phi_d(\cC)\).
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Part~(i) is Theorem~\ref{thm:e1-stabilization-cy} for \(d\geq3\) and the CY-A$_2$ output theorem for \(d=2\), with the \(d=1\) commutative refinement regarded by restriction as an \(\Etwo\)-chiral algebra.

Part~(ii) is the Deligne/BZFN comparison. The higher Deligne conjecture gives \(\HH^\bullet(A_\cC,A_\cC)\) an \(\Etwo\)-structure for \(A_\cC\) an \(\Eone\)-algebra. BZFN identifies the same braided structure with the Drinfeld centre of the \(\Eone\)-monoidal category \(\Rep^{\Eone}(A_\cC)\), under the dualisability and presentability hypotheses used in Theorem~\ref{thm:bzfn}. Both constructions have \(A_\cC\) as input; neither changes the operadic level of \(A_\cC\).

Part~(iii) applies higher Deligne once more to the \(\Etwo\)-algebra \(Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_\cC)\), producing an \(E_3\)-algebra. A further application to that \(E_3\)-algebra is a legitimate Hochschild operation, but it is an iterated centre of an iterated centre. The definition of \(\Phi_d\) has already ended at the curve-specialised algebra of part~(i), so this \(E_4\)-term is not a CY-to-chiral output.

Part~(iv) is the case distinction:
\begin{itemize}
 \item $d = 1$: $\Phi_1(\cC)$ is $\Etwo$-chiral with an $E_\infty$ refinement in the commutative cases.
 \item $d = 2$: $\Phi_2(\cC)$ is $\Etwo$ (Corollary~\ref{cor:e2-chiral-from-cy2}).
 \item $d = 3$: $\Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2,C)}(\cC)$ is $\Eone$ on the witnessed filtered locus of Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}; native level $n_{\mathrm{native}}(3) = 1$ per Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-platonic}~\ref{phi:U3}. Two derived centre steps give an \(E_3\) centre object.
 \item $d \geq 4$: $\Phi_d(\cC)$ is $\Eone$-stabilized when the CY-A$_d$ host exists. Two derived centre steps may be applied to this \(\Eone\)-algebra, but the resulting \(\Etwo\) and \(E_3\) objects are centre objects.
\end{itemize}
\end{proof}

\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}
\small
\begin{tabular}{llll}
 \toprule
 Step & Mechanism & Drinfeld center & Derived center \\
 \midrule
 $\Eone \to \Etwo$ & half-braidings / Hochschild cochains & BZFN comparison & BZFN comparison \\
 $\Etwo \to E_3$ & double braiding / Hochschild of $\Etwo$ & $\cB \boxtimes \cB^{\mathrm{rev}}$ & $E_3$ by higher Deligne \\
 $E_3 \to E_4$ & iterated centre / Hochschild of $E_3$ & fails & algebraic only; not a $\Phi_d$ output \\
 \bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$E_3$}{E-3} Hochschild cohomology as deformation theory}
\label{subsec:e3-hochschild-deformation-theory}

The CY centre cascade used here terminates at $E_3$. The \emph{deformation
theory} controlled by $\HH^\bullet_{E_3}(A, A)$ is the $6$d holomorphic
analogue of the Costello--Francis--Gwilliam deformation quantisation of
$3$d Chern--Simons.

\begin{conjecture}[\texorpdfstring{$E_3$}{E-3} Hochschild tricomplex and deformation quantisation]
\label{conj:e3-hochschild-deformation}
Let $A$ be a chiral algebra arising from $6$d holomorphic theory on $\C^3$
with $\Omega$-background parameters $(h_1, h_2, h_3)$ satisfying
$h_1 + h_2 + h_3 = 0$. Let $\sigma_2 = h_1 h_2 + h_1 h_3 + h_2 h_3$
and $\sigma_3 = h_1 h_2 h_3$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
 \item \emph{Tricomplex structure.}
 The $E_3$ Hochschild cohomology $\HH^\bullet_{E_3}(A, A)$ admits a
 tricomplex decomposition $C^{p,q,r}(A)$ with three commuting
 differentials $d_1, d_2, d_3$ (one per complex direction on $\C^3$),
 satisfying $d_i^2 = 0$ and $d_i d_j = d_j d_i$. For $A$ with $n$
 generators, the chain-level dimension is
 $\dim C^{p,q,r} = \binom{n}{p}\binom{n}{q}\binom{n}{r}$,
 with total dimension $8^n$.

 \item \emph{Spectral sequence.}
 The tricomplex admits a spectral sequence
 $E_1 \Rightarrow E_2 \Rightarrow E_3 \Rightarrow E_4 \Rightarrow E_\infty
 = \HH^\bullet_{E_3}(A, A)$.
 The degeneration page depends on the shadow class \textup{}:
 \begin{center}
 \small
 \renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.15}
 \begin{tabular}{lccl}
 \toprule
 Shadow class & Degeneration & $\dim \HH^\bullet_{E_3}$ & Poincar\'e polynomial \\
 \midrule
 $G$ \textup{(free field)} & $E_1$ & $8^n$ & $((1+s)(1+t)(1+u))^n$ \\
 $L$ \textup{(rational)} & $E_3$ & $8^n$ & $(1+t)^{3n}$ \\
 $C$ \textup{(convergent)} & $E_3$ & $8^n$ & $(1+t)^{3n}$ \\
 $M$ \textup{(Gevrey-$1$)} & $E_4$ & $6^n$ & $(3t(1+t))^n$ \\
 \bottomrule
 \end{tabular}
 \end{center}
 For class~$M$, the differential $d_4$ is nonvanishing with coefficient
 $\Delta = 8\,\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} \cdot S_4$, where $S_4$ is the quartic
 shadow invariant.

 \item \emph{Maurer--Cartan deformation.}
 The deformation $A \to A[[\epsilon_1, \epsilon_2, \epsilon_3]]$
 is controlled by a Maurer--Cartan element
 $\alpha \in \HH^2_{E_3}(A, A)$ satisfying
 \[
 d\alpha + \tfrac{1}{2}[\alpha, \alpha]_{E_3} = 0,
 \]
 where $[-,-]_{E_3}$ is the degree-$(-1)$ Lie bracket from the $E_4$
 structure on $\HH^\bullet_{E_3}$ \textup{(higher Deligne conjecture,
 Lurie~HA~5.3.1.30)}. The MC element expands as
 $\alpha = \sum_{k \ge 1} \sigma_3^{k-1} \alpha_k$,
 with $\alpha_k$ identified with:
 \begin{itemize}
 \item the shadow invariant $S_k$ \textup{(Vol~I shadow tower)},
 \item the $(k{-}1)$-loop Feynman diagram contribution,
 \item the $A_\infty$ operation $m_k$.
 \end{itemize}
 The MC equation order-by-order reproduces the shadow tower recursion.

 \item \emph{Shadow class controls convergence.}
 Class~$G$: $\alpha = 0$ (no deformation).
 Class~$L$: $\alpha$ is a finite polynomial in~$\sigma_3$.
 Class~$C$: $\alpha$ is a convergent series in~$\sigma_3$.
 Class~$M$: $\alpha$ is Gevrey-$1$ divergent (Borel-summable).
\end{enumerate}
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[The Mukai Heisenberg as trivial deformation]
\label{rem:mukai-heisenberg-e3-trivial-deformation}
For the Mukai Heisenberg $H_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ (rank~$24$, class~$G$):
$\HH^\bullet_{E_3}(H_{\mathrm{Muk}}, H_{\mathrm{Muk}})
= \Sym(H_{\mathrm{Muk}}[-3])$
with $\dim = 8^{24} = 2^{72}$. All three differentials $d_i$ vanish
(abelian input), so the spectral sequence degenerates at $E_1$. The
MC space is a point: no nontrivial deformation exists. The
$\Omega$-background parameters $\epsilon_i = h_i$ are immaterial for
class~$G$ algebras, consistent with the tree-level exactness of free
fields.
\end{remark}

\section{Configuration space integrals on \texorpdfstring{$\C^3$}{C-3} and the perturbative partition function}
\label{sec:config-space-c3}

The perturbative partition function of $6$d holomorphic Chern--Simons on $\C^3$ is a sum over Feynman diagrams whose individual contributions are integrals over configuration spaces $\Conf_n(\C^3) = \{(z_1, \ldots, z_n) \in (\C^3)^n : z_i \neq z_j\}$. The $E_3$ operad organises these integrals via the framed little $3$-disks action. This is the $\C^3$ analogue of the Costello--Francis--Gwilliam perturbative Chern--Simons construction, where the partition function on $\R^3$ is a sum over integrals on $\Conf_n(\R^3)$.

\subsection{Topology of \texorpdfstring{$\Conf_n(\C^3)$}{Conf-n(C-3)}}
\label{subsec:conf-topology-c3}

The configuration space $\Conf_n(\C^d)$ is an open submanifold of $(\C^d)^n = \R^{2dn}$, hence a smooth manifold of real dimension $2dn$. For $d = 3$: $\dim_\R \Conf_n(\C^3) = 6n$.

The diagonal $\Delta_{ij} \subset \C^d \times \C^d$ has real codimension $2d$, and the linking sphere is $S^{2d-1}$. The fundamental group of $\Conf_2(\C^d) \simeq \C^d \times (\C^d \setminus \{0\}) \simeq \C^d \times S^{2d-1} \times \R_{>0}$ is
\[
 \pi_1(\Conf_2(\C^d)) \;=\; \pi_1(S^{2d-1}) \;=\;
 \begin{cases}
 \Z & d = 1 \text{ (braid group, nontrivial braiding),} \\
 0 & d \geq 2 \text{ (trivially braided topologically).}
 \end{cases}
\]
For $d = 3$: the topological braiding is \emph{trivial} ($\pi_1(S^5) = 0$). The nontrivial $E_3$ braiding for the $6$d theory on $\C^3$ arises from the $\Omega$-background deformation $(h_1, h_2, h_3)$, not from the topology of $\Conf_2(\C^3)$ (this is the topological $E_3$ of Conjecture~\ref{conj:topological-e3-comparison}, not the algebraic $E_3$ from the Deligne conjecture).

\begin{proposition}[Cohomology of \texorpdfstring{$\Conf_n(\C^d)$}{Conf-n(C-d)}]
\label{prop:cohomology-conf-n-cd}
The rational cohomology algebra $H^*(\Conf_n(\C^d); \Q)$ has Poincar\'{e} polynomial
\begin{equation}\label{eq:poincare-conf-n}
 P(\Conf_n(\C^d);\, t)
 \;=\;
 \prod_{k=1}^{n-1} (1 + k\, t^{2d-1}).
\end{equation}
The algebra is generated by classes $\omega_{ij} \in H^{2d-1}$ for $1 \leq i < j \leq n$, subject to graded commutativity ($\omega_{ij}^2 = 0$ since $2d - 1$ is odd) and the Arnold relation
\begin{equation}\label{eq:arnold-c3}
 \omega_{ij}\, \omega_{jk} + \omega_{jk}\, \omega_{ki} + \omega_{ki}\, \omega_{ij} = 0
\end{equation}
for all distinct $i, j, k$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}[Attribution]
Cohen~\cite{Cohen76} for $\C^d$ ($d \geq 2$); Arnol'd~\cite{Arnold70} for $d = 1$. The product formula follows from the fibration $\Conf_n(\C^d) \to \Conf_{n-1}(\C^d)$ with fiber $\C^d \setminus \{n-1 \text{ points}\}$, inductively. See also Totaro~\cite{Totaro96}.
\end{proof}

For $d = 3$ specifically:
\begin{itemize}
 \item Generators $\omega_{ij} \in H^5(\Conf_n(\C^3))$ (degree $5$, from the linking sphere $S^5$).
 \item Number of generators: $\binom{n}{2}$; number of Arnold relations: $\binom{n}{3}$.
 \item $\Conf_2(\C^3)$: $P(t) = 1 + t^5$, so $b_0 = b_5 = 1$, all other Betti numbers zero.
 \item $\Conf_3(\C^3)$: $P(t) = (1 + t^5)(1 + 2t^5) = 1 + 3t^5 + 2t^{10}$.
 \item $\sum_k b_k(\Conf_n(\C^3)) = P(1) = n!$.
 \item $\chi(\Conf_n(\C^3)) = P(-1) = 0$ for $n \geq 2$ (since the $k = 1$ factor vanishes).
\end{itemize}

\begin{remark}[Formality]
\label{rem:formality-conf-c3}
The configuration space $\Conf_n(\C^d)$ is \emph{formal} over $\Q$ for all $d \geq 1$: $C^*(\Conf_n(\C^d); \Q) \simeq H^*(\Conf_n(\C^d); \Q)$ as commutative DGAs. For $d = 1$: this is Kontsevich's formality (1999) of the real Fulton--MacPherson compactification. For $d \geq 2$: this follows from the formality of smooth complex algebraic varieties (Deligne 1971, mixed Hodge theory), applied to the complement of the diagonal arrangement; see Kriz~\cite{Kriz94}.

\emph{Scope warning}: the formality is over $\Q$. At the chain level (integral coefficients), the $E_3$ structure is genuine and does \emph{not} collapse to $\Etwo$. The physical content of the $E_3$ factorization homology on $\C^3$ (the Miki automorphism, the Zamolodchikov tetrahedron corrections, the crossing factors in the two-parameter $R$-matrix) lives at the chain level and is destroyed by formality.
\end{remark}


\subsection{Propagators and Feynman rules on \texorpdfstring{$\C^3$}{C-3}}
\label{subsec:propagators-c3}

The holomorphic Chern--Simons propagator on $\C^3$ is determined by the holomorphic volume form $\Omega = dz_1 \wedge dz_2 \wedge dz_3$ and the $\bar{\partial}$-inverse on $\C^3$. For each pair of points $(z_i, z_j)$ in $\Conf_n(\C^3)$ with spectral parameter difference $u = u_i - u_j$:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
 \item \emph{Heisenberg (free field).} $P_{ij} = \omega^{ab}/(z_i - z_j)^2$, where $\omega^{ab}$ is the CY pairing. This produces free-field correlators.
 \item \emph{Yangian (one-loop deformed).} The propagator acquires poles at $u = \pm h_k$ ($k = 1, 2, 3$), with the full kernel given by the rational structure function:
 \begin{equation}\label{eq:propagator-yangian-c3}
 P_{ij}^{\mathrm{Yang}} \;=\; g(u_i - u_j), \qquad
 g(z) = \prod_{k=1}^3 \frac{z - h_k}{z + h_k}.
 \end{equation}
 The spectral parameter $u$ is a \emph{Yangian spectral parameter}, not a worldsheet coordinate.
 \item \emph{Quantum toroidal (two-parameter).} With two spectral parameters $(u, v)$ from the two transverse directions beyond the Wilson line, the propagator is the Zamolodchikov factorisation
 \begin{equation}\label{eq:propagator-toroidal-c3}
 P_{ij}^{\mathrm{tor}} \;=\; g_{\mathrm{ch}}(u_i - u_j, v_i - v_j),
 \qquad g_{\mathrm{ch}}(u, v) = g(u)\, g(v)\, g(u - v).
 \end{equation}
 The three factors encode: direction-$1$ braiding, direction-$2$ braiding, and the crossing interaction (the Miki kernel). The two parameters $(u, v)$ arise from the $\Omega$-background $(\varepsilon_1, \varepsilon_2)$, not from the normal bundle grading directly.
\end{enumerate}


\subsection{Feynman integrals on \texorpdfstring{$\Conf_n(\C^3)$}{Conf-n(C-3)}}
\label{subsec:feynman-conf-c3}

The $n$-point Feynman integral on $\C^3$ is $I_n = \int_{\Conf_n(\C^3)} \prod_{\mathrm{edges}} P_{ij}$, where the product runs over the edges of the Feynman graph. For the abelian gauge algebra $\fgl_1$ (no cubic vertex), the relevant diagrams are:

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Tree diagrams ($\Eone$).} The line graph with edges $(1,2), (2,3), \ldots, (n-1, n)$:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:tree-integral}
 I_n^{\mathrm{tree}} \;=\; \prod_{k=1}^{n-1} g(u_k - u_{k+1}).
\end{equation}
This is the labeled-ordered ($\Eone$) contribution: the product over consecutively ordered pairs. The factorisation into a product of one-parameter structure functions is the content of $\Eone$ coassociativity.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{One-loop diagrams ($\Etwo$).} The cycle graph with edges $(1,2), (2,3), \ldots, (n,1)$:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:loop-integral}
 I_n^{\mathrm{1-loop}} \;=\; \prod_{k=1}^{n} g(u_k - u_{k+1 \bmod n}).
\end{equation}
At $n = 2$: $I_2^{\mathrm{1-loop}} = g(z) \cdot g(-z) = 1$ by the CY inversion identity $g(z)\, g(-z) = 1$ (which follows from $h_1 + h_2 + h_3 = 0$). The triviality of the $n = 2$ loop is the statement that the $S$-matrix at charge~$1$ is trivial (Proposition~\ref{prop:s-matrix-charge1-trivial} in \S\ref{subsec:fact-hom-c3}).

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Two-loop ($E_3$) at $n = 3$.} The complete graph $K_3$ with all pairwise propagators:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:two-loop-integral}
 I_3^{E_3} \;=\; \prod_{1 \leq i < j \leq 3} g(u_i - u_j).
\end{equation}
This is the pairwise-factored version of the $3$-particle $S$-operator. By Theorem~\ref{thm:zte-failure}, the factored operator $S_{ijk} = R_{ij}\, R_{ik}\, R_{jk}$ does \emph{not} satisfy the Zamolodchikov tetrahedron equation: genuine $E_3$ corrections beyond the pairwise product are required.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Two-parameter extension.}
With two spectral parameters, the $3$-particle integral is:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:two-loop-two-param}
 I_3^{E_3, \mathrm{2-param}} \;=\; \prod_{1 \leq i < j \leq 3} g_{\mathrm{ch}}(u_i - u_j,\, v_i - v_j)
 \;=\; \prod_{1 \leq i < j \leq 3} g(u_i - u_j)\, g(v_i - v_j)\, g(u_i - u_j - v_i + v_j).
\end{equation}
This is a product of $9$ rational structure functions, encoding the full $E_3$ factorisation into three $\Eone$-factors (one per complex direction, by Dunn additivity $E_3 \simeq \Eone^{\otimes 3}$).


\subsection{The Fulton--MacPherson compactification of \texorpdfstring{$\Conf_n(\C^3)$}{Conf-n(C-3)}}
\label{subsec:fm-c3}

The Fulton--MacPherson compactification $\mathrm{FM}_n(\C^3)$ is a smooth manifold with corners that compactifies $\Conf_n(\C^3)$. The codimension-$1$ boundary strata are indexed by subsets $S \subseteq \{1, \ldots, n\}$ of size $|S| \geq 2$ (the ``collision screens''), giving $2^n - n - 1$ codimension-$1$ strata. For $n = 3$: four strata (three pairwise collisions $\{12\}, \{13\}, \{23\}$ and one triple collision $\{123\}$).

The cohomology generators $\omega_{ij}$ extend from $\Conf_n(\C^3)$ to $\mathrm{FM}_n(\C^3)$, and their restriction to the boundary strata factorises as products of lower-degree generators. The Feynman integrals~\eqref{eq:tree-integral}--\eqref{eq:two-loop-two-param} are defined on the interior $\Conf_n(\C^3) \subset \mathrm{FM}_n(\C^3)$; their boundary contributions (from the FM strata) encode OPE singularities.


\subsection{Perturbative partition function and the MacMahon function}
\label{subsec:pert-part-fn}

The perturbative partition function of the $6$d theory on $\C^3$ is:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:z-pert-c3}
 Z_{\mathrm{pert}} \;=\; 1 + \sum_{n \geq 2} \frac{g^n}{n!} \, I_n,
\end{equation}
where $g$ is the coupling constant.

At tree level ($\sigma_3 = 0$, the self-dual point), the $E_3$ structure degenerates to $E_\infty$ (Conjecture~\ref{conj:topological-e3-comparison}), and the factorisation homology integral reduces to the MacMahon function~\eqref{eq:equiv-index-hilb-c3}:
\[
 Z_{\mathrm{pert}}\big|_{\sigma_3 = 0} \;=\; M(q)
 \;=\; \prod_{n=1}^{\infty} \frac{1}{(1 - q^n)^n}
 \;=\; 1 + q + 3q^2 + 6q^3 + 13q^4 + 24q^5 + 48q^6 + \cdots,
\]
counting $3$D partitions (plane partitions). At generic $\sigma_3 \neq 0$, the perturbative corrections deform the MacMahon function by the $\Omega$-background, and the full partition function recovers the quantum toroidal Fock space character (conditional on Conjecture~\ref{conj:topological-e3-comparison}).


\subsection{Comparison: \texorpdfstring{$\R^3$}{R-3} (Chern--Simons) vs \texorpdfstring{$\C^3$}{C-3} (holomorphic Chern--Simons)}
\label{subsec:r3-vs-c3}

The $\R^3$ and $\C^3$ constructions share the same operadic framework ($E_3$) but differ in geometric content.

\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}
\small
\begin{tabular}{lll}
 \toprule
 & $\R^3$ (Chern--Simons) & $\C^3$ (holomorphic CS) \\
 \midrule
 Config.\ space dim & $3n$ (real) & $6n$ (real) \\
 Linking sphere & $S^2$ & $S^5$ \\
 Generator degree & $2$ & $5$ \\
 $\pi_1(\Conf_2)$ & $0$ & $0$ \\
 Formality & Kontsevich 1999 & Kriz 1994 \\
 Braiding source & Lie algebra cubic vertex & $\Omega$-background $(h_1, h_2, h_3)$ \\
 Tree-level $Z$ & $1$ (no topology) & $M(q)$ (MacMahon) \\
 ZTE obstruction & nontrivial & nontrivial (Theorem~\ref{thm:zte-failure}) \\
 \bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

The key structural parallel: in both cases, $\pi_1(\Conf_2) = 0$, so the topological braiding is trivial. For $\R^3$ CS, the nontrivial content comes from the Lie algebra structure constants (the cubic vertex). For $\C^3$ hCS, it comes from the $\Omega$-background deformation of the holomorphic factorisation structure (Conjecture~\ref{conj:topological-e3-comparison}(a)). In both cases, the Zamolodchikov tetrahedron equation is \emph{not} automatically satisfied, and the genuine $E_3$ corrections are controlled by higher operations beyond the pairwise product.

\section{Stratified factorization homology and chiral knot invariants}
\label{sec:stratified-fh-chiral}

Factorization homology $\int_M \cF$ for a smooth manifold $M$ and $\En$-chiral factorization algebra $\cF$ is extended to \emph{stratified} manifolds by Ayala--Francis--Tanaka (arXiv:1706.09912): for a manifold $M$ stratified by a closed subspace $S \subset M$, the integral $\int_{M \setminus S} \cF$ produces invariants of the pair $(M, S)$. In Chern--Simons theory, $M = \R^3$, $S = L$ is a framed link, and $\int_{\R^3 \setminus L} \Obs^q$ produces link invariants (colored Jones, HOMFLY-PT). The holomorphic chiral analogue sets $M = \C^3$, $S = C$ a holomorphic curve (an algebraic knot), and produces \emph{chiral knot invariants} $\int_{\C^3 \setminus C} \cF_{\mathrm{ch}}$ from the stage-1 $E_3$-holomorphic factorisation algebra $\PhiFA_3(\cC)$ on $\C^3$.

\subsection{Stratification by holomorphic curves}
\label{subsec:strat-hol-curves}

A holomorphic curve $C \subset \C^3$ induces a stratification of $\C^3$ into strata:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item the \emph{bulk} $\C^3 \setminus C$ (codimension~$0$), to which $\cF_{\mathrm{ch}}$ is assigned as an $E_3$-chiral algebra;
 \item the \emph{defect} $C^{\mathrm{sm}}$ (the smooth locus of $C$, codimension~$2$), to which a module $M_C$ over $\cF_{\mathrm{ch}}$ is assigned (the Wilson line module, \S\ref{subsec:universal-defect});
 \item the \emph{singularity} $C^{\mathrm{sing}}$ (the singular locus, codimension~$3$), to which vertex operators encoding fusion data are assigned.
\end{enumerate}
For a smooth curve ($C^{\mathrm{sing}} = \emptyset$), only strata~(i) and~(ii) appear.

\begin{definition}[Algebraic knot]
\label{def:algebraic-knot}
An \emph{algebraic knot} is an irreducible germ of a holomorphic curve $C \subset \C^2 \subset \C^3$ at the origin. The \emph{link} of $C$ is $L_C = C \cap S^3_\varepsilon$ for small $\varepsilon > 0$, which is a smooth knot in~$S^3$. A \emph{torus knot} $T(p, q)$ with $\gcd(p, q) = 1$ and $p, q \geq 2$ is the algebraic knot defined by
\[
 C_{p,q} \;=\; \{(z_1, z_2) \in \C^2 : z_1^p = z_2^q\},
\]
whose link is the classical torus knot $T(p, q) \subset S^3$.
\end{definition}

The singularity invariants of $C_{p,q}$ enter the chiral knot invariant:
\begin{itemize}
 \item Milnor number $\mu = (p - 1)(q - 1)$ (the dimension of the Jacobian ring).
 \item Milnor fiber genus $g = (p - 1)(q - 1)/2$ (the genus of the fiber of the Milnor fibration $f/|f| \colon S^3_\varepsilon \setminus L_C \to S^1$).
 \item Delta invariant $\delta = (p-1)(q-1)/2$ (the number of gaps of the numerical semigroup $\langle p, q \rangle$).
 \item Newton polygon: the triangle with vertices $(0,0)$, $(p,0)$, $(0,q)$, with $g = (p-1)(q-1)/2$ interior lattice points (Pick's theorem).
\end{itemize}


\subsection{The Wilson line as stratified FH (= coproduct)}
\label{subsec:wilson-line-strat-fh}

The simplest stratification is by a line: $C = C_{\mathrm{line}} \simeq \C \subset \C^3$. The complement $\C^3 \setminus C_{\mathrm{line}} \simeq \C \times (\C^2 \setminus \{0\})$ retracts to the normal circle bundle, and the factorization homology reduces to the defect module data.

\begin{proposition}[Wilson line = Drinfeld coproduct]
\label{prop:wilson-line-coproduct}
For $d=2$, and for $d=3$ after fixing a witnessed
\(\Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2,C)}\) locus with defect-module excision data, the
stratified factorization homology
$\int_{\C^3 \setminus C_{\mathrm{line}}} \cF_{\mathrm{ch}}$ of the
$E_3$-chiral factorization algebra along a Wilson line
$C \subset \C^3$ produces the Drinfeld coproduct:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{Spin~$1$}: the coproduct is primitive, $\Delta_z(J_n) = J_n^L \otimes 1 + 1 \otimes J_n^R$, with no spectral parameter dependence.
 \item \emph{Spin~$2$}: the coproduct $\Delta_z(T_n) = T_n^L + T_n^R + \frac{\Psi - 1}{\Psi} (J \cdot J)_{\mathrm{cross}, n} + z \cdot J_n^R$ encodes the $R$-matrix braiding through the cross-coefficient $(\Psi - 1)/\Psi$.
 \item \emph{$R$-matrix}: the structure function $g(z)$ extracted from the Wilson line braiding satisfies the CY inversion identity $g(z) \cdot g(-z) = 1$ (unitarity of the defect).
\end{enumerate}
The spectral parameter $z$ is a Yangian spectral parameter from the Ran
space factorization structure; worldsheet insertion coordinates belong
to a different datum.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
At $d = 2$, the $\Etwo$-chiral factorization algebra on a curve is the vertex algebra $A_\cC = \Phi_2(\cC)$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-a-d2}. The defect module is the Fock module, and the Ran space colimit produces the multiplicative Drinfeld coproduct $\Delta_z(T(u)) = T^L(u) \cdot T^R(u - z)$. The spin-$2$ mode coproduct is the direct Heisenberg Fock-space computation. The CY inversion $g(z) g(-z) = 1$ follows from the CY condition $h_1 + h_2 + h_3 = 0$ by direct factorization of the rational function.

At $d = 3$, Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3} supplies the framed object-level algebra $A_\cC^{(\Sigma_2,C)}$ on the witnessed filtered locus. Dunn--Lurie additivity decomposes the local $E_3$ input as an $E_1$ Wilson-line direction tensored with the transverse $E_2$ factor, and Ayala--Francis--Tanaka stratified excision identifies the line defect with the $E_1$ defect module when the defect comparison data are fixed. The resulting operation on two ordered Wilson-line insertions is the Drinfeld coproduct on that witnessed defect locus; the transverse $E_2$ factor contributes the $R$-matrix through the Drinfeld-centre recovery, not through a global $\Phi_3$ morphism functor.
\end{proof}


\subsection{Chiral torus knot invariants}
\label{subsec:chiral-torus-knot}
\label{subsec:chiral-torus-knots}

For the torus knot $T(p, q)$, the stratified FH $\int_{\C^3 \setminus C_{p,q}} \cF_{\mathrm{ch}}$ is computed by factorization descent along the Milnor fibration. The Milnor fiber $F_{p,q}$ is a smooth surface of genus $g = (p-1)(q-1)/2$, and the monodromy is a finite-order diffeomorphism $h \colon F_g \to F_g$ with eigenvalues on the unit circle.

\begin{conjecture}[Chiral torus knot invariant]
\label{conj:chiral-knot-invariant}
The stratified factorization homology of the $E_3$-chiral factorization algebra $\cF_{\mathrm{ch}}$ on $\C^3$ along the torus knot $C_{p,q}$ is:
\[
 Z_{p,q}(h_1, h_2, h_3) \;=\; \int_{\C^3 \setminus C_{p,q}} \cF_{\mathrm{ch}} \;=\; \Tr_{\mathrm{Fock}}\!\left(R_{p,q}^{\mathrm{twist}} \cdot q_\tau^{L_0}\right),
\]
where $R_{p,q}^{\mathrm{twist}}$ is the $pq$-fold iterated braiding twisted by the Milnor fiber monodromy. At tree level, the invariant is:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:chiral-knot-tree}
 Z_{p,q}^{\mathrm{tree}}(h_1, h_2, h_3) \;=\; \prod_{(a,b) \in \mathrm{int}(\Delta_{p,q})} g(a h_2 + b h_1),
\end{equation}
where $\mathrm{int}(\Delta_{p,q})$ denotes the interior lattice points of the Newton polygon of $z_1^p - z_2^q$, and $g(z)$ is the rational structure function of the quantum toroidal algebra. The product has $g = (p-1)(q-1)/2$ factors (one per interior point, by Pick's theorem).

The tree-level invariant diverges for every torus knot $T(p,q)$ with $p, q \geq 2$: the interior point $(1,1)$ gives argument $h_1 + h_2 = -h_3$, which is always a pole of $g(z)$. This divergence is the tree-level manifestation of the singularity at the origin and requires regularization by the one-loop correction.

The one-loop correction is controlled by the Milnor fiber genus:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:chiral-knot-one-loop}
 Z_{p,q}^{1\text{-loop}} \;=\; \frac{\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}^g}{\det(h - \mathrm{Id})},
\end{equation}
where $\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}} = h_1 h_2 h_3$ is the Planck constant (= $\sigma_3$, the Yangian deformation parameter), and $\det(h - \mathrm{Id})$ is the determinant of the monodromy operator minus the identity on $H_1(F_g; \C)$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[Tree-level divergence and singularity regularization]
\label{rem:tree-level-divergence}
The universal divergence of $Z_{p,q}^{\mathrm{tree}}$ at the $(1,1)$ interior point is the chiral analogue of the framing anomaly in Chern--Simons theory. In the classical (R\'{e}shetikhin--Turaev) setting, the framing anomaly is absorbed by a choice of framing on the knot. In the holomorphic chiral setting, the regularization is the one-loop correction~\eqref{eq:chiral-knot-one-loop}, which introduces the Milnor fiber genus $g$ and the monodromy eigenvalues as the regularization data. The regularized invariant $Z_{p,q}^{\mathrm{reg}} = \mathrm{Res}_{(1,1)} Z_{p,q}^{\mathrm{tree}} \cdot \prod_{(a,b) \neq (1,1)} g(a h_2 + b h_1)$ is finite and nonzero for generic Omega-background parameters.
\end{remark}


\subsection{The Hopf link and the categorical S-matrix}
\label{subsec:hopf-link-s-matrix}
\label{subsec:e3-s-matrix-charge2}

Two intersecting Wilson lines $C_1, C_2 \subset \C^3$ form the algebraic Hopf link. The Ayala--Francis--Tanaka excision formula gives:
\[
 \int_{\C^3 \setminus (C_1 \cup C_2)} \cF_{\mathrm{ch}} \;=\; \int_{\C^3 \setminus C_1} \cF_{\mathrm{ch}} \;\underset{\int_{S^3_\varepsilon \setminus L_{12}} \cF_{\mathrm{ch}}}{\otimes}\; \int_{\C^3 \setminus C_2} \cF_{\mathrm{ch}},
\]
where $L_{12} = C_1 \cap S^3_\varepsilon$ is the link of $C_1$ near $C_2$, and the tensor product is over the factorization homology of the link complement.

\begin{proposition}[Hopf link = categorical S-matrix]
\label{prop:hopf-link-s-matrix}
\label{prop:s-matrix-charge1-trivial}
The stratified factorization homology of the algebraic Hopf link (two intersecting lines) recovers the categorical S-matrix $S_{V,W} = \Tr_W(R_{VW} \cdot R_{WV})$ of \S\ref{subsec:e3-s-matrix-charge2}:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{Charge~$1$}: $S_{1,1}(u) = g(u) \cdot g(-u) = 1$ by CY inversion. The charge-$1$ linking contribution is trivially~$1$ for all spectral parameters $u$, by analytic continuation.
 \item \emph{Charge~$2$}: $S_{(2)}(u) = g(u + h_2) \cdot g(h_2 - u)$ and $S_{(1^2)}(u) = g(u + h_1) \cdot g(h_1 - u)$, both nontrivial (unlike the Yang $R$-matrix, for which the double braiding is the identity).
 \item The S-matrix is even: $S(u) = S(-u)$, reflecting the unoriented nature of the Hopf link.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Item~(i) is the CY inversion identity of Proposition~\ref{prop:wilson-line-coproduct}(iii). Items~(ii) and~(iii) are direct computations using the diagonal Fock $R$-matrix of the quantum toroidal algebra.
\end{proof}


\subsection{Excision for algebraic links}
\label{subsec:excision-alg-links}

For an algebraic link $C = C_1 \cup \cdots \cup C_r$ (a reducible curve with $r$ irreducible components), the Ayala--Francis--Tanaka excision formula decomposes the stratified FH into:
\[
 \int_{\C^3 \setminus C} \cF_{\mathrm{ch}} \;=\; \bigotimes_{i=1}^{r} Z_{C_i} \;\cdot\; \prod_{i < j} S_{C_i, C_j}^{\mathrm{lk}(C_i, C_j)},
\]
where $Z_{C_i}$ is the individual component invariant and $S_{C_i, C_j}$ is the linking contribution raised to the linking number. At charge~$1$, the linking contribution is~$1$ by CY inversion (Proposition~\ref{prop:hopf-link-s-matrix}(i)), and the excision reduces to the product of component invariants. At higher charge, the linking contribution is the nontrivial categorical S-matrix.

\begin{conjecture}[Chiral-RT identification]
\label{conj:chiral-rt-identification}
The chiral knot invariant $Z_C^{\mathrm{chiral}}(h_1, h_2, h_3)$ of an algebraic knot $C \subset \C^3$ equals the R\'{e}shetikhin--Turaev invariant $\mathrm{RT}_{L_C}(q, t)$ of the topological link $L_C \subset S^3$ colored by representations of the quantum toroidal algebra $U_{q,t}(\widehat{\widehat{\fgl}}_1)$, under the DIM parametrization $q = e^{h_1}$, $t = e^{-h_2}$:
\[
 Z_C^{\mathrm{chiral}}(h_1, h_2, h_3) \;=\; \mathrm{RT}_{L_C}(e^{h_1}, e^{-h_2}).
\]
This identification requires the witnessed CY-A$_3$ Stage-$2$ locus, a compact $E_3$ hCS comparison when compact targets are involved, and the full Reshetikhin--Turaev construction for the quantum toroidal algebra. This is a composite arrow: each individual step is established at lower dimension, but the composite 6d construction is conjectural.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[Connection to DAHA and Hilbert scheme knot homology]
\label{rem:daha-hilb-connection}
The chiral torus knot invariant $Z_{p,q}$ is conjecturally related to:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
 \item The DAHA-Jones polynomial $J_{p,q}(q,t)$ of Morton--Samuelson (arXiv:1709.03006): the tree-level product~\eqref{eq:chiral-knot-tree} is the leading term of the DAHA trace.
 \item The Oblomkov--Rasmussen--Shende invariant from $\mathrm{Hilb}^n(\C^2)$ (arXiv:1712.03676): the Newton polygon interior points correspond to fixed-point contributions in equivariant localization on the Hilbert scheme.
 \item The refined BPS count of Gukov--Stosic: the Milnor fiber genus $g$ enters as the BPS ``spin'' content.
\end{enumerate}
These identifications are all conditional on Conjecture~\ref{conj:chiral-rt-identification}.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Categorified chiral torus knot invariants}
\label{subsec:categorified-chiral-torus-knots}

The chiral torus knot invariant $Z_{p,q}$ of Conjecture~\ref{conj:chiral-knot-invariant} is a \emph{number} (or formal series). The categorification replaces it with a \emph{graded vector space} $\mathrm{CKh}_{p,q}$ whose Euler characteristic recovers $Z_{p,q}^{\mathrm{reg}}$, analogous to the passage from the Jones polynomial to Khovanov homology in $3$d.

\subsubsection{The \texorpdfstring{$T(2,n)$}{T(2,n)} family}

For the two-strand torus knots $T(2, 2k+1)$ with $k \geq 1$, the interior lattice points of the Newton polygon of $z_1^2 - z_2^{2k+1}$ are
\[
 \mathrm{int}(\Delta_{2, 2k+1}) = \{(1, b) : 1 \leq b \leq k\},
\]
so the genus is $g = k = (n-1)/2$ by Pick's theorem, and the tree-level invariant~\eqref{eq:chiral-knot-tree} becomes
\begin{equation}\label{eq:t2n-tree}
 Z_{2,n}^{\mathrm{tree}} = \prod_{b=1}^{g} g(h_2 + b \cdot h_1).
\end{equation}
The universal $(1,1)$-divergence (Remark~\ref{rem:tree-level-divergence}) manifests at $b = 1$: the argument $h_1 + h_2 = -h_3$ (by the CY condition) is always a pole of $g(z)$. The \emph{regularized} invariant replaces this singular factor with the residue:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:t2n-regularized}
 Z_{2,n}^{\mathrm{reg}} = \operatorname{Res}_{z = -h_3} g(z) \;\cdot\; \prod_{b=2}^{g} g(h_2 + b \cdot h_1).
\end{equation}
The residue is $\operatorname{Res}_{z=-h_3} g(z) = \prod_{i=1}^{3}(-h_3 - h_i) \,\big/\, \prod_{i \neq 3}(-h_3 + h_i)$, which is finite and nonzero for generic $\Omega$-background parameters.

\subsubsection{The categorification}

\begin{conjecture}[Categorified chiral torus knot invariant]
\label{conj:categorified-chiral-torus-knot}
For each torus knot $T(2, 2k+1)$ with $k \geq 1$, the regularized chiral invariant $Z_{2,n}^{\mathrm{reg}}$ is the graded Euler characteristic of a bigraded vector space
\[
 \mathrm{CKh}_{2,n} = \bigoplus_{i=0}^{g} \bigoplus_{j \in \Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}} \mathrm{CKh}^{i,j}_{2,n},
\]
where $i$ is the \emph{homological grading} (from the Khovanov-type resolution of the Newton polygon factors) and $j$ is the \emph{Mukai weight grading} (from the K3 Yangian lattice structure). The construction proceeds as follows:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item Each interior lattice point $(1, b)$ contributes a $2$-term chain complex $[k_0^{(b)} \to k_1^{(b)}]$, where $k_0$ (resp.\ $k_1$) is the ``unresolved'' (resp.\ ``resolved'') state at that point.
 \item The total complex is the tensor product over all $g$ interior points:
 \[
 \mathrm{CKh}_{2,n} = \bigotimes_{b=1}^{g} [k_0^{(b)} \to k_1^{(b)}],
 \]
 so the homological graded dimension is $(1+t)^g$\textup{:}
 \[
 \dim \mathrm{CKh}^{i,*}_{2,n} = \binom{g}{i}, \qquad i = 0, 1, \ldots, g.
 \]
 \item The total dimension is $2^g$ and the Euler characteristic vanishes\textup{:} $\chi(\mathrm{CKh}_{2,n}) = (1-1)^g = 0$ for $g \geq 1$.
 \item The Mukai weight grading $j$ records the Mukai lattice contribution from each factor. At the singular point $(1,1)$, the Mukai weight is $\pm 1$ in the $h_3$~direction (from the pole/residue structure). At regular points $(1, b)$ for $b \geq 2$, the Mukai weight records the equivariant content $(b, 1, 0)$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[Explicit examples]
\label{rem:categorified-torus-knot-examples}
For the first three members of the $T(2,n)$ family:
\begin{center}
\small
\begin{tabular}{llllll}
 \toprule
 \textbf{Knot} & $g$ & $\dim \mathrm{CKh}$ & \textbf{Graded dims} & \textbf{Bigraded Betti} & $\chi$ \\
 \midrule
 $T(2,3)$ (trefoil) & $1$ & $2$ & $(1, 1)$ & $b^{0,-1}\!=\!1,\; b^{1,+1}\!=\!1$ & $0$ \\
 $T(2,5)$ (cinquefoil) & $2$ & $4$ & $(1, 2, 1)$ & \text{4 states, 2 Mukai weights} & $0$ \\
 $T(2,7)$ & $3$ & $8$ & $(1, 3, 3, 1)$ & \text{8 states, 4 Mukai weights} & $0$ \\
 \bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The pattern: at each step $n \to n+2$, the categorified dimension doubles, the homological grading adds one row of Pascal's triangle, and the Jones polynomial breadth increases by~$2$.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Jones polynomial limit]
\label{rem:jones-limit}
The Jones polynomial of $T(2, 2k+1)$ has breadth $2k = 2g$. The Poincar\'{e} polynomial $(1+t)^g$ of $\mathrm{CKh}_{2,n}$ has degree $g$, so the bigraded categorification (using both the homological and Mukai gradings) has total breadth $2g$, matching the Jones breadth. In the topological limit $h_i \to 0$, the chiral categorification should reduce to Khovanov homology: $\mathrm{CKh}_{2,n} \to \mathrm{Kh}(T(2,n))$. The two categorifications use different complexes (Newton polygon tensor product vs.\ Kauffman state sum), so the individual Betti numbers differ, but the total dimension and breadth are compatible.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Mukai lattice grading and the K3 Yangian]
\label{rem:mukai-grading-knots}
The Mukai weight grading~$j$ on $\mathrm{CKh}_{2,n}$ is the shadow of the $24$-dimensional Mukai lattice grading from the K3 Yangian $Y(\fg_{K3})$ of~Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-yangian}. In the CY$_3$ reduction to $3$~parameters $h_1, h_2, h_3$, the Mukai weight is a vector in $\Z^3$; for the full K3 Yangian, it lives in the Mukai lattice $\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}} = U^3 \oplus E_8(-1)^2$ of signature $(4, 20)$. The grading is by the Mukai lattice because the structure function $g(z) = \prod_{i=1}^{24}(z - h_i)/(z + h_i)$ factors over the $24$ lattice directions, and the categorified state records the subset of directions that are ``excited'' at each Newton polygon point.
\end{remark}

\section{Chiral homology on stratified real \texorpdfstring{$3$}{3}-manifolds}
\label{sec:chiral-homology-3folds}

The preceding sections develop stratified factorization homology on $\C^3$ (a complex $3$-fold). This section addresses the complementary question: the chiral homology on \emph{real} $3$-manifolds, the natural setting for the CFG25 link invariants and the $S^3$-framing data required for CY-A$_3$.

\subsection{The three real subsectors of \texorpdfstring{$\C^3$}{C-3}}
\label{subsec:three-real-subsectors}

Inside $\C^3 = \R^6$, three distinguished real $3$-dimensional submanifolds carry different sectors of the $6$d holomorphic theory:

\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\arabic*)}]
 \item \textbf{$\R^3 \subset \C^3$ (topological).} The restriction of the $6$d theory to $\R^3 = \{(x_1, x_2, x_3) \in \C^3\}$ is \emph{topological Chern--Simons} (Costello--Gwilliam). The resulting $E_3$-algebra $\mathrm{Obs}^q(\R^3)$ produces the quantum group $U_q(\fg)$ via Koszul duality (Route~A of \S\ref{subsec:qg-from-fh}). Only the symmetric function $\sigma_3 = h_1 h_2 h_3$ of the $\Omega$-background is visible: the topological theory does not distinguish the individual $h_i$. This is the CFG25 topological restriction theorem.

 \item \textbf{$\C \times \R \subset \C^3$ (holomorphic-topological).} The embedding $(z, t) \mapsto (z, t, 0)$ restricts the $6$d theory to the $3$d $\cN = 2$ holomorphic-topological theory of Costello--Gaiotto. The $\R$~direction provides the $\Eone$~ordering; the $\C$~direction provides the chiral structure. This is the natural home of the $\Eone$-chiral bialgebra (\S\ref{sec:e1-chiral-bialgebras} of Chapter~\ref{ch:e1-chiral-algebras}): the spectral coproduct $\Delta_z$ arises from the collision of Wilson lines along the $\R$~direction. Costello's 2013 $5$d-to-boundary construction supplies the proof input.

 \item \textbf{$S^3 \subset \C^3$ (CY$_3$ framing).} The unit sphere $S^3 = \{|z_1|^2 + |z_2|^2 = 1\} \subset \C^2 \subset \C^3$ is the CY$_3$ framing manifold. The restriction of the $6$d theory to $S^3$ should encode the $S^3$-framing data required for CY-A$_3$ (\S\ref{subsec:s3-framing-programme}). The Hopf fibration $S^1 \to S^3 \to S^2$ decomposes this into the $S^2$-framing (CY-A$_2$, proved) and the Connes~$B$-operator (the $S^1$~fibre), with an obstruction from the Hopf Euler class; the missing input is the CY-A$_3$ framing comparison.
\end{enumerate}

\noindent The three subsectors form a hierarchy:
\begin{center}
\small
\begin{tabular}{llll}
 \toprule
 \textbf{Subsector} & \textbf{Theory} & \textbf{$E_n$ level} & \textbf{Parameters visible} \\
 \midrule
 $\R^3$ & Topological CS & $E_3$ (topological) & $\sigma_3$ only \\
 $\C \times \R$ & 3d $\cN=2$ hol-top & $\Eone$-chiral & $h_1$, $h_3$ \\
 $S^3$ & CY$_3$ framing & $E_3$ (chain level) & all $h_i$ \\
 \bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent The distinction between the topological $E_3$ on $\R^3$ and the chain-level $E_3$ on $S^3$ is the algebraic-vs-topological $E_3$ split: formality collapses the chain-level structure to the rational/topological one, but the physical content (Miki automorphism, $S^3$-framing data) lives at the chain level.

\subsection{Factorization homology on \texorpdfstring{$S^3$}{S-3}: the CY-A\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3} framing}
\label{subsec:fh-s3-framing}
\label{subsec:s3-framing-programme}

For an $E_3$-algebra $A$, the factorization homology $\int_{S^3} A$ is the critical computation underlying CY-A$_3$. At the $E_\infty$ level:
\[
 \int_{S^3} A \;=\; A \otimes H_\bullet(S^3; \bk) \;=\; \text{rank } 2,
\]
since $H_\bullet(S^3) = \bk[0] \oplus \bk[3]$ (Betti numbers $1, 0, 0, 1$).

At the $E_3$ chain level, the Hopf fibration $S^1 \hookrightarrow S^3 \twoheadrightarrow S^2$ gives the decomposition of Proposition~\ref{prop:hopf-fibration-decomposition}:
\[
 F^{(3)} \;=\; F^{(2)} \circ B^{(1)} \;+\; \mathrm{Obs},
\]
where $F^{(2)}$ is the $S^2$-framing (CY-A$_2$, proved) and $B^{(1)}$ is the Connes $B$-operator from the $S^1$~fibre. The obstruction $\mathrm{Obs}$ has three components:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
 \item $\mathrm{Obs}_{\mathrm{top}} = 0$ universally ($\pi_3(B\mathrm{Sp}) = 0$).
 \item $\mathrm{Obs}_{A_\infty}$: the raw commutator with the termwise pair-contraction can be nonzero; in a strict non-formal model
 \[
  [m_3,B^{(2)}_{\mathrm{term}}][a|a|a|a|b]=2\alpha[b]\neq0 .
 \]
 The vanishing used in CY-A$_3$ is the corrected Costello identity
 \(\{b,B^{(2)}_{\mathrm{TCFT}}\}=0\) for the total Hochschild differential after the TCFT correction datum is fixed, not a raw per-\(m_3\) cancellation.
 \item $\mathrm{Obs}_{\mathrm{BV}}$: the obstruction to extending the \v{C}ech/Dolbeault homotopy to commute with the BV operator. This is the analytically hard component.
\end{enumerate}

\noindent The $E_\infty$ computation is proved. The $d=3$ object-level assignment $\Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2,C)}$ exists on the witnessed filtered locus of Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}. A complete chain-level compact $S^3$ computation still requires the corrected TCFT comparison and the filtered \(\HH^{-2}_{E_1}\) theorem, or a direct replacement by explicit $E_3$ bar cohomology.

\subsection{Lens spaces and orbifold data}
\label{subsec:lens-space-chiral}

The lens space $L(p,q) = S^3/\Z_p$ is the quotient of $S^3$ by a free $\Z_p$~action. The factorization homology decomposes via the orbifold formula:
\[
 \int_{L(p,q)} A \;=\; \Bigl(\int_{S^3} A\Bigr)^{\Z_p} \;+\; \sum_{k=1}^{p-1} \text{(twisted sectors)}.
\]
Over $\Q$, the $\Z_p$-invariants of $H_\bullet(S^3)$ are $H_\bullet(S^3)$ itself (the action is free; torsion in $H_1(L(p,q); \Z) = \Z/p\Z$ is invisible over $\Q$), so $\int_{L(p,q)} A = \text{rank } 2$ for $E_\infty$-algebras.

For the chiral $E_3$-algebra from $6$d hCS, the $\Z_p$~action produces $p-1$ \emph{twisted sectors}. At the $k$-th sector, the effective quantum group parameter specialises to a $p$-th root of unity: $q_{\mathrm{eff}} = q \cdot e^{2\pi i k/p}$. This connects to the Kazhdan--Lusztig theory.

\begin{remark}[Kummer connection: \texorpdfstring{$L(2,1) = \R\bP^3$}{L(2,1) = RP-3}]
\label{rem:lens-kummer-connection}
The lens space $L(2,1) = \R\bP^3$ is the linking lens space at each $A_1$~singularity of the Kummer surface $\mathrm{Km}(T^4) = T^4/\Z_2$. Each of the $16$ fixed points of $T^4/\Z_2$ contributes one untwisted sector and one twisted sector (conformal weight $h = \tfrac{1}{2}$). The total twisted contribution of $16$ states at $h = \tfrac{1}{2}$ is the origin of the $16 \to 32$ blowup in the Kummer route (Proposition~\ref{prop:kummer-orbifold}, Step~3).
\end{remark}

\subsection{Surgery presentation and excision}
\label{subsec:surgery-excision}

Every closed oriented $3$-manifold $M$ admits a surgery presentation (Lickorish--Wallace): $M$ is obtained from $S^3$ by Dehn surgery on a framed link $L = K_1 \cup \cdots \cup K_r$. The factorization homology decomposes via excision:
\[
 \int_M A \;=\; \int_{S^3 \setminus N(L)} A \;\underset{\int_{T^2 \times \R} A}{\bigotimes^r}\; \int_{S^1 \times D^2} A,
\]
where the tensor product is over the boundary torus algebras $\int_{T^2 \times \R} A$ at each surgery site, and $N(L)$ denotes the tubular neighbourhood.

\begin{center}
\small
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}
\begin{tabular}{lll}
 \toprule
 \textbf{Manifold} & \textbf{Surgery description} & \textbf{Components} \\
 \midrule
 $S^3$ & Empty link (no surgery) & $0$ \\
 $L(p,1)$ & $p$-surgery on unknot & $1$ \\
 $S^2 \times S^1$ & $0$-surgery on unknot & $1$ \\
 Poincar\'e sphere & $+1$-surgery on left trefoil & $1$ \\
 \bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent The surgery linking matrix $L_{ij}$ (framing on the diagonal, linking numbers off-diagonal) encodes the gluing data; its signature is the signature of the bounding $4$-manifold.

\subsection{Product \texorpdfstring{$3$}{3}-manifolds: \texorpdfstring{$\Sigma_g \times S^1 = \mathrm{trace}$}{Sigma-g x S-1 = trace}}
\label{subsec:product-3folds-trace}

For a product $3$-manifold $\Sigma_g \times S^1$, the factorization homology decomposes:
\[
 \int_{\Sigma_g \times S^1} A \;=\; \int_{S^1}\Bigl(\int_{\Sigma_g} A\big|_{\Sigma_g}\Bigr) \;=\; \Tr_{\cH^{\mathrm{ch}}(\Sigma_g, A)}(\mathrm{id}),
\]
where the inner integral is the space of conformal blocks and the outer $S^1$ integration is the trace. For the Mukai Heisenberg $H_{\mathrm{Muk}}$:

\begin{itemize}
 \item \textbf{Genus $0$}: the vacuum block is $1$-dimensional ($\dim \cH^{\mathrm{ch}}(\bP^1, H_{\mathrm{Muk}}) = 1$).
 \item \textbf{Genus $1$}: the block is $1$-dimensional, and the character is
 \[
 \Tr(q^{L_0}) \;=\; \frac{1}{\eta(\tau)^{24}},
 \]
 the generating function of the $24$-coloured partition numbers $p_{24}(n) = \chi(\mathrm{Hilb}^n(K3))$ (G\"ottsche 1990).
 \item \textbf{Genus $g \geq 2$}: the block is $1$-dimensional for $H_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ (abelian: Verlinde number $= 1$ at all genera), so $\Tr(\mathrm{id}) = 1$.
\end{itemize}

\noindent The Betti numbers of $\Sigma_g \times S^1$ are $(1, 2g+1, 2g+1, 1)$ by K\"unneth, giving $\chi = 0$ (as required for all closed oriented $3$-manifolds by Poincar\'e duality).

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$E_3$}{E-3} bar cohomology on \texorpdfstring{$3$}{3}-manifolds by shadow class}
\label{subsec:e3-bar-3folds}

The $E_3$ bar cohomology of a chiral algebra $A$ on a $3$-manifold $M$ depends on both the Heegaard genus $g$ of $M$ and the shadow class of $A$, following the pattern of

\begin{center}
\small
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}
\begin{tabular}{llll}
 \toprule
 \textbf{Shadow class} & \textbf{$H_\bullet(B_{E_3}(A); M)$} & \textbf{$S^3$ ($g = 0$)} & \textbf{$S^2 \times S^1$ ($g = 1$)} \\
 \midrule
 $G$ (formal) & $P(q)^{3g}$ (chain = cohom.) & $P(q)^0 = 1$ & $P(q)^3$ ($\dim = \infty$) \\
 $L$ (depth $2$) & $(1+t)^{3g}$ & $1$ & $1 + 3t + 3t^2 + t^3$ \\
 $C$ (charge cons.) & $(1+t)^{3g}$ & $1$ & $1 + 3t + 3t^2 + t^3$ \\
 $M$ (mock modular) & $(3t(1+t))^g = 6^g$ & $1$ & $3t + 3t^2$ ($\dim = 6$) \\
 \bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent For class~$G$ (formal, all differentials vanish): the bar complex is formal and the cohomology equals the chain complex, $P(q)^{3g}$ (infinite-dimensional; Proposition~\ref{prop:virasoro-d4}(iv)). For classes $L$, $C$: the total dimension is $2^{3g}$ (e.g.\ $2^3 = 8$ for $S^2 \times S^1$; $2^9 = 512$ for $T^3$ with Heegaard genus~$3$). For class~$M$: the $d_4$ differential from the quartic shadow $S_4 \neq 0$ kills $\Lambda^0$ and $\Lambda^3$ at each handle, giving $E_\infty = [0,3,3,0]^{\otimes g}$ with total dimension $6^g$ (Corollary~\ref{cor:class-m-higher-genus}). Thus $(1+t)^{3g}$ is the class-$L/C$ formula, whereas $6^g$ is the class-$M$ cohomological count; class~$G$ remains formal and infinite-dimensional.

\begin{remark}[Systematic 3d/6d obstruction catalogue]
\label{rem:3d-6d-obstruction-catalogue}
The structures of $3$d Chern--Simons theory that do \emph{not} lift to the $6$d holomorphic theory are classified into $8$ obstruction vectors, grouped by type:

\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\arabic*)}]
\item \emph{Parameter obstructions} (finite-dimensionality, unitarity, volume conjecture, rationality): these arise because the $6$d theory has continuous parameters $(h_1, h_2, h_3)$ where $3$d has the discrete level~$k$. They are recovered at special loci (root of unity, Gepner point, NS limit), but no single locus recovers all four simultaneously.
\item \emph{Topological obstructions} (knot complement topology, RT functor): these arise because the complement of a codimension-$2$ submanifold in a $6$-manifold has fundamentally different topology from the complement of a knot in $S^3$ (the boundary is not a torus for curves of genus $g \neq 1$; cf.\ \S\ref{subsec:strat-hol-curves}). These are the deepest obstructions and admit no parameter-space workaround.
\item \emph{Structural obstructions} (surgery formula, categorification): these require new mathematics (the ZTE corrections of Theorem~\ref{thm:zte-failure} for surgery, and the $7$d M-theory lift for categorification) that may or may not exist.
\end{enumerate}

\noindent Quantitatively, the aggregate lift fraction across all $8$ categories is below $30\%$: of $41$ generating structures in $3$d knot theory, quantum topology, and conformal field theory, only $10$ survive the passage to $6$d. The cross-obstruction interaction matrix has approximate block-diagonal structure (the three types interact weakly across blocks but strongly within), and the critical resolution path begins with the finite-dimensionality obstruction, which has the highest synergy with the remaining obstructions, particularly the RT functor.
\end{remark}

\begin{conjecture}[Chiral homology on stratified \texorpdfstring{$3$}{3}-folds]
\label{conj:chiral-homology-3folds}
For the $E_3$-chiral algebra $\cF_{\mathrm{ch}}$ from $6$d holomorphic Chern--Simons on $\C^3$, restricted to a stratified real $3$-manifold $(M^3, L)$ with framed link $L = K_1 \cup \cdots \cup K_r$:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item The stratified chiral homology $\int_{(M, L)}^{\mathrm{ch}} (\cF_{\mathrm{ch}}, \{V_i\})$ is a well-defined element of $\C[\![h_1, h_2, h_3]\!]$.
 \item For $M = S^3$ and $L$ the algebraic link of a plane curve singularity, the invariant equals the chiral knot invariant $Z_C^{\mathrm{chiral}}(h_1, h_2, h_3)$ of~\S\ref{subsec:chiral-torus-knots}.
 \item In the topological limit $h_1 = h_2 = h/2$, $h_3 = -h$ (reducing to one parameter $q = e^h$), the invariant recovers the Reshetikhin--Turaev invariant of $U_q(\widehat{\widehat{\fgl}}_1)$.
 \item For $M = L(p,q)$, the invariant decomposes into $1$ untwisted and $p-1$ twisted sectors, with the twisted sectors encoding root-of-unity specialisations $q_{\mathrm{eff}} = q \cdot e^{2\pi i k/p}$.
 \item For $M = \Sigma_g \times S^1$, the invariant equals the graded trace $\Tr_{\cH^{\mathrm{ch}}(\Sigma_g)}(q^{L_0})$ over the genus-$g$ conformal block space.
\end{enumerate}
This conjecture uses the witnessed CY-A$_3$ Stage-$2$ locus and the excision principle for stratified chiral homology on real $3$-folds inside~$\C^3$. It does not assert a compact $E_3$ hCS algebra without the separate compact comparison data.
\end{conjecture}

\section{The K3 chiral bialgebra as bi-based \texorpdfstring{$E_n$}{E-n} factorisation}
\label{sec:en-fact-K3-bibased}

\begin{remark}[K3 chiral bialgebra as bi-based factorisation]
\label{rem:en-fact-bi-based}
The platonic K3 chiral bialgebra $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ of Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-chiral-bialgebra-platonic} is a bi-based factorisation algebra: on the chiral base $\mathrm{Ran}(E^{\mathrm{nod,sm}}_{24})$ with Beilinson--Drinfeld chiral bracket on the smooth locus of the $24$-node discriminant curve and nearby-cycles $\mathcal{D}$-modules at each node; on the parameter base $\overline{\mathcal{A}_2}$ with Francis--Gaitsgory factorisation $\infty$-category. This is the $d=3$ specialisation of the $E_n$-factorisation framework where $n$ varies with stratum: $E_1$ on the generic smooth locus of the chiral base, $E_2$ on parameter-base $\overline{\mathcal{A}_2}$ via the Drinfeld centre of $\mathrm{Rep}^{E_1}(\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5})$ (per the CY-to-chiral correspondence at $d\geq 3$, Chapter~\ref{ch:cy-to-chiral} (U3)).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[{CY-\texorpdfstring{$2$}{2} \texorpdfstring{$[2]$}{[2]}-shift}]
\label{rem:en-fact-cy2-shift}
For K3 input at $d=2$: the bar-cobar Koszul shift is $[2]$ (matching $\mathrm{CY}_2$), with $\mathrm{HH}^\bullet(D^b\mathrm{Coh}(K3))$ bi-graded. Under $\widetilde{M}_{24}$-equivariance the Serre functor carries a projective Schur cocycle of order $6$ in $H^2(M_{24},\mathrm{U}(1))\cong\mathbb{Z}/12$ (Bridgeland--Maciocia $2001$; Gaberdiel--Hohenegger--Volpato $2012$).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Averaging morphism and Humbert identity]
\label{rem:en-fact-averaging}
The averaging morphism $\mathrm{av}\colon \mathrm{Ran}(\mathbb{P}^1)_{M_{24}}\to\overline{\mathcal{A}_2}$ is the composition $\mathrm{Torelli}_{K3}\circ\mathrm{KugaSatake}\circ j_{\mathrm{Kodaira}}$, linking the chiral Ran-space base to the Siegel parameter base. The order-$8$ monodromy of the Humbert divisor $H_1$ coincides with the Mukai-doubling Koszul conductor $K^{\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}}=2\,c_+(\mathrm{Mukai}(K3))=8$ and the Lusztig $\ell=8$ specialisation via Bruinier $2002$ Proposition~$5.1$. Universal identity $\hbar^2\cdot K^{\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}}=-1$ on the $\mathcal{B}$-family.
\end{remark}

\begin{conjecture}[First-order \texorpdfstring{$E_3$}{E-3}-moduli of 6D \texorpdfstring{$\hCS$}{hCS} on \texorpdfstring{$\C^3$}{C-3} and the \texorpdfstring{$6^g$}{6-g} Class M ceiling]
\label{conj:first-order-moduli-6g-class-m}
For holomorphic Chern--Simons on $\C^3$ with semisimple gauge algebra $\frakg$, the tangent space to the formal moduli problem $\mathrm{Def}(\Obs_{\hCS}) = \HH^\ast_{E_3}(\Obs_{\hCS}, \Obs_{\hCS})[3]$ in cohomological degree zero is
\[
 T_{\Obs_{\hCS}}\mathrm{Def} \;\simeq\; H^{0, 3}_{\bar\partial, c}(\C^3) \otimes \mathrm{Sym}^2(\frakg^\vee)^{\mathrm{inv}} \;\simeq\; \C \cdot \Omega^\vee \otimes \C \cdot \mathrm{Kil} \;\simeq\; \C,
\]
a one-dimensional Killing-form deformation $\epsilon \cdot \int_{\C^3} \Omega \wedge \langle \cA, [\cA, \cA]_\epsilon \rangle$ matching the Costello--Gaiotto Yangian $Y_{\epsilon_1, \epsilon_2, \epsilon_3}(\frakg)$ at the Calabi--Yau slice $\epsilon_1 + \epsilon_2 + \epsilon_3 = 0$. The local Dolbeault calculation has no terms with \(q>3\), so no obstruction appears in that formal local degree range; this is not a compact CY$_3$ closure theorem and not an hCS--Hall comparison. The genus-$g$ Class M $E_3$-bar dimension $6^g$ is the Riemann-surface-factorisation-homology shadow of this one-dimensional $E_3$-deformation direction: Lurie additivity $E_2 \otimes E_1 = E_3$ distributes the moduli along the $g$ genus-handles of a Class M surface, with the ambient $E_2$-stratum on each handle contributing a factor of $6$ via the six-vertex / Kac--Moody counting, producing the $6^g$-genus-$g$ cohomology count at genus $g \geq 4$ (not infinite, uniform in $g$).
\end{conjecture}
